# gjsify — Project Status

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-04, NativeScript on-device polyfill smoke suite — first NS V8 execution proof + `@gjsify/buffer` fix):** New local-only `tests/integration/nativescript/` runs gjsify `nativescript:'polyfill'` packages on the **real Android NativeScript V8 runtime** and asserts pass/fail from `adb logcat` markers — the first proof the declared `nativescript` slots actually *execute*, not just bundle. **14/14 green**: `@gjsify/path` 7/7 + `@gjsify/buffer` 7/7 (NS CLI 9.0.6 / runtime 9.0.4, `@nativescript/core` 9.x, Vite 8.0.16). Bundled via the `@gjsify/nativescript-vite` composer; a tiny local `reporter.ts` is used instead of `@gjsify/unit` (its `process.exit`/`system.exit` terminal path no-ops on NS V8). **Excluded from the root workspace** (`!tests/integration/nativescript`) so the heavy NS toolchain stays out of `gjsify install`; not wired into CI (needs the NS CLI + an emulator, like `autobahn/`). **Root-cause fix surfaced + landed: `@gjsify/buffer` built `TextEncoder`/`TextDecoder` at module-eval time** (`buffer-codec.ts`, `blob.ts`) — on NS V8 those globals register *after* module evaluation, so the top-level `new TextEncoder()` threw and rejected the whole bundle on app start; now lazy-initialised (first-use), no behaviour change on GJS/Node/browser (Node 317 / GJS 326 buffer tests unchanged). The deterministic runner does `ns prepare` → manual copy of the Vite bundle into the APK assets → `gradle assembleDebug` → install → launch (NS CLI 9.0.6's Vite copy only fires in watch mode — see Open TODOs, with the `css-tree` `createRequire` + `nativescript-vite@0.4.37` `workspace:`-leak follow-ups).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-04, `gjsify login` / `logout` — Node-free npm auth trio completed):** New `gjsify login` + `gjsify logout` commands complete the Node-free npm auth surface alongside the existing `whoami` + `publish`. `gjsify login` implements npm's **legacy (`--auth-type=legacy`) credentials flow** — prompts for username + password (hidden, no-echo via raw-mode TTY; piped stdin supported for automation) + email, PUTs the CouchDB user doc to `<registry>/-/user/org.couchdb.user:<u>` with an `npm-otp` header for 2FA, receives a bearer token, and writes it to the userconfig `.npmrc` (`$NPM_CONFIG_USERCONFIG` or `~/.npmrc`) as `//<host>/:_authToken=<token>` — then verifies with `whoami`. `gjsify logout` best-effort-revokes the token (`DELETE /-/user/token/<token>`) and strips the line. The npm-9+ web-OAuth flow is intentionally not implemented (the legacy flow is the Node-free-friendly path). New `utils/auth-npmrc.ts` (the write side of `load-npmrc.ts`: nerf-dart key derivation + upsert/remove preserving other lines, chmod 0600) + `utils/prompt.ts` (visible + hidden prompts) + `auth-npmrc.spec.ts` (5 tests, temp-`.npmrc`). Also hardens **`gjsify publish`**: the 404 dead-token-vs-missing-package diagnostic now runs under `--otp` too (was skipped), and the live-token-404 message documents the transient first-publish provisioning race (re-run, or bootstrap with `npm publish`) — surfaced when `@gjsify/nativescript-vite` was first-published (a live token still 404'd on gjsify-publish while npm-publish succeeded). `@gjsify/cli` 336 (+5 auth-npmrc, +1 publish-diagnose updated); tsc/oxfmt/oxlint clean; `cli.gjs.mjs` rebuilt with the new commands.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-04, install-perf consolidation — packument gzip + shared cache primitive):** Follow-up to the merged install-perf series (#452/#455/#456). **(1) Packument gzip** — packuments are now fetched `accept-encoding: gzip` (~4× smaller on the wire; measured 770 KB → 186 KB for 10 `@girs/*` packuments) instead of the old blanket `identity`. The libsoup chunked-gzip decoder bug the `identity` workaround avoided is real (empirically reconfirmed: `@gjsify/fetch`'s **streaming** `DecompressionStream` trips `G_IO_ERROR_PARTIAL_INPUT` on npm-CDN gzip), so `fetchPackumentConditional` passes `compress: false` and **buffer-then-gunzips** the complete body itself (`decodeJsonBody`, gzip-magic-byte sniff + full-buffer `DecompressionStream` — the same pattern `@gjsify/tar` uses for `.tgz`, which is reliable; only the streaming decode is fragile). Cross-platform: Node/undici may pre-decode (no magic → parse as-is), GJS yields raw gzip (magic → gunzip). **Tarballs stay `identity`** (a `.tgz` is already gzipped; transport-gzip would break the raw-bytes SRI check) — `buildHeaders` now takes a per-call-site `acceptEncoding`. **(2) Shared cache primitive** — new `install-cache-fs.ts` (`gjsifyCacheRoot`/`atomicWrite`/`readCacheFile`) de-duplicates the XDG-root + atomic-write + read-or-MISS logic the tarball + packument caches each re-implemented; the SRI base64→hex decode (inlined twice in the tarball cache) is unified into a local `parseSri`. Pure extraction — public signatures unchanged. Verified: install A/B lockfile **byte-identical** to the `identity` baseline on a 6-spec gtk/webkit/adw/gst graph; re-resolve produces packument 304s + tarball-cache hits as before. `@gjsify/npm-registry` 88 (+10: header-split + gzip-decode) + `@gjsify/cli` 320 (+6 cache-fs primitive) + e2e 2/2 green; tsc/oxfmt/oxlint clean. A general fix for `@gjsify/fetch`'s streaming gzip decode (so any fetch caller can request gzip) is tracked in Open TODOs.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-04, standalone NativeScript teapot showcase + workspace `!`-negation):** Adds `showcases/dom/three-geometry-teapot-nativescript/` — the NativeScript-Android target of the `three-geometry-teapot` showcase. It renders the same Utah teapot from the same shared `start(canvas)` seam (a copy of `three-demo.ts`, self-contained) via `@gjsify/nativescript-vite` on Vite 8: `@nativescript/canvas` for the native GL surface + `@nativescript/canvas-polyfill` for the DOM shims. It is deliberately **NOT a workspace member** — the heavy NativeScript toolchain (`@nativescript/canvas` AARs, the `nativescript` CLI) would otherwise be pulled into every `gjsify install`. To support this, `@gjsify/workspace`'s `discoverWorkspaces` gained **npm/yarn-compatible `!`-negation patterns**, and the root `package.json` excludes the showcase with `"!showcases/dom/three-geometry-teapot-nativescript"`; its dependencies install only when you `cd` in + `npm install` to build it (`npm run run:android`). The committed `packages/infra/cli/dist/cli.gjs.mjs` bundle is rebuilt so CI's bootstrap install honors the negation. The showcase build itself is not CI-validated (no NativeScript toolchain in CI); the teapot was verified rendering on the Android emulator. New `@gjsify/workspace` spec for the negation; `gjsify-lock.json` unchanged (the showcase is excluded, so it adds no resolved deps).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-04, `@gjsify/nativescript-vite` — Vite 8 / Rolldown compatibility composer):** New package `@gjsify/nativescript-vite` (`packages/infra/nativescript-vite/`) makes NativeScript apps build under Vite 8 / Rolldown with gjsify's stack. It is a thin composer: it imports `@nativescript/vite`'s `typescriptConfig({mode})` (an optional peer) and applies the two fixes Vite 8 / Rolldown require on the returned config object — (1) it **drops every `resolve.alias` entry whose `replacement` is a function** (Vite 8's native alias rejects function replacements with `Failed to convert builtin plugin 'ViteAlias' … function replacement into rust type String`; the upstream `nativescript-package-resolver` `resolveId` plugin plus the string `~/` / `@` aliases already cover that resolution), and (2) it **removes the explicit `@rollup/plugin-commonjs` plugin** (named `'commonjs'`, warning if absent), which crashes Rolldown with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'currentLoadingModule')` — Rolldown handles `@nativescript/core`'s CommonJS modules natively. It then spreads `@gjsify/vite-plugin-gjsify`'s `gjsifyNativescript()` preset (`gi://` → empty, platform file resolution, platform defines, node-builtin aliases incl. `module` → `@gjsify/module`) on top. Public API: `defineNativescriptConfig(options?, userConfig?)` returns an async Vite config **function** (Vite resolves `mode` first); the optional second arg is a Vite config `mergeConfig`'d in last (the composability seam — e.g. `external()`-ing a canvas/WebGL app's unused `@nativescript/audio-context`). Consumer pattern: `vite.config.ts` → `export default defineNativescriptConfig();` and `nativescript.config.ts` → `bundler: 'vite'`. Its only hard dependency is `@gjsify/vite-plugin-gjsify`; all NativeScript packages (`@nativescript/{vite,core,canvas,canvas-polyfill}`, `nativescript`, `vite ^8.0.14`) are **optional peers** supplied by the consuming app — a missing `@nativescript/vite` throws a clear, actionable error. **Validated end-to-end on-device:** with pristine `@nativescript/vite` 2.0.3 + this composer, a real `@nativescript/canvas` three.js teapot app builds on Vite 8.0.16 → 612 modules → `bundle.mjs` (~450 kB), `ns run android` installs + launches and **the teapot renders on the Android emulator**, with zero `@nativescript/vite` / `gi://` / `@girs/*` leakage. New CI-safe regression test `tests/e2e/ns-vite-fixes` (synthetic config → asserts function-aliases + the `commonjs` plugin are stripped, string aliases + other plugins survive). **⚠ NEW PACKAGE** — needs the manual first-publish + npm Trusted Publisher bootstrap before the next `release.yml` run (see Open TODOs).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-04, NativeScript `module` import resolution + `@gjsify/empty` dependency):** Two fixes that make the `gjsifyNativescript()` Vite preset produce a resolvable bundle. (1) The NativeScript node-builtin alias table (`ALIASES_NODE_FOR_NATIVESCRIPT` in `resolve-npm/lib/index.mjs`) now routes `module` to **`@gjsify/module`** instead of `@gjsify/empty` — `@gjsify/empty` only exports `default {}`, so a dependency doing `import { createRequire } from 'module'` (e.g. `css-tree`) failed the build with `"createRequire" is not exported`; `@gjsify/module` supplies the named `createRequire`/`builtinModules`/`isBuiltin` exports (same target the GJS table already uses). (2) `@gjsify/vite-plugin-gjsify` now declares `@gjsify/empty` as a dependency — the presets alias node builtins to it, but it was used-but-undeclared, so a consumer that didn't otherwise install it hit `Could not load @gjsify/empty`.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-03, `gjsify install` cold-install speed — PR 3: packument metadata cache):** Final of the install-perf series. Adds an on-disk packument cache with **ETag revalidation** so a re-resolve (lockfile miss — e.g. a dependency range changed) skips re-downloading every unchanged packument. `@gjsify/npm-registry` gains `fetchPackumentConditional()` (sends `If-None-Match`; a `304 Not Modified` resolves to `{status:'not-modified'}` with no body, a `200` to `{status:'fresh', packument, etag}`); `fetchPackument()` now delegates to it (behaviour unchanged for existing callers). New CLI `install-packument-cache.ts` stores `{etag, packument}` under `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/gjsify/metadata/v1/<shard>/<enc-registry>|<enc-name>.json`, **keyed by (registry, name)** so scope-registry overrides never cross-contaminate; disabled with `GJSIFY_PACKUMENT_CACHE=0`. `resolveDeps`' `fetchPkg` reads the cached ETag, revalidates, and reuses the cached body on 304. Revalidation (not a TTL) because packuments are mutable — a TTL would silently miss a just-published version. End-to-end verified against the live registry: a second resolve of a 7-package `@girs/*` graph produced **7 × 304** (real ETags, zero body re-downloads). `@gjsify/npm-registry` 78 (+4 conditional) + `@gjsify/cli` 307 (+6 packument-cache) green; tsc/oxfmt/oxlint clean. **Install-perf series complete** (PR 1 conn-cap + npm cacache, PR 2 parallel resolve, PR 3 packument cache).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-03, `gjsify install` cold-install speed — PR 2: parallel resolve):** Second of the install-perf series. `resolveDeps()` in `install-backend-native.ts` was a fully serial BFS — it `await`ed one `fetchPackument` per edge, so a cold resolve of N unique packages was N sequential ~RTT round-trips (the dominant cost before the first tarball even downloads). Rewritten into a **wave-based BFS**: each iteration drains the current queue level, prefetches every not-yet-cached packument in that level **in parallel** (bounded to the same `DEFAULT_CONCURRENCY` 16 as the download pool, via the new `prefetchPackuments` helper), then applies hoist/nest placement **serially in the identical FIFO order** the single-edge loop used. Because newly-discovered children always append to the queue end, "the current queue contents" is exactly one BFS level — so placement order, and therefore the lockfile, is **byte-for-byte unchanged** (verified by diffing `gjsify-lock.json` from the serial vs parallel bundle on a real 9-spec → 24-entry `@girs/*` graph: identical). `packumentCache` still guarantees ≤1 fetch per unique name, so the parallel batch fetches the same SET, just concurrently; optional-dep 404s reject into the cache and are re-surfaced + skipped by the per-edge `try/catch`. New `tests/e2e/native-install` second case exercises a version-conflict graph (`app → dep@^1 + mid@^1`, `mid → dep@^2`) that forces `dep@2` to nest under `mid` while `dep@1` hoists — the order-sensitive path; both e2e cases green. Builds on PR 1's lifted Soup `max-conns-per-host` (at the old cap of 2 this parallelism would have throttled anyway). `@gjsify/cli` 295 + e2e 2/2 green; tsc/oxfmt/oxlint clean. Remaining: packument metadata cache (PR 3).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-03, `gjsify install` cold-install speed — PR 1):** First of an install-perf series. Profiled the map-editor cold install (687 tarballs): the shared `Soup.Session` in `@gjsify/fetch` ran at libsoup's default `max-conns-per-host=2`, serialising bulk fetches two-at-a-time **even over the negotiated HTTP/2 connection** — measured 16 packuments at ~3.0s vs ~0.24s once lifted (**12.8×**, same h2 conn). Fixes: (1) `getSharedSession()` constructs with `maxConnsPerHost: 16` + `maxConns: 64` (construct-only in libsoup 3), env-overridable (`GJSIFY_FETCH_MAX_CONNS_PER_HOST` / `GJSIFY_FETCH_MAX_CONNS`); (2) install download pool `8 → 16`; (3) **npm `_cacache` interop** — `getForeignCachedTarball()` reads npm's content store (same SRI key) as a second-chance source before the network, write-through to gjsify's own store, opt-out via `GJSIFY_NPM_CACHE=0`. `@gjsify/cli` 295 + `@gjsify/fetch` 79 (GJS) green; tsc/oxfmt/oxlint clean. Serial-resolve parallelization (PR 2) + packument metadata cache (PR 3) tracked in Open TODOs.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-03, NativeScript platform file resolution + platform defines):** gjsify's own build system now owns the first slice of `@nativescript/vite`'s NS-specific features (the "build-feature ownership" Level 2 in Open TODOs). New `platformResolvePlugin` — a Rolldown/Vite `resolveId` **hook** — resolves platform source variants (`foo.android.ts` / `foo.ios.ts` / `foo.native.ts`) ahead of the base `foo.ts`, in priority `…<platform>` → `…native` → base; platform comes from `NATIVESCRIPT_PLATFORM` / `NATIVESCRIPT_BUNDLER_ENV`. New `nativescriptPlatformDefines` bakes the standard NS compile-time flags `__ANDROID__` / `__IOS__` / `__APPLE__` / `__VISIONOS__` / `__DEV__` into `transform.define`, so per-platform branches are statically resolved + dead-code-eliminated. Both are wired into `app/nativescript.ts` (`gjsify build --app nativescript`) AND the `gjsifyNativescript()` Vite preset. Implemented as a plugin hook (not a `resolve.alias`) specifically because `@nativescript/vite`'s function-based platform alias is **rejected by Vite 8 / Rolldown** — gjsify fixes the feature at the root. New `tests/e2e/ns-platform-resolve` (android variant + `__ANDROID__=true`; native fallback + `__ANDROID__=false`). The Level-3 north-star (gjsify as a standalone NS production bundler, gated on an upstream NS-CLI pluggable-bundler PR) is tracked in Open TODOs.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-03, `refs/nativescript-canvas` vendored):** Adds the NativeScript/canvas monorepo (Rust/Skia native Canvas2D/WebGL/WebGPU + framework adapters, Apache-2.0) as a read-only `refs/` submodule — groundwork for porting a WebGL showcase (the three.js teapot) to NS-Android. **Use-vs-fork stance:** `packages/canvas` (native GL surface — the NS pendant to GJS' `Gtk.GLArea`) + `packages/canvas-polyfill` (MinimalDOM / `Image` / event shims via `@xmldom/xmldom` + `query-selector`, so three.js `TextureLoader`/`OrbitControls` run) are the intended runtime deps; `packages/canvas-three` (a thin `Renderer(gl,…)` factory) stays **reference-only** — we keep the cross-platform `start(canvas)` seam (`new THREE.WebGLRenderer({ canvas })`, identical to browser/GJS) rather than adopt its gl-context construction pattern. AGENTS.md `### Mobile (NativeScript)` + Source-Attribution updated.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-02, strict runtime-audit gate enabled):** Flips CI's runtime-triplet audit to `node scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs --check --strict` in `.github/workflows/audit-runtimes.yml` — the previously-parked **PR-B**. `--strict` runs the functional probes on every declared slot: each `native` slot's `globals.mjs` re-exports must resolve on-target, and every `browser:"polyfill"` package that ships a standard `src/test.{mts,ts}` must also ship a `src/test.browser.{mts,ts}` entry for the `tests/browser/` Playwright discovery. Unblocked by the browser-test coverage waves below **+** a carve-out in `probeBrowserBuildable` (`scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs`): packages with NO standard test entry — dependency-only meta (`@gjsify/{node-polyfills,browser-node-polyfills}`) and design-asset (`@gjsify/adwaita-{fonts,icons,web}`) packages — are exempt from `no-browser-test` (nothing to derive a browser test from). `audit-runtimes --check --strict` is green (80 declarable, 0 drift, functional probes pass on every slot). Net effect: browser-test coverage is now CI-enforced — a new `browser:"polyfill"` package with a standard test that forgets its `test.browser.mts` now fails the audit instead of silently drifting.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.36, 2026-06-02, NativeScript slot backfill + Browser-axis completion):** Two parallel waves landed across 18 PRs (#429–#446). **(1) NativeScript quadruplet backfill — every declarable package now carries a `nativescript` slot** (was: 0 declared, Welle 4-T foundation-only). Classified via the `deriveNativescriptSlot()` heuristic and verified against source: **20 `polyfill`** (portable pure-TS node-api — assert/buffer/events/path/stream/process/util/querystring/string_decoder/timers/console/constants/domain/async_hooks/diagnostics_channel/https/sys + the node/browser polyfill meta pkgs), **15 `native`** (web-api the NS V8 ships natively — abort-controller/dom-events/dom-exception/domparser/eventsource/message-channel/compression-streams/formdata/gamepad/webassembly/webcrypto/webstorage/web-streams/web-globals/web-polyfills, routed via each package's existing `globals.mjs`), **45 `none`** (GJS-/server-bound — net/tls/dgram/child_process/http2/readline/… plus framework-gjs/dom and the Soup/GStreamer web packages). `audit-runtimes --check` stays green (0 drift). Portable `--app nativescript` bundling validated locally (path/stream/process/abort-controller build clean, no `gi://`/`@girs/` leakage). This completes the **slot-declaration** half of Welle 5-A across all 80 packages. **(2) Browser-axis completion:** `@gjsify/crypto` browser ECDH + Sign/Verify + PBKDF2/HKDF/scrypt (WebCrypto `subtle` + a pure-BigInt ECDH backend; honest ENOTSUP where no web pendant); `@gjsify/fs` OPFS persistence behind the memfs volume (feature-detected, in-memory fallback); `@gjsify/http` fetch-backed client — `IncomingMessage` is now a real readable stream (flowing+paused, `setEncoding`, `pipe`, async-iteration), `createServer` stays honest ENOTSUP; `@gjsify/worker_threads` browser `Worker` over Web Workers (workerData transport, message/error/exit events, parentPort parity); `@gjsify/zlib` dropped a faked `br` codec → honest brotli/zstd ENOTSUP (gzip/deflate via Compression Streams kept); `@gjsify/https` own https-defaulting `request`/`get`; `@gjsify/{dns,sqlite,module,vm}` honest browser behavior locked in (dns resolves only IP-literals/`localhost`, sqlite `ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, module real `builtinModules`/`isBuiltin`). **(3) Browser-test coverage** grew from 12 to ~30 packages — `src/test.browser.mts` + `build:test:browser` added for the portable node-api set, the native re-export set, and the completed browser-partial packages. Per the `### Browser tests` contract these are **browser-globals-direct** suites (the buffer-style `export * from './test.mjs'` re-export tree-shakes to a 0-byte bundle and was avoided). **Still future (Welle 5-B/C/D/E/F/G):** native NS bridges (`@gjsify/fs` via `java.io.File`/`NSFileManager`, `@gjsify/crypto` TweetNaCl path for NS' `subtle`-less V8, the `packages/nativescript-bridge/` pillar), the `tests/integration/nativescript/` smoke suite, and `gjsify create-app --template nativescript-*`.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-31, `@gjsify/ws` loopback `permessage-deflate` fix):** Closes the silent-message-drop bug that blocked pixel-rpg/map-editor's Pair-Editing for multiple hand-test cycles. `@gjsify/ws` `WebSocket` (client) defaults `perMessageDeflate: true` to match npm-`ws`. Soup ships `WebsocketExtensionDeflate` registered by default on every `Soup.Server`, so the handshake negotiates `permessage-deflate`. But Soup's loopback encode/decode path is buggy across Fedora Soup 3.x builds — server-to-client frames go out compressed, the listener never sees a usable `message` event, and the connection times out. The map-editor hand-test sent 13 frames from host to joiner; the joiner reported `0 frames delivered`. Reproduced cleanly in the new `packages/node/ws/src/rapid-server-send.spec.ts` (8 specs incl. bisects + diagnostic variants). Fix in `_setupHandlers`: call `soupServer.remove_websocket_extension(WebsocketExtensionDeflate.$gtype)` so the server refuses deflate negotiation. Clients fall back to plain frames (which work end-to-end on every path we test — Autobahn against external server, two `@gjsify/ws` peers in the same GJS process, two `@gjsify/ws` peers across `dbus-run-session`). Test count `@gjsify/ws` 59 → 67, all green. The deflate code-path stays reachable for genuinely-bandwidth-constrained cross-internet peers: the caller can re-register the extension on the underlying `soupServer` after constructing the WebSocketServer.

> **Previous state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-31, `@gjsify/dom-events` listener-exception reporting fix):** Closes the long-standing "`{}`-instead-of-error" diagnostic disaster. `EventTarget.dispatchEvent` previously routed listener exceptions to `console.error(err)` — under `@gjsify/console`'s `_formatArgs` JSON.stringify fallback (which renders non-string args via `JSON.stringify(a)`), that reduced every Error subclass to a single `{}` line with NO message + NO stack, because `Error`'s enumerable property set is empty in JS. Surfaced via pixel-rpg/map-editor's Pair-Editing hand-test: a WebSocket message-event listener was throwing on inbound SDP frames, the throw was caught by EventTarget, logged as `{}`, and silently dropped — leaving the joiner with `[peer-session/joiner] waiting for host SDP offer` followed by a 15s `CollabTimeoutError` and zero diagnostic context. Fix: route to `globalThis.reportError` when wired (W3C standard since 2022 — runtime's full ErrorEvent / unhandledrejection machinery), otherwise fall back to `console.error(<head>\\n<stack>)` where `<head>` is always `${err.name}: ${err.message}` (SpiderMonkey's `err.stack` is frames-only, no message embedded — V8's stack includes the message at the top; always prepending keeps the runtime difference invisible to the operator). Three new active regression specs in `packages/web/dom-events/src/error-handler.spec.ts` (alongside the pre-existing pair gated on full W3C ErrorEvent dispatch which we still don't implement): delegate-to-reportError, fallback-prints-stack-not-bare-Error (THE 2026-05-31 marker), fallback-formats-non-Error-via-String. Test count 281 → 283, all green on Node + GJS.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-30, `tests/integration/loro-crdt` expansion):** Expanded the Loro CRDT integration suite from 57 to **166 tests** (Node 166/166, GJS 166/166 — both green; GJS is 2.5× faster end-to-end). Added 4 new spec files ported from `refs/loro/crates/loro-wasm/tests/`: **`loro-counter.spec.ts`** (8 tests — increment/decrement, snapshot/update/JSON-updates round-trips, two-doc sync, counter event subscribe, persistence across commits, initial-zero invariant); **`loro-movable-list.spec.ts`** (12 tests — push/pop, move(from,to), set(i,v), sync via snapshot, insertContainer + setContainer for nested CRDTs, concurrent-move length invariant, concurrent-set peer-id-wins tiebreak, subscribe lifecycle, attached-container detachment, nested LoroMap value, insert+delete length consistency); **`loro-version.spec.ts`** (17 tests — Frontiers cmpWithFrontiers + cmpFrontiers, peer-id consistency across frontiers/version/container-IDs, VersionVector ↔ Frontiers round-trip via vvToFrontiers, current-frontiers shape, getAllChanges per-peer, getOpsInChange, decodeImportBlobMeta on update / snapshot / multi-peer / partial-from-vv blobs); **`loro-awareness.spec.ts`** (7 tests — AwarenessWasm setLocalState, encode/apply sync, peer-filter via encode([peers]), removeOutdated by timeout, newer-bytes-don't-overwrite consistency, binary Uint8Array state, wrapper Awareness with local/remote/timeout listener origins). **No `@gjsify/*` source changes needed** — the `loro-crdt` npm package (Rust → WASM via wasm-bindgen, distributed as base64-embedded WASM in the `loro-crdt/base64/` subpath) runs unchanged on GJS via the existing `@gjsify/webassembly` Promise-API polyfill + `@gjsify/web-globals` (TextEncoder/Decoder) + `@gjsify/webcrypto` (`crypto.getRandomValues`) + `gjsify build --globals 'auto,crypto'` (the wasm-bindgen `__wbg_*` crypto-access pattern isn't statically detectable, hence the explicit hint). Demonstrates that **the bundled-toolchain axis (sixth axis in Strategic direction) extends naturally to Rust-via-WASM npm packages** — Loro is a 2nd validated proof-of-concept beyond `@gjsify/tsc`, this time for the WASM-via-base64 distribution shape.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-30, `@gjsify/child_process` env-undefined fix + `@deltachat/jsonrpc-client` integration suite):** Discovered (and fixed at the root) a silent-data-loss bug in `@gjsify/child_process.spawn`: when the `env` option contained an entry with value `undefined` (a common Node pattern — e.g. `env: { RUST_LOG: process.env.RUST_LOG, … }` that intentionally passes-through-or-omits), the underlying `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.setenv(key, value, true)` GI call threw a type-marshalling error on the `undefined` arg that aborted the spawn AND silently nulled out `proc.stdin` + `proc.stdout` via the partially-constructed launcher. Symptom: spawn returned a `ChildProcess` with both pipes `null`, no error event ever emitted. Fix in `_spawnSubprocess`: skip `undefined`-valued entries (matches Node's documented behavior — undefined values are omitted, not stringified) + coerce non-string values via `String(value)` (matches Node's number/boolean handling). Two new GJS regression specs in `packages/node/child_process/src/index.spec.ts`. Surfaced via the new `tests/integration/deltachat/` suite (port of `@deltachat/jsonrpc-client` + `@deltachat/stdio-rpc-server` on GJS) — 7/7 tests green on Node, 7/7 green on GJS (268ms) after the fix. The deltachat suite exercises: spawn the ~27 MB `deltachat-rpc-server` Rust binary, bidirectional JSON-RPC over stdin/stdout, `getSystemInfo` / `checkEmailValidity` / `addAccount` / `getAllAccountIds` / `removeAccount` / `getProviderInfo` / `sleep` round-trips, clean process termination. **First integration suite that crosses the GJS↔native-subprocess boundary in both directions with a long-running subprocess** (existing `@gjsify/child_process` units cover one-shot exec; here the subprocess outlives many JSON-RPC round-trips). Unblocks the path toward a native Adwaita+GJS DeltaChat app.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-30, Welle 2-S — browser-polyfill refs vendored):** First commit of the cross-runtime browser-polyfill expansion (sequel to v0.4.34's `gjsify.runtimes` triplet declaration). Adds 9 must-have submodules under `refs/` as read-only inspiration sources for the upcoming Browser-polyfill wave: `node-stdlib-browser` (canonical npm-name → browser-polyfill mapping aggregator), `path-browserify` (drop-in `node:path` polyfill), `process` (defunctzombie/node-process — canonical browser `process` shim), `stream-browserify` (slim `node:stream` re-export wrapper), `buffer-browserify` (feross/buffer — industry-standard `Buffer` class), `pako` (zlib in pure JS — backbone of `browserify-zlib`), `browserify-zlib` (`node:zlib`-compatible streaming wrapper around pako), `memfs` (in-memory Node `fs` impl with full async + sync + streams + watcher API), `wa-sqlite` (WebAssembly SQLite with modular OPFS/IndexedDB/Memory VFS layers). All MIT or Apache-2.0; Source Attribution table in `AGENTS.md` (+ symlinked `CLAUDE.md`) gains the 9 corresponding pipe-rows; `## References — refs/` gains a new `### Browser polyfills` subsection. Source: Welle-1 audits R1 (browser polyfills) / S (submodule plan) / C (credits inventory). Unblocks the upcoming Browser-polyfill wave that needs these as references for `@gjsify/{path,buffer,stream,zlib,fs,sqlite,process}` on the Web axis.
 **Current state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-30, `@gjsify/child_process` Node-parity audit):** Systematic differential audit of `@gjsify/child_process` against `refs/node/lib/child_process.js` + 50+ `refs/node-test/parallel/test-child-process-*.js` reference tests. 14 silent-semantic-mismatch bugs fixed (env value coercion incl. `undefined`/`null`/number/boolean/array; env prototype-chain `for…in`; empty-env wipes parent env; `cwd` accepts `URL`/`Buffer`; `argv0`/`detached`/`uid`/`gid`/`windowsHide`/`windowsVerbatimArguments` accepted with POSIX wrappers where needed; exec/execFile/execSync `encoding: 'buffer'`/`null` returns `Buffer`; exec/spawn/spawnSync honour `timeout`+`killSignal` — spawnSync via `/usr/bin/timeout` argv wrapper since `communicate()` blocks the GLib mainloop; exec honours `maxBuffer` + `AbortSignal`; spawn/spawnSync validate option/argv/input types synchronously; ENOENT-for-missing-binary surfaces as `err.code='ENOENT'`/`err.errno=-2`/`err.syscall='spawn <file>'`/`err.path`/`err.spawnargs` via `GLib.SpawnError.matches()` detection + Error-wrap so `err instanceof Error` holds; `kill()` maps named signals to POSIX numbers). New `packages/node/child_process/src/parity.spec.ts` adds 86 regression tests organised in 13 describe blocks; test count 124 → 210, all green on Node + GJS. `@gjsify/integration-execa` 44/44 still green. Same class of bug the env-undefined-nulls-stdin discovery (#407) caught accidentally; this turns the rest into a permanent regression net.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, Welle 3-F browser-polyfill wiring):** Four-commit follow-up to Welle 2-T (PR #388) and the staged browser-polyfill PRs #392 – #396. Lands the wiring that makes the foundation operational while keeping CI green. (1) `packages/infra/rolldown-plugin-gjsify/src/app/browser.ts` now consumes `ALIASES_NODE_FOR_BROWSER` from `@gjsify/resolve-npm` — a `for…of` loop generates both the bare (`stream`) and `node:*` (`node:stream`) form for every entry, eliminating per-target hand-curation. The legacy `browserPolyfillAliases` Record is emptied to `{}` (its `process: '@gjsify/empty'` and `assert: '@gjsify/assert'` entries shadowed the curated table — `process` now correctly routes to `@gjsify/process`). (2) New meta-pkg `packages/node/browser-polyfills/` (`@gjsify/browser-node-polyfills`) — browser counterpart to `@gjsify/node-polyfills`. Curated 28 workspace deps covering every Node polyfill that ships a browser-runnable build (post-PR-#392-#396 slot state). Triplet `{gjs: polyfill, node: native, browser: polyfill}`. `globals.mjs` wires `@gjsify/buffer/register` today; grows as per-package `/register` granular subpaths land. (3) `packages/infra/resolve-npm/lib/globals-map.mjs` gains `BROWSER_NATIVE_IDENTS` (Set of every browser-native identifier from `GJS_GLOBALS_GROUPS.{web,dom}` + shared Node-group), `BROWSER_GLOBALS_MAP` (strict subset of `GJS_GLOBALS_MAP` still needing browser-side polyfills — today: just `Buffer`), and `getGlobalsMapFor(target)` helper. `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` `BROWSER_NATIVE_RE_EXPORTS` no longer carries a TODO empty placeholder; sources the curated set. (4) PR-B (`--check` default flip to strict) is PARKED behind a follow-up — 26 packages on the integration base lack `src/test.browser.{mts,ts}` entries; forcing the flip would put every CI run into permanent failure. Forward-compatible `--quick` flag wired today (no-op until the future flip); the rollback path is documented inline so the eventual flip becomes a one-constant change. T-Plan sections 2 (PR-D), 4 (PR-F), 5 (PR-G) landed; section 1 (PR-B) prep-only.

> Last updated: 2026-05-30 — Welle 3-D: in-memory browser polyfill for `@gjsify/fs` (memfs-inspired), runtime slot `browser:"partial"`.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, `@gjsify/fs` browser polyfill — welle 3-D):** New `packages/node/fs/src/browser.ts` barrel + `src/browser/{volume,sync,async,promises,promises-entry,stream,types,errors,constants,path-utils}.ts` sub-modules ship an in-memory POSIX-shaped file system that the `--app browser` bundler picks up via the package's new top-level `"browser":"./lib/esm/browser.js"` field + the `"."` exports `"browser"` condition + new `./browser` / `./browser/promises` / `./browser/{volume,sync,async,stream}` subpaths. Architecture is a single global `__defaultVolume = new Volume()` whose tree is a `Map<string, FileNode | DirNode | SymlinkNode>` keyed by path segment, with all sync (`readFileSync`/`writeFileSync`/`appendFileSync`/`statSync`/`lstatSync`/`existsSync`/`accessSync`/`readdirSync`/`mkdirSync`/`rmdirSync`/`rmSync`/`unlinkSync`/`renameSync`/`copyFileSync`/`linkSync`/`symlinkSync`/`readlinkSync`/`realpathSync`/`chmodSync`/`chownSync`/`truncateSync`/`utimesSync`/`mkdtempSync`/`openSync`/`closeSync`/`readSync`/`writeSync`) routed straight at it. Callback (`fs.X(...,cb)`) and promise (`fs.promises.X()` / `import 'fs/promises'`) APIs wrap the sync impls via `queueMicrotask` so Node's "always async" callback contract holds, errors become `{code:'ENOENT',errno:-2,syscall,path}` shaped from `src/browser/errors.ts`, and the three surfaces share one volume — a `writeFile()` is visible to the next `readFileSync()`. `Stats` / `BigIntStats` / `Dirent` are the proper Node-prototype classes (`isFile()`/`isDirectory()`/`isSymbolicLink()`), backed by a shared `KindTests` abstract base. `createReadStream` / `createWriteStream` are minimal Node-shape streams (`on`/`once`/`pipe`/`destroy`/async iterator) over `queueMicrotask`-pumped chunks (no `@gjsify/stream` dep — the bundle stays self-contained). `Volume` is also exported as a class so test code can build sandbox volumes (`new Volume()` → `fromJSON()` / `toJSON()` memfs parity helpers). Slot is `partial` — known gaps documented in the file header: no OPFS / IndexedDB persistence (page reload wipes the volume), `FSWatcher` is a stub (registers events, never fires), symlinks are stored but never followed by `realpath` / `stat`, `chmod` / `chown` alter mode bits but enforce nothing, file descriptors are minimal (no `readv` / `writev` / `fsync` / `fdatasync`), no recursive `cp`. The runtimes triplet is now `{gjs:"polyfill", node:"none", browser:"partial"}` — `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` got a `has_browser_polyfill` signal (detects `src/browser.ts`) so the auto-suggestion matches the new declared shape (drift 0). Future iteration: bridge the volume to OPFS for persistence + thread mutator events through `FSWatcher` (chokidar / vite-watch parity).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, `@gjsify/crypto` browser polyfill — wave 3-C):** XL lift from `.gjsify-browser-audit-R1.md` § "Major lifts" #1 lands. `packages/node/crypto/src/browser.ts` (212 LoC barrel) + `src/browser/{random,hash,hmac,kdf,cipher,sign,stubs}.ts` (1229 LoC across seven feature-grouped modules, 1441 LoC total) ship a WebCrypto-first browser polyfill behind the new `"browser"` field + `exports."."` `"browser"` condition (Rolldown/Vite `mainFields:['browser','module','main']` + `conditionNames:[…,'browser']` resolve it automatically; the GJS index.ts is bypassed in `--app browser` builds). Strategy: native re-export wherever WebCrypto already exposes the primitive (`webcrypto`→`globalThis.crypto`, `subtle`→`globalThis.crypto.subtle`, `randomBytes/Fill/UUID/Int` over `getRandomValues`/`randomUUID`); WebCrypto-backed async classes for Hash/Hmac/Cipher/Sign with explicit `digestAsync()`/`finalAsync()`/`signAsync()` entries and structured `ENOTSUP_SYNC_*` errors on the sync surface (no pure-JS sha-256/aes impls vendored — the bundle stays tight and the async path is first-class). PBKDF2 + HKDF via `subtle.deriveBits`; AES-CBC/CTR/GCM Cipher/Decipher with `setAAD`/`getAuthTag`/`setAuthTag` GCM helpers. Classic DH/ECDH/RSA-OAEP/scrypt/KeyObject/X509Certificate throw ENOTSUP unconditionally (no WebCrypto pendant), each with a per-API hint pointing at the closest `subtle.*` call. `runtimes.browser` flips `none` → `partial`. `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` learns a new `has_browser_polyfill` source-signal (`src/browser.ts` presence) so GJS-bound packages that opt into a dedicated browser entry suggest `browser:partial` instead of the default `none`. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `audit-runtimes --check` clean (79 declarable packages match).

> Last updated: 2026-05-30 — Welle 3-B: browser-polyfill entries for the stream / http / https / zlib family (sequel to Welle 3-A which shipped process / os / dns / ws / module).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, Welle 3-B — stream/http/https/zlib browser polyfills):** Second implementation batch of the cross-runtime browser-polyfill wave. Ships dedicated `src/browser.ts` entries for the stream-family Node polyfills alongside their existing GJS-bound `src/index.ts` impls. **`@gjsify/stream`** — EventEmitter + `queueMicrotask`-backed `Readable` / `Writable` / `Duplex` / `Transform` / `PassThrough` + `Stream` (legacy `.pipe()`) + `pipeline` + `finished`, declaring the full stream-browserify surface in 505 LOC. Slot stays `browser:"polyfill"` but now ships a proper browser entry instead of bundling the GJS impl. **`@gjsify/http`** — `request()` / `get()` ride on top of native `fetch()`, response bodies stream through an EventEmitter-shaped `IncomingMessage`; `createServer` / `Server` / `ServerResponse` / `OutgoingMessage` throw ENOTSUP (no listening socket in a browser). 392 LOC. Slot `browser:"none"→"partial"`. **`@gjsify/https`** — thin wrapper re-exporting `@gjsify/http`'s browser entry with an additional `Agent` stub (browser `fetch()` handles TLS transparently for `https://` URLs). 54 LOC. Slot `browser:"polyfill"→"partial"`. **`@gjsify/zlib`** — top-level callback APIs (`gzip`/`gunzip`/`deflate`/`inflate`/`deflateRaw`/`inflateRaw`/`brotliCompress`/`brotliDecompress`) ride on native `CompressionStream` / `DecompressionStream`; matching Transform-stream classes (`Gzip`/`Gunzip`/`Deflate`/`Inflate`/`DeflateRaw`/`InflateRaw`/`BrotliCompress`/`BrotliDecompress`) layered over `@gjsify/stream/browser`; sync variants throw with an ENOTSUP-hint pointing at the async API (CompressionStream is async-only). Brotli requires Chrome 117+; older browsers will surface a runtime error from the native ctor. 242 LOC. Slot `browser:"none"→"partial"`. `audit-runtimes.mjs` learns one extra wiring step: the pure-TS branch now also honours `has_browser_src + browser_src_is_partial` to recognise the `@gjsify/https` shape (pure-TS index but partial browser). `tsc --noEmit` clean across all four modified packages; `node scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs --check` passes (79 declarable packages match, 0 drift). Browser-build smoke-test deferred — depends on `ALIASES_NODE_FOR_BROWSER` from PR #388 being merged so `gjsify build --app browser` can resolve `node:*` specifiers to `@gjsify/<X>/browser` routes.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, Welle 3-A browser polyfill entries):** First implementation batch of the cross-runtime browser-polyfill wave (sequel to Welle 2 — refs vendored in #386, slot flips in #387, tooling foundation in #388). Six new dedicated `src/browser.ts` entries land alongside the existing GJS-bound `src/index.ts` impls in `packages/node/{process,os,dns,ws,module}` plus a `src/test.browser.mts` re-export entry for `@gjsify/buffer`. Each `browser.ts` is a self-contained SPDX-MIT-licensed ESM module exporting a runtime-coherent surface — `@gjsify/process` ships the canonical defunctzombie/node-process subset (nextTick via queueMicrotask, env={}, cwd='/', browser=true, EventEmitter no-op surface, stdin/stdout/stderr stubs); `@gjsify/os` mirrors os-browserify + the modern Node 22 additions (availableParallelism, userInfo, getPriority/setPriority); `@gjsify/dns` is a `partial` slot — `lookup()` resolves to loopback, every real-resolution method throws ENOTSUP with a Node-shaped err `{code, errno, syscall, hostname}`; `@gjsify/ws` re-exports `globalThis.WebSocket` for the client path + ENOTSUP-throws `WebSocketServer` / `Server` / `createWebSocketStream` for the server path (so npm `ws` consumers' static prop reads survive bundle init even when the runtime branch never fires); `@gjsify/module` ships `vite-plugin-node-polyfills`'s `builtinModules=[]` / `isBuiltin()=false` / `createRequire(url) → require-stub` shape. Companion cross-runtime fix: `@gjsify/console` + `@gjsify/timers` had `globals.mjs` files re-exporting from `node:*`, which fail to resolve in the browser bundle — both now route through `globalThis.*` (with a Promise-based setImmediate polyfill in `@gjsify/timers` for the browser path), flipping both slots from `browser:"polyfill"` to `browser:"native"`. `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` learned to read these new signals (`has_browser_src`, `browser_src_is_partial`, `globals_mjs_browser_safe`) so `--check` continues to pass at 79/79 declarable packages with zero drift. `package.json` shape per browser-entry package: top-level `"browser": "./lib/esm/browser.js"` for legacy bundler conditionals + `exports."."."browser"` + new `./browser` subpath export. TS check clean across all 5 modified packages individually (process/os/dns/ws/module). Browser-build smoke-test deferred to a follow-up (depends on `ALIASES_NODE_FOR_BROWSER` from #388 being merged).
