(1) `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` gains a `--check --strict` opt-in mode that statically validates declared `gjsify.runtimes` slots against the mechanics on disk — `native` slots probe `globals.mjs` re-export sources against `EXTERNALS_NODE` / a curated (today-empty, TODO Welle 5) browser set / `@gjsify/<X>/globals` self-delegation; `browser:"polyfill"` slots probe for `src/test.browser.{mts,ts}`. Default `--check` byte-identical. (2) `packages/infra/resolve-npm/lib/index.mjs` exports `ALIASES_NODE_FOR_BROWSER` (56 entries — every Node built-in + `node-fetch` / `ws` / `isomorphic-ws`, mapped to `@gjsify/<X>` or `@gjsify/empty` per the browser-polyfill plan). Unwired: `app/browser.ts` does not consume the map yet — PR-D flips the wiring once R1 ships browser-baubable `@gjsify/{process,buffer,stream,...}`. (3) `tests/browser/scripts/discover-bundles.mjs` joins `packages/node` to the existing `packages/{web,dom}` discovery (no-op until per-package `src/test.browser.mts` skeletons land in R1); each discovered bundle now carries a `pillar` field and the Playwright spec title is prefixed `<pillar>/@gjsify/<pkg> — browser unit tests`. Audit `--check` still passes (79 declarable). `--check --strict` reports 25 probe failures today (expected baseline; primarily missing `src/test.browser.mts` on `browser:"polyfill"` Node packages).
> **Current state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-30, `pre-commit` hook auto-rebuilds stale CLI/tsc bundles):** New `.githooks/pre-commit` (plain bash, no husky/lefthook dep) detects staged changes under `packages/infra/{cli,tsc}/src/` + the corresponding `package.json`, rebuilds the affected GJS bundle(s) via `gjsify workspace @gjsify/{cli,tsc} build[ && build:gjs-bundle]`, and auto-stages the refreshed `dist/<bundle>.gjs.mjs` into the same commit. Resolves `gjsify` via three fallbacks (workspace-local `node_modules/.bin/gjsify` → PATH → committed `cli.gjs.mjs` GJS bundle). Bypass: `git commit --no-verify` or `SKIP_GJSIFY_HOOKS=1`. Install: `node scripts/install-git-hooks.mjs` sets `core.hooksPath=.githooks` (idempotent; auto-wired by `gjsify install` via a new `maybeInstallGitHooks()` post-check, gated on `existsSync(.git) && existsSync(scripts/install-git-hooks.mjs)` so consumer projects are skipped silently). Removes the recurring CI-red-on-PR foot-gun documented in the `### Completed` entry — every CLI/tsc-src edit now refreshes the bundle transparently rather than asking the contributor to remember a 2-step rebuild + re-stage dance. New e2e suite `tests/e2e/git-hooks-cli-bundle-staleness/` (3 tests) locks the contract: hook is no-op for unrelated changes, auto-rebuilds + auto-stages on CLI-src changes, and `SKIP_GJSIFY_HOOKS=1` skips cleanly.
> Last updated: 2026-05-30 — Welle 3-E: browser polyfills for `@gjsify/{worker_threads, vm, sqlite}`. Slots flip to `partial` (worker_threads, sqlite) / stay `polyfill` (vm). Total LOC budget 582 / 600.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, welle 3-E browser polyfills):** Three new `packages/node/{worker_threads,vm,sqlite}/src/browser.ts` entries close the last three Major-Lift rows from `.gjsify-browser-audit-R1.md`. (1) `@gjsify/worker_threads` wraps `globalThis.Worker` into a Node-shape `Worker` (`on('message'|'error'|'exit')`/`postMessage`/`terminate`/`ref`/`unref`), re-exports native `MessageChannel`/`MessagePort`/`BroadcastChannel`, exposes a `parentPort` proxy over `globalThis.self` when running inside a `DedicatedWorkerGlobalScope`, returns `isMainThread`/`threadId`/`workerData`/`SHARE_ENV` shaped to Node — `transferList`, second-arg shape, in-process `setEnvironmentData`/`getEnvironmentData` Map. Slot flips `browser:"none" → "partial"`. (2) `@gjsify/vm` ships an `eval`-backed `runInThisContext`, a `Function`-scope `runInNewContext`/`runInContext`, a `Script` class replaying both, `compileFunction` via `new Function`, `createContext`/`isContext` markers. `Module`/`SourceTextModule`/`SyntheticModule`/`measureMemory` throw structured ENOTSUP. No real Realm isolation — pure best-effort `Function`-scope per `vm-browserify` (James Halliday, MIT). Slot stays `browser:"polyfill"`. (3) `@gjsify/sqlite` ships a Capability-Slot stub: `DatabaseSync`/`StatementSync` constructors + every method throw structured ENOTSUP pointing at the WASM-backend follow-up; `constants.SQLITE_*` re-exported as-is from the GJS impl. This shape lets consumers `import 'node:sqlite'` and call `instanceof` / inspect `constants` without bloating bundles by 0.5-1.5 MB of unused SQLite-WASM. A future welle will land a `@gjsify/sqlite/wasm-backend` hook wiring `refs/wa-sqlite` (Roy Hashimoto, MIT) or `refs/sql.js` (Apache-2.0 / MIT) at runtime. Slot flips `browser:"none" → "partial"`. All three packages set top-level `"browser"` + `exports."."` `"browser"` condition + `./browser` subpath. `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` keeps the `has_browser_polyfill` signal introduced in welle 3-D — drift is 0 across all 79 declarable packages. `tsc --noEmit` clean on all three packages.
 **Current state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-30, Welle 2-S — browser-polyfill refs vendored):** First commit of the cross-runtime browser-polyfill expansion (sequel to v0.4.34's `gjsify.runtimes` triplet declaration). Adds 9 must-have submodules under `refs/` as read-only inspiration sources for the upcoming Browser-polyfill wave: `node-stdlib-browser` (canonical npm-name → browser-polyfill mapping aggregator), `path-browserify` (drop-in `node:path` polyfill), `process` (defunctzombie/node-process — canonical browser `process` shim), `stream-browserify` (slim `node:stream` re-export wrapper), `buffer-browserify` (feross/buffer — industry-standard `Buffer` class), `pako` (zlib in pure JS — backbone of `browserify-zlib`), `browserify-zlib` (`node:zlib`-compatible streaming wrapper around pako), `memfs` (in-memory Node `fs` impl with full async + sync + streams + watcher API), `wa-sqlite` (WebAssembly SQLite with modular OPFS/IndexedDB/Memory VFS layers). All MIT or Apache-2.0; Source Attribution table in `AGENTS.md` (+ symlinked `CLAUDE.md`) gains the 9 corresponding pipe-rows; `## References — refs/` gains a new `### Browser polyfills` subsection. Source: Welle-1 audits R1 (browser polyfills) / S (submodule plan) / C (credits inventory). Unblocks the upcoming Browser-polyfill wave that needs these as references for `@gjsify/{path,buffer,stream,zlib,fs,sqlite,process}` on the Web axis.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-30, `gjsify install` timeout safety net):** Closes the root cause of an observed `gjsify install` hang (10+ min, 0% CPU, sleeping on an await that never settles) caused by a reachable-but-slow npm registry CDN where TCP connect succeeded but the response body never finished. Two layers of timeout coverage: (1) per-request timeout in `@gjsify/npm-registry` (`FetchOptions.timeoutMs`, default 30s, override / disable via `0`) — every `fetchPackument` / `fetchTarball` attempt gets a fresh `AbortController` composed with the caller's signal via `AbortSignal.any`; timeout-aborts are classified as transient and retried, caller-aborts propagate as `AbortError`, all-attempts-timeout surfaces a new typed `RegistryTimeoutError extends Error` with the clear message `GET <url> timed out after Xs × N attempt(s)…` (replaces the inscrutable `signal is aborted without reason`). (2) Overall install timeout in the CLI — new `gjsify install --timeout <ms>` flag (default 300000 = 5 min wall-clock); on fire, every in-flight registry fetch aborts via the threaded `InstallOptions.signal` and the handler exits 1 with `gjsify install: timed out after Xs — likely a registry slowdown. Re-run, or override with --timeout <ms>`. 6 new mock-driven tests in `packages/infra/npm-registry/src/timeout.spec.ts` (62 → 68 on Node + GJS) + 1 new e2e suite `tests/e2e/install-timeout/run.mjs` that runs `gjsify install --timeout 800` against a slow mock-registry and asserts a clean non-zero exit < 10s. CLI bundle (`packages/infra/cli/dist/cli.gjs.mjs`) refreshed. See `### Completed`.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.35, 2026-05-30, `gjsify workspace --with-dependencies`):** New `--with-dependencies` flag (short alias `-d`, foreach-style synonyms `-t` / `--topological`) on `gjsify workspace <name> <script>` walks the target's transitive workspace-dep closure via DFS over `dependencies` + `optionalDependencies` whose specifier starts with `workspace:` (plus `devDependencies` under `--include-dev`), feeds the closure into `@gjsify/workspace`'s `buildDependencyGraph` + `topologicalSort`, and runs the script in each dep in dependency order, finally in the target. Intermediate deps without the script are skipped (`--if-present` semantics). Default stops on first failure; `--continue-on-error` keeps going past failures. Without the flag the command behaves exactly as before. Closes the single biggest local-dev pain point in this monorepo: cascade-failing `gjsify workspace @gjsify/<deep-pkg> build` when transitive `lib/` dirs aren't built yet. New e2e suite `tests/e2e/workspace-with-dependencies/` validates the closure walk + topological order + `-d`/`-t` aliases + `--if-present` skip + stop-on-fail + `--continue-on-error` against a synthetic 4-deep monorepo (8/8 tests green). Root `build:infra` script kept as-is — it mixes `build` + `build:types` calls in the same chain, so it can't be a drop-in replacement (separate follow-up).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, `undici` integration suite):** New `tests/integration/undici/` validates the npm `undici` package (Node's official HTTP/1.1 + WebSocket client) end-to-end on Node + GJS. 3 spec files (fetch-basic, request, websocket) exercise the HTTP surfaces every Node consumer uses — `undici.fetch()` (GET/POST/PUT/DELETE + headers + `.text()`/`.json()`/`.arrayBuffer()`), `undici.request()` (status codes + body streaming), and `undici.WebSocket` (handshake + send/receive + close). All specs start a local `http.createServer()` inline so the suite needs no network. Validates `@gjsify/{fetch,http,https,websocket}` against the canonical Node consumer. Local test counts pending until /tmp space recovers from concurrent-agent install churn; CI validates end-to-end.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, chalk integration suite scaffolded):** New `tests/integration/chalk/` suite ports the upstream `test/{chalk,level,instance}.js` ANSI escape-code + level-gating coverage into 3 spec files (`basic.spec.ts` 18, `templates.spec.ts` 11, `level.spec.ts` 17 — 46 assertions total). Validates the `@gjsify/{tty,process}` color/TTY-detection path that chalk's vendored supports-color probes (every CLI tool depends on chalk). Test counts on Node + GJS are pending a clean run; the suite-local install was interrupted by tmpfs disk-full on the shared worktree volume before extraction completed. See `### chalk` below. Source-only — no `@gjsify/*` source change anticipated since chalk 5 is pure-ESM JS over vendored ansi-styles + supports-color and bundles cleanly through `gjsify build` on both runtimes.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, `debug` integration suite):** New `tests/integration/debug/` validates the npm `debug` package (TJ Holowaychuk) end-to-end on Node + GJS. 3 spec files (enable-disable, format, output) exercise the TTY-color-aware `process.stderr.write` path that Express / socket.io / eslint / morgan all pull through — covers namespace matching with wildcards + exclusion, format specifiers (`%s` `%d` `%o` `%O` `%j` plus custom `%h`), and stripped-vs-colored output via a `process.stderr.write` capture swap (mirrors `debug`'s own sinon-stub pattern). Local test counts pending until /tmp space recovers from concurrent-agent install churn; CI validates end-to-end.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, TLS Phase 2 round-trip integration suite):** Closes acceptance-criteria items 5 + 6 of `docs/poc/tls-phase2-session-access.md` — the real-handshake session-resumption + channel-binding-byte tests that PR #360 explicitly deferred once the C shim landed. New `tests/integration/tls-session/` suite (sibling of `tests/integration/cosmiconfig/`): a self-signed cert+key fixture pair generated at prebuild time via `openssl req -x509 -nodes`, two specs both running on Node (reference) and GJS (validation). `session-resumption.spec.ts` opens conn 1 against a `node:tls.createServer`, captures the session blob via the `'session'` event, opens conn 2 with `{session}`, asserts `isSessionReused() === true` (TLS 1.2 forced for predictable ticket semantics). `channel-binding.spec.ts` runs a handshake, asserts `getFinished()`+`getPeerFinished()` return non-empty `Buffer`s of matching length, asserts they differ (peer Finished MAC ≠ local Finished MAC — identical bytes would indicate a `gnutls_channel_binding_t` handedness bug). Runs in TLS 1.2 (`tls-unique`) + TLS 1.3 (`tls-exporter`) modes via `{minVersion, maxVersion}`. The GJS branches additionally hard-assert `hasTlsSessionAccess() === true` so a degraded native bridge surfaces as a loud failure rather than a silent skip — the whole point of the suite is to validate the Phase 2 bridge IS working on glib-networking GnuTLS backends. No production code touched — packages/node/tls/ + packages/node/tls-native/ stay byte-identical. Both specs run via the standard `node:tls` alias (= `@gjsify/tls` on `--app gjs`), keeping the spec source identical across runtimes.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, http2 server/response.ts per-concern split):** Applies the `install*Methods(proto)` pattern (PRs #261, #273, #309) to `packages/node/http2/src/server/response.ts`. The 903-LoC monolith — `Http2ServerResponse` + `ServerHttp2Stream` + `Http2NativeBackend` — is split into four per-concern sibling modules under a new `src/server/response/` directory: `headers.ts` (159 LoC — `setHeader`/`getHeader`/`removeHeader`/`hasHeader`/`getHeaderNames`/`getHeaders`/`appendHeader`/`flushHeaders`/`writeHead`/`respond`/`writeContinue`/`writeEarlyHints`/`addTrailers`/`setTimeout`), `stream-io.ts` (168 LoC — `_startStreaming`/`_write`/`_final`/`_sendBatchResponse`/`end`, with the Writable lifecycle hooks deliberately excluded from the declaration-merge to avoid colliding with the inherited `Writable._write(chunk: any, ...)` overload — they still get attached to the runtime prototype via `Object.assign`), `respond-with-file.ts` (271 LoC — `respondWithFD`/`respondWithFile` + the file-local `_respondFromFD`/`_fdPath` helpers + the exported `StatCheck` type), and `push.ts` (289 LoC — both classes' `pushStream` entry points + `Http2ServerResponse.createPushResponse` + the file-local `_makeDetachedSoupMessage` factory; the two push surfaces stay together because they share the PUSH_PROMISE allocator + frame-builder coupling). Same shape as PR #309: each sibling exports a typed `*Methods` interface declaration-merged into the class via `declare module '../response.js'` and an `install<Group>Methods(proto)` function copying its `ThisType<>`-bound methods object onto the prototype; the base file wires all four in after the class declarations with the deliberate side-effect-import-next-to-named-import pattern so tsc preserves the augmentations in the emitted `.d.ts`. Base `response.ts` 903 → 288 LoC, owning the type definitions (`Http2NativeBackend`, `StatCheck`), both class declarations (fields + constructors + read-only getters), and `ServerHttp2Stream`'s delegate methods (the ones that just forward to `_res.*` without their own logic — `write`/`end`/`destroy`/`close`/`priority`/`setTimeout`/`sendTrailers`/`additionalHeaders`/`respondWithFD`/`respondWithFile`). Previously-`private` fields on both classes were demoted to default visibility with `@internal` JSDoc so sibling modules can access them via `this.*` (matches the webgl2-rendering-context split). Public surface preserved verbatim: every export from `@gjsify/http2` / `@gjsify/http2/server` works unchanged. 102 Node + 231 GJS tests still green; `tsc --noEmit` clean.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, `@gjsify/querystring` per-concern split):** Closes the `packages/node/querystring/src/index.ts` monolith (1165 LoC, a deno_std + Node.js `lib/querystring.js` port) by extracting four focused leaf modules under `src/internal/`: **`tables.ts`** (745 LoC, the four pre-computed `Int8Array` / `string[]` lookup tables — `hexTable`, `isHexTable`, `noEscape`, `unhexTable`), **`encode.ts`** (101 LoC — `encodeStr`, `qsEscape`, `ERR_INVALID_URI`), **`decode.ts`** (82 LoC — `unescapeBuffer`, `qsUnescape`), **`parse.ts`** (255 LoC — `parse`, `ParseOptions`, file-local `charCodes` + `addKeyVal`), **`stringify.ts`** (122 LoC — `stringify`, `StringifyOptions`, `stringifyPrimitive` + the two `encodeStringified*` helpers). The dependency graph is a strict DAG: `tables` ← `encode` ← `stringify`, `tables` ← `decode` ← `parse`, with the public `index.ts` (68 LoC) doing nothing but re-exports + the legacy `decode`/`encode`/`escape`/`unescape` aliases + the default-export object that matches `module.exports` shape for CJS consumers. Public API surface byte-identical: every spec import (`qs.parse`, `qs.stringify`, `qs.escape`, `qs.unescape`, `qs.unescapeBuffer`, `qs.decode`, `qs.encode`, `qs.ERR_INVALID_URI`, `ParsedUrlQuery` type) resolves through the same names; `tsc --noEmit` on the package is clean. No test edits, no `package.json#exports` change, no new external runtime deps.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, `@gjsify/webgl` shader-program per-concern split):** Third slice of the long-running `@gjsify/webgl` per-concern split, sibling to PR #309 + PR #273 (which split `webgl2-rendering-context.ts`). Decomposes the 1141-LoC monolithic `packages/framework/webgl/src/ts/context/shader-program.ts` into four per-concern sub-modules under `packages/framework/webgl/src/ts/context/shader-program/`: `shader-lifecycle.ts` (10 methods — `createShader`/`deleteShader`/`compileShader`/`shaderSource`/`getShaderInfoLog`/`getShaderParameter`/`getShaderPrecisionFormat`/`getShaderSource` + the GLSL `_checkShaderSource` tokenizer pass and the `_wrapShader` version/preamble injector, 274 LoC), `program-lifecycle.ts` (14 methods — `createProgram`/`deleteProgram`/`useProgram`/`linkProgram`/`validateProgram`/`attachShader`/`detachShader`/`bindAttribLocation`/`getAttachedShaders`/`getProgramInfoLog`/`getProgramParameter` + the `_switchActiveProgram`/`_fixupLink`/`_deleteLinkable` helpers; `MAX_UNIFORM_LENGTH`/`MAX_ATTRIBUTE_LENGTH` constants moved alongside `_fixupLink`/`bindAttribLocation` since they're spec-mandated limits scoped to those methods, 300 LoC), `introspection.ts` (5 methods — `getActiveAttrib`/`getActiveUniform`/`getAttribLocation`/`getUniformLocation`/`getUniform` — the `getUniformLocation` array-uniform branching block stays intact, 240 LoC), `uniforms.ts` (19 methods — the full `uniform[1234][fi]` scalar / `uniform[1234][fi]v` vector / `uniformMatrix[234]fv` square-matrix setter battery + the `_checkLocation`/`_checkLocationActive`/`_checkUniformValid`/`_checkUniformValueValid`/`_checkUniformMatrix` validation chain, 410 LoC). Same `install*Methods(proto)` shape as PR #309 / PR #273: each module declares a typed `<Group>Methods` interface, augments `ShaderProgramMethods` via `declare module '../shader-program.js' { interface ShaderProgramMethods extends <Group>Methods {} }`, declares the methods object with `ThisType<WebGLContextBase>`, and exports an `install<Group>Methods(proto)` function. Base file `shader-program.ts` 1141 → 51 LoC — now just the aggregate `ShaderProgramMethods` interface (empty — populated by the four `declare module` augmentations), the `declare module '../webgl-context-base.js' { interface WebGLContextBase extends ShaderProgramMethods {} }` link to the consuming class, and the composing `installShaderProgramMethods(proto)` that calls all four `install<Group>Methods(proto)` in order. Each `install<Group>Methods` is imported twice — once as a named import (for the call site) and once as a bare side-effect import (so tsc preserves the augmentation in the emitted `.d.ts`; same technique as the PR #309 + PR #273 splits). No `private` field had to be demoted because the original file already used `_`-prefixed package-internal fields throughout (`_gl`, `_shaders`, `_programs`, `_activeProgram`, `_extensions`, `_textureUnits`, `_checkOwns`, `_checkWrapper`, `_saveError`, `_restoreError`, `_validGLSLIdentifier`, `_getGlslVersion`) — all reachable through the `this: WebGLContextBase` typing in every extracted method. Public surface preserved verbatim: `installShaderProgramMethods` + the `ShaderProgramMethods` type alias still ship from `context/shader-program.ts`; the four focused `install<Group>Methods` are also re-exported from the root so a consumer that wants to install one group without the rest can reach the focused entry points without traversing internal paths. `context/index.ts` (the barrel that wires every method group into `WebGLContextBase.prototype`) needed no changes. 860 GJS webgl conformance tests still green. See `### Completed`.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, per-concern split of `@gjsify/webgl`'s `texture-management.ts`):** Third slice in the long-running `@gjsify/webgl` per-concern splits (siblings: PR #309 `webgl2-rendering-context.ts` 1245 → 866 + 4 modules; PR #273 the prior WebGL2 lifecycle slice). Same `install*Methods(proto)` pattern, applied this time to the largest remaining base-class method-group module — `packages/framework/webgl/src/ts/context/texture-management.ts` (941 LoC) — extracting it into five per-concern sibling modules under `context/texture-management/`. **Lifecycle** (`lifecycle.ts`, 229 LoC) — `createTexture` / `deleteTexture` / `activeTexture` / `bindTexture` / `pixelStorei` + the `_detachTextureFromAllFramebuffers` helper that detaches a doomed texture from every FBO that still references it. **2D texture upload** (`tex-image.ts`, 335 LoC) — `texImage2D` / `texSubImage2D` including the 6-arg-vs-9-arg / 7-arg-vs-9-arg overload dispatch, the `GdkPixbuf.Pixbuf` + `TexImageSource` source extraction, the `UNPACK_PREMULTIPLY_ALPHA_WEBGL` premultiplication step and the `UNPACK_FLIP_Y_WEBGL` row-reverse step. **Copy + compressed** (`copy-and-compressed.ts`, 184 LoC) — `copyTexImage2D` / `copyTexSubImage2D` / `compressedTexImage2D` / `compressedTexSubImage2D`. **Parameters** (`parameters.ts`, 111 LoC) — `texParameterf` / `texParameteri` / `getTexParameter` plus the `_getTexParameterDirect` helper that bypasses the parameter-validation switch and reads straight from the native GL. **Introspection** (`introspection.ts`, 187 LoC) — every `_`-prefixed helper the other modules reach for: `_getActiveTextureUnit` / `_getActiveTexture` / `_getTexImage` / `_checkTextureTarget` / `_validTextureTarget` / `_validCubeTarget` / `_verifyTextureCompleteness` / `_computePixelSize` / `_computeRowStride` / `_checkDimensions`. Each new module declaration-merges its `<Group>Methods` interface into `WebGLContextBase` via `declare module '../../webgl-context-base.js' { ... }` and exports an `install<Group>Methods(proto)` function. Base file `texture-management.ts` 941 → 61 LoC — now a barrel + composite `installTextureManagementMethods` that calls all five installs, plus a re-exported aggregate `TextureManagementMethods` interface (extends all five) so existing imports of just `TextureManagementMethods` keep compiling. Each per-concern install is imported twice — once as a named import for the call site and once as a bare side-effect import — so tsc preserves the augmentation in the emitted `.d.ts` (same technique as PR #309 / PR #273 / the canvas2d-core split in PR #262). `context/index.ts`'s `installAllContextMethods(WebGLContextBase.prototype)` aggregator is unchanged — it still imports the single `installTextureManagementMethods` from `texture-management.ts`, which now fans out to the five new installs internally. tsc `--noEmit` clean across `@gjsify/webgl`. 860 GJS webgl conformance tests still green (no count change).
> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, `upgrade --exclude-workspace` + CI dep-consistency gate):** Extends `gjsify upgrade` with an `--exclude-workspace <pattern>` flag (glob-matched, repeatable, comma-separated) and wires the resulting `upgrade --check` into the GJS workflow as a hard gate. `--exclude-workspace` reuses `@gjsify/workspace.filterWorkspaces`'s existing exclude option, so the same matching semantics as `--workspace` / `-p`. The CI step lives in `.github/workflows/main.yml` after the `Build` step (CLI's `lib/` populated, `node_modules/.bin/gjsify` callable) and runs `gjsify upgrade --check --exclude-workspace '@gjsify/integration-*'`. The carve-out reflects intentional drift in integration tests: `@gjsify/integration-mcp-typescript-sdk` pins `zod@4.3.6` (literal, exact match with the bundled mcp-typescript-sdk version); `@gjsify/integration-{deepkit-type-compiler,loro-crdt,typescript-tsc}` pin `typescript@^5.4.5` / `^5.7.3` because those upstream packages don't support TS 6 yet. Two new e2e cases cover the flag (`--check` passes after exclusion; glob pattern matches multiple). All 14 e2e tests green locally. After this PR and the companion `rolldown`/`vite` alignment PR land, the gjsify monorepo's external dep consistency is enforced on every PR.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-29, `gjsify upgrade` is workspace-aware):** Lifts `gjsify upgrade` from single-`package.json` to whole-workspace scope, with three new modes. `discoverContext()` walks up from cwd via `findWorkspaceRoot()` → calls `discoverWorkspaces()` from `@gjsify/workspace` to enumerate every package.json in the monorepo. Per-workspace dep declarations flatten into a `DepDeclaration` list (extended from the old `DepEntry` with `workspace` + `workspaceLocation` fields), then `groupByDependency()` aggregates by dep name → `DependencyGroup` exposes `occurrences`, `declaredRanges`, `dominantRange`, `highestVersion`. The interactive table gains a `fan` column (count of workspaces declaring the dep) and a magenta `⚠` badge on inconsistent rows (same dep, different ranges). Three new modes: `--align` (offline consistency-only — aligns every inconsistent dep to its highest declared version, preserving the dominant prefix), `--check` (CI gate — exits non-zero on any inconsistency, points operators at `--align`), `--workspace <pattern>` / `-p <pattern>` (restrict to a subset via `filterWorkspaces`'s glob matcher). Write-back groups by `workspace.location` so each affected `package.json` is read + serialized once even when the same dep upgrade hits multiple workspaces. Single-package mode preserved as fallback when `findWorkspaceRoot()` returns null. Aggregation helpers extracted to `packages/infra/cli/src/utils/dep-aggregation.ts` so they're independently importable for future tooling. `tests/e2e/upgrade/` expanded with 5 workspace-mode cases (aggregation, `--check` fail / pass, `--align` write-back, `--workspace` filter); all 12 e2e tests green locally.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.32, 2026-05-28, @gjsify/fetch native routing on Node + browser):** Closes the audit-classified `node:"none"`/`browser:"none"` gap for `@gjsify/fetch`. Source is gjs-bound (Soup-backed impl) — the first-pass audit (#362) conservatively suggested `"none"` for non-GJS slots, and the hardcoded `ALIASES_WEB_FOR_NODE` map left the bare `'fetch'` specifier explicitly unaliased on Node (consumers were expected to read `globalThis.fetch` directly). Now: new `packages/web/fetch/globals.mjs` re-exports `globalThis.{fetch, Request, Response, Headers, FormData}` (native on Node 18+ and modern browsers); `package.json#gjsify.runtimes` updated to `{gjs:"polyfill", node:"native", browser:"native"}`; the hardcoded `'fetch'` entry in `ALIASES_WEB_FOR_NODE` now routes to `@gjsify/fetch/globals` — same path as the dynamic resolver picks via the `node:"native"` slot. Both entry forms (`import 'fetch'` and `import '@gjsify/fetch'`) converge on the same re-export. `audit-runtimes --check` passes — the refined heuristic landed in #368 (gjs-bound + has globals.mjs → suggest `native`) now naturally agrees with the declaration.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.32, 2026-05-28, cross-runtime portability graduated out of experimental):** The strategic-direction section in `AGENTS.md` no longer carries the "experimental, internal" label or the blanket "do NOT document in website" gate. Four of the five axes have validated cross-runtime consumers that satisfy the original `≥2 real consumers` graduation criterion: **Axis 1 — Node-API**: `tests/integration/{streamx,webtorrent,socket.io,chokidar,dotenv,ts-for-gir,autobahn,…}` exercise `@gjsify/{fs,stream,buffer,path,events,…}` on both Node and GJS via 10+ ported upstream test suites; `ts-for-gir` itself is a dual-runtime consumer of `@gjsify/cli` + `@gjsify/path`. **Axis 2 — Web-API**: `tests/browser/` Playwright suite drives 12 packages cross-runtime — `abort-controller`, `compression-streams`, `dom-events`, `domparser`, `eventsource`, `fetch`, `formdata`, `message-channel`, `streams`, `webcrypto`, `websocket`, `webstorage`. **Axis 3 — DOM**: `pixel-rpg/map-editor` runs `@gjsify/dom-elements`+`@gjsify/canvas2d-core` via Excalibur.js on the GJS+GTK target; `showcases/dom/excalibur-jelly-jumper` mirrors the same path on the browser target. **Axis 4 — Design-identity**: was already exempt (pre-strategy) — `@gjsify/adwaita-*` shipped in 7 browser-target showcases plus `easy6502/app-web`. **Axis 5 — Platform-bridge** stays pre-graduation (single bridge, no external consumer yet); `refs/node-gtk` remains read-only reference. Avoid blanket "WinterCG-compatible" / "Node-compatible runtime" runtime-class claims — describe what's actually validated by name.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.32, 2026-05-28, @gjsify/dom-events Node routing fix):** Closes the known dom-events gap flagged in the v0.4.37 cross-runtime-exemplars entry. `ALIASES_WEB_FOR_NODE` previously routed the bare `dom-events` specifier to `@gjsify/dom-events/globals` on `--app node` — but `globals.mjs` is browser-targeted: it re-exports the full lib.dom event class hierarchy from `globalThis`, while Node 22 ships only `Event`/`EventTarget`/`CustomEvent` natively. Routing the bare specifier to the polyfill (`@gjsify/dom-events`) instead keeps the full event class hierarchy available on Node — the polyfill subclasses native `Event`/`EventTarget` where present and supplies the rest (`UIEvent`/`MouseEvent`/`KeyboardEvent`/`WheelEvent`/`PointerEvent`/`InputEvent`/`FocusEvent`/…). The `/globals` path stays reachable for `--app browser` builds via the dynamic per-runtimes-triplet resolver in `runtime-aliases.mjs` (browser slot = `native`, all classes present). `@gjsify/dom-events` now wires up `build:test:node` + `test:node` — exemplar package is finally validated on all three target runtimes per its declared triplet.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.32, 2026-05-28, cross-runtime test wiring batch + audit heuristic refinement):** Three follow-up tightenings of the cross-runtime foundation. **(a) Test wiring**: `@gjsify/eventsource` and `@gjsify/web-globals` now define `build:test:node` + `test:node` scripts and run them as part of their `test` lifecycle — both packages declare `node: "polyfill"` and were missing the per-target validation. `@gjsify/eventsource`'s aggregator now also runs `build:test:browser` (the script was defined but not invoked). **(b) Triplet refinement**: `@gjsify/websocket` was declared `{gjs:"polyfill", node:"none", browser:"none"}` by the first-pass audit because its impl is gjs-bound (Soup-backed). But it ships a `globals.mjs` re-exporting `globalThis.{WebSocket, MessageEvent, CloseEvent}` — Node 22+ and modern browsers have native WebSocket. Corrected to `{gjs:"polyfill", node:"native", browser:"native"}` — the resolver now routes the `@gjsify/websocket` specifier to `/globals` on non-GJS, matching the existing bare-`websocket`-specifier routing in `ALIASES_WEB_FOR_NODE`. **(c) Audit heuristic**: `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` refined to consider `globals.mjs` presence when classifying gjs-bound packages — packages with a `globals.mjs` re-export at the native value now suggest `native` for non-GJS slots instead of `none`. The refined heuristic prevents this same drift for any future gjs-bound-with-globals package. `audit-runtimes --check` passes across all 79 declarable packages.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.31, 2026-05-28, fs.rmSync workspace-source-wipe fix):** Second iteration of the [v0.4.29 install data-loss fix](#current-state-v0429-2026-05-27-install-data-loss-fix) — the original PR addressed the case where the native install backend's resolver leaked a workspace package into the fetch queue (path-shape guard + `workspaceInstall` symlink-on-any-name-match). A subsequent run from a real consumer surfaced a SECOND failure mode at a different layer: `@gjsify/fs.rmSync(path, { recursive: true })` itself follows symlinks at the top-level when the path is a symlink-to-directory, then walks the TARGET's children into the recursive descent — wiping every file under whatever the symlink points at. Trigger in the wild: `gjsify install` in a monorepo where `node_modules/@scope/<pkg>` is a preexisting symlink to a workspace source dir (a re-install after the very first install creates this state); the bin-link / pre-symlink cleanup `rmSync(linkPath, { recursive: true, force: true })` then deletes the workspace's `src/**`. Fix lands in `@gjsify/fs.rmSync` (`packages/node/fs/src/sync.ts`) and `fs.promises.rm` (`packages/node/fs/src/promises.ts`): probe the top-level path with `Gio.FileQueryInfoFlags.NOFOLLOW_SYMLINKS` instead of the default `NONE`; if `Gio.FileType.SYMBOLIC_LINK`, remove the link as one entry (`file.delete(null)`) and return — never descend. Matches Node's `fs.rmSync` semantics (a top-level symlink to a directory is removed as a single entry; the target is untouched). New regression spec `packages/node/fs/src/rm-symlink.spec.ts` covers the symlink-to-directory + symlink-to-file + plain-recursive-dir cases for both sync and promise APIs (5 tests). Verified end-to-end against `pixel-rpg/map-editor`: `gjsify install` now preserves `packages/{engine,gjs,story-gjs}/src/` + `games/zelda-like/{maps,spritesets,package.json}`. Total fs tests: 651 → 656.

> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-28, cross-runtime exemplars validated):** Three pure-TS `packages/web/*` packages (`@gjsify/abort-controller`, `@gjsify/dom-events`, `@gjsify/dom-exception`) are wired with per-target test scripts and now build cleanly on all three runtime targets they declare in `gjsify.runtimes`. Each package's `build:test` aggregator now runs `build:test:gjs && build:test:node && build:test:browser` (dom-events skips Node — see below); each produces a `gi://`-free `dist/test.browser.mjs` bundle picked up by `tests/browser/` Playwright discovery. Added `src/test.browser.mts` for `@gjsify/dom-exception` (was missing) modeled on the existing browser-only test pattern (browser globals only — no `@gjsify/*` value imports — per the `### Browser tests` contract in AGENTS.md). Local validation: `abort-controller` 23/23 on Node + 23/23 on GJS, `dom-exception` 64/64 on Node + 64/64 on GJS, `dom-events` 274/274 on GJS. AGENTS.md `## Strategic direction` now names these three as the canonical exemplars for new cross-runtime packages. Known pre-existing gap (NOT touched here): `@gjsify/dom-events` Node test routing — the `dom-events` bare specifier aliases to `@gjsify/dom-events/globals` (re-export of `globalThis.{Event,EventTarget,…}`) on Node, which is incomplete because Node 22 lacks `UIEvent`/`MouseEvent`/`KeyboardEvent`/etc. — and the `globals.mjs` re-export pattern is the wrong choice for a slot declared `"polyfill"`. Either route should change to the actual polyfill or the runtime triplet should switch to `"native"` — tracked for a follow-up; the `test:node` script is intentionally not wired on dom-events so this gap doesn't break CI.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.31, 2026-05-28, cross-runtime routing live — resolver consults `gjsify.runtimes` triplet):** The bundler's alias resolver now derives `@gjsify/<X>` routing per `--app` target from each package's declared `gjsify.runtimes` triplet, on top of the hardcoded `ALIASES_*_FOR_*` baseline. New `packages/infra/resolve-npm/lib/runtime-aliases.mjs` walks the workspace `packages/**` (in-repo builds) or the consumer's `node_modules/@gjsify/*` (installed builds) once at config-time, caches the triplets, and emits per-target alias maps with one rule per slot: `polyfill`/`partial` → no rewrite (keep `@gjsify/<X>` → resolves to the polyfill at `lib/esm/index.js`); `native` → `@gjsify/<X>/globals` (re-exports the runtime-native value); `none` → `@gjsify/empty`. The derived layer is composed UNDER the hardcoded maps (hardcoded wins on conflict) so every existing consumer's behavior is preserved verbatim — only `@gjsify/<X>`-direct imports for packages not already in the hardcoded map gain new routes. A `native`-declared package missing its `globals.mjs` re-export file falls back to `@gjsify/empty` with a one-shot `[@gjsify/resolve-npm] ...` warn-once, surfacing the gap to maintainers. **`globals.mjs` files added (22 new):** 19 Node-API natives `assert | async_hooks | buffer | cluster | console | constants | diagnostics_channel | domain | events | https | inspector | querystring | readline | stream | string_decoder | sys | timers | util | vm` each `export * from 'node:<X>'; export { default } from 'node:<X>'`; 2 Web-API browser natives `domparser | webstorage` re-export the matching browser globals (`DOMParser`, `Storage`/`localStorage`/`sessionStorage`); 1 expanded `web-globals` aggregator + empty stubs for the `node-polyfills` / `web-polyfills` meta packages. Each ships `./globals` in the package.json `exports` map + `globals.mjs` in `files` + `./globals.mjs` in `sideEffects`. New `tests/e2e/runtimes-routing/run.mjs` (6 cases) verifies the routing end-to-end: Node-target bundles for `@gjsify/{assert,util}` contain `'node:<X>'` strings (re-export route fired); GJS-target bundles for the same packages contain NEITHER (polyfill route fired); the hardcoded `webcrypto` bare-specifier route still wins (regression guard); GJS-target `@gjsify/events` stays on the polyfill (negative). Two `browser:"native"` packages — `@gjsify/canvas2d-core`, `@gjsify/dom-elements` — intentionally lack a browser `globals.mjs` (their DOM surfaces are heavy and best left to the resolver's warn-once + empty fallback for now). Public surface, CLI flags, and tooling unchanged — internal-only routing improvement.

> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-28, TLS Phase 2 Path-A native flip):** `@gjsify/tls-native` Phase 2 SessionAccess is **functional** end-to-end against glib-networking's GnuTLS backend (Fedora 43+ / gjs 1.86+ default). The Phase-2 POC scaffold from #342 is flipped from "always throw NOT_SUPPORTED" to real `gnutls_session_t`-backed calls via a new C shim (`packages/node/tls-native/src/c/gjsify-tls-private.{c,h}`) that reaches into `GTlsConnectionGnutls`'s private struct using the public `g_type_instance_get_private` + a runtime `g_type_from_name("GTlsConnectionGnutls")` lookup. Struct layout vendored verbatim from `refs/glib-networking/tls/gnutls/gtlsconnection-gnutls.c` @ `be1c870` (master HEAD at submodule-add) — the 4-pointer `{credentials, session, interaction_id, cancellable}` layout is stable across glib-networking 2.74–2.84 (covers Fedora 43 + 44). `is_supported()` now returns `true`; `hasTlsSessionAccess()` accordingly. Force-loads the dynamic GIO TLS module via `g_tls_backend_get_client_connection_type()` on each entry point so the type registers even when no connection has been instantiated yet. Backward-compatible cleanly: a hypothetical `GIO_USE_TLS=openssl` backend selection returns `is_supported() === false`, and all `TLSSocket.{getFinished,getPeerFinished,getSession,setSession,isSessionReused}` accessors degrade to the same `undefined`/`false`/no-op contract Node uses on a build without session support. JS-side surface from #342 unchanged — flipping the predicate flips every consumer transparently. Updated unit specs (11 in `@gjsify/tls-native`, 13 in `@gjsify/tls`) to assert the new `true`-by-default; pre-handshake graceful-degradation assertions stay since a `TLSSocket` constructed without going through `connect()` still has no bound `_tlsConnection`. Refreshed `.so`/`.gir`/`.typelib` prebuilds (linux-x86_64). Real round-trip resumption + channel-binding-byte assertions land at the integration layer once a fixture TLS server is in place.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.31, 2026-05-28, `audit-runtimes --check` mode + CI drift guard):** `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` gains a `--check` mode that re-uses the existing source-tree signal probes (`@girs/*` value-imports, `gi://` URL imports, dynamic `import('gi://...')`, legacy `imports.X` globals, `<obj>.imports?.gi` guarded access) to compare every package's declared `gjsify.runtimes` triplet against what the script would suggest today. If any slot drifts — e.g. a previously pure-TS package added a `@girs/*` value-import without flipping its `node`/`browser` slots to `none`, or a new package landed without a triplet at all — the script exits 1 with a per-package diff (`declared:` vs `suggested:`, which slots mismatch, which source signals drove the suggestion, and a pointer to the AGENTS.md strategy section). A new `.github/workflows/audit-runtimes.yml` workflow (Node-only, Ubuntu runner, no `yarn install`) runs `node scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs --check` on every PR and on pushes to `main`, so the runtime-triplet declarations stay honest as the source code evolves. Pure read-only; never edits `package.json`. Current state of `main`: 79 declarable packages all match the signal-based suggestion, 21 infra/build-tooling packages are skipped (not user-facing runtime polyfills). Local sanity: `node scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs --check` exits 0 today; flipping any single slot in a `package.json#gjsify.runtimes` reproduces the drift failure with a clear error message.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.31, 2026-05-28, cross-runtime strategy declared — `gjsify.runtimes` triplet on all packages):** Introduced the AGENTS.md `## Strategic direction — cross-runtime portability (experimental, internal)` section. GJS stays the primary target; the long-term vision is "alles unter allem lauffähig" — share what's shareable across GJS × Node × Browser, runtime-specific where not. Five orthogonal axes (Node-API, Web-API, DOM, design-identity, platform-bridge). Each package declares a runtime-triplet via `package.json#gjsify.runtimes` (`{gjs, node, browser}` × `{polyfill, native, partial, none}`). Strategy is **opportunistic, not driven**: existing GJS-bound packages stay GJS-only (no refactor budget); NEW pure-TS-portable packages get cross-runtime treatment from day one. **Foundational landing in this PR:** (a) new `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` classifies every `packages/**/package.json` on five signals (`@girs/*` value-imports, `gi://` URL imports, dynamic `import('gi://...')`, legacy `imports.X` globals, `<obj>.imports?.gi` guarded access) → suggests a default triplet → optionally applies it via `--apply` (never overwrites existing); (b) the suggested triplet is now declared on all 79 user-facing `@gjsify/*` packages (21 infra/gjs packages skipped — build tooling, not runtime polyfills); (c) `refs/node-gtk` added as read-only submodule for the platform-bridge axis (Node-on-GTK reference; NOT actively worked). Until proven on ≥2 real cross-runtime consumers: NOT documented in website / `gjsify --help` / README / package READMEs; runtime claims withheld. `@gjsify/adwaita-*` is the exception (already shipping to browser via showcases, counts as Production for the design-identity axis).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.31, 2026-05-28, node-target bundles loadable on Node — gi:// + imports.byteArray cascade):** Fixed a two-layer regression on `main` where node-target test bundles crashed at module init. **Layer 1 — `gi://` / `@girs/*` leak:** `gjsify build --app node` left bare `gi://Gio?version=2.0` / `@girs/gio-2.0` imports in the bundle (they were externalised, but the `@girs/*` package entry contains a top-level `import 'gi://Gio?version=2.0'` that Node's default ESM loader rejects with `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_ESM_URL_SCHEME`). The `--app browser` target already redirects these specifiers to an empty virtual ESM module via `gjsImportsEmptyPlugin` — that same plugin is now wired into `app/node.ts` and the `gi://` / `@girs/` branches removed from the `external` predicate. Same composition order as `app/browser.ts` (`gjsImportsEmptyPlugin` runs in `resolveId` order: 'pre', before `aliasPlugin`). **Layer 2 — top-level `imports.byteArray` access:** with the gi:// layer resolved, the bundle next hit `ReferenceError: imports is not defined` on `const byteArray = imports.byteArray;` at the top of `@gjsify/utils/{file,gio,cli}.ts` and `@gjsify/os/linux.ts` — the GJS legacy global doesn't exist under Node. Fix: move the `imports.byteArray` access INSIDE the function bodies that use it (functions only ever run on GJS, gated by `on('Gjs', ...)` / `typeof imports !== 'undefined'` checks at the call site), so the modules' top level stays free of a hard `imports` reference. Both layers surfaced by the v0.4.37 TLS Phase 2 POC: the new `session-access.gjs.spec.ts` imports from `@gjsify/tls` directly (per CLAUDE.md rule 2b for `*.gjs.spec.ts`), which transitively pulls in `connect.ts` (`import Gio from '@girs/gio-2.0'`) → `tls-server.ts` → `@gjsify/utils` (`const byteArray = imports.byteArray;`) → the bundle. Pre-#342 specs only imported `node:tls`, so neither value chain reached the node test bundle. Net effect: `@gjsify/tls test:node` (and every future node-target bundle that touches `@girs/*` or `@gjsify/utils` transitively) loads cleanly under Node again; `main`'s `GJS` workflow is un-red.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.34, 2026-05-29, flatpak .desktop MimeType= from provides.mimetypes):** `gjsify flatpak init`'s `.desktop` renderer now emits a `MimeType=` line sourced from `gjsify.flatpak.provides.mimetypes` (the same field that already populates `<mediatype>` entries in the MetaInfo XML — one knob configures both surfaces). Required for apps that handle custom URL schemes (e.g. `pixelrpg://join/<roomid>` for the PixelRPG map editor's Pair-Editing flow) or specific file MIME types: without `MimeType=` set, the desktop session has no way to route browser/chat-client URL clicks to the app. Backward compatible — apps that omit `provides.mimetypes` get no `MimeType=` line (no stray blank line either; the placeholder collapses cleanly). One-line `desktop.tmpl` change + 8 lines in `renderDesktop`. Tests: existing 136 CLI tests still green (renderDesktop has no dedicated test today; covered indirectly by manual `gjsify flatpak init` against the PixelRPG map editor's package.json with mimetypes set). 

> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-28, no-explicit-any sweep + chokidar/dotenv/TLS Phase 2 consolidation):** Patch release rolling up 24 commits since v0.4.30. Headline: **`typescript/no-explicit-any` is 0 repo-wide** — 13 PRs (#346-#358) typed every `any` across `@gjsify/{node-utility,fs,small-node,sqlite,crypto,vala-bridges,ws,http2,webrtc,webgl,node-mixed,web-small,url,domain,bridge-types,formdata,inspector,sab-native,compression-streams,web-globals}` (~890 → 0 repo-wide warnings). Public Node/W3C/npm-`ws`/WebRTC API surfaces preserved verbatim (no `any → unknown` narrowing of consumer-facing types, lesson from the #334/#336 cascade), with strengthening fixes where the new types caught real ambiguity (`dns.promises.resolve` got overloaded `Promise<string[]>` / `Promise<unknown[]>` signatures; `fs.fstat`/`ftruncate`/`readv`/`writev` properly delegate to `*Async`; `os.constants` and the `constants` re-export are now typed `number`; `cluster.fork()` returns `never`; ecdsa buffers typed `Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>`). Pervasive-intent disables use file-level `// oxlint-disable typescript/no-explicit-any -- <reason>` with concrete justification; isolated casts use `as unknown as <T>` or structural intersections. Other consolidated work: chokidar integration suite (4 ports, 19 Node / 19 GJS) + `FSWatcher` root-cause fix emitting Node-contract `'change'` events with `(eventType, basename)` (unblocks Vite/Rolldown/tsc-watch under GJS); dotenv integration suite (4 ports, 127 Node / 127 GJS) validating the `@gjsify/process` `env` Proxy round-trip through `GLib.{get,set,unset}env`; `@gjsify/tls-native` Phase 2 POC scaffold landing the surface (`hasTlsSessionAccess()`, `TlsChannelBindingType`, `TLSSocket.{getFinished,getPeerFinished,getSession,setSession,isSessionReused}`, `'session'` event, `tls.connect({session})`) ahead of the native flip (gated on `refs/glib-networking/` for the `gnutls_session_t` struct-layout source-of-truth).

> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-27, @gjsify/tls Phase 2 POC):** `@gjsify/tls-native` Phase 2 (TLS session resumption + channel binding) lands as a POC scaffold (Path B). New native `GjsifyTls.SessionAccess` Vala class wraps a `Gio.TlsConnection` and exposes the same surface (`is_session_reused` / `get_session_data` / `set_session_data` / `get_channel_binding` / `get_finished` / `get_peer_finished` / `get_negotiated_protocol_version`) the real implementation will use — currently every gnutls-bound method throws `SessionAccessError.NOT_SUPPORTED` because extracting `gnutls_session_t` from `Gio.TlsConnection`'s GIO-internal private struct is gated on adding a `refs/glib-networking/` submodule for the struct-layout source-of-truth (open question documented in `docs/poc/tls-phase2-session-access.md`). The JS-side surface is fully wired: `@gjsify/tls` now exposes `hasTlsSessionAccess()` (predicate, currently `false`), `TlsChannelBindingType` (RFC 5929 / 9266 constants), `TLSSocket.getFinished()` / `getPeerFinished()` / `getSession()` / `setSession()` / `isSessionReused()` (all degrading to `undefined` / `false` / no-op while the bridge is non-functional, matching Node's "build without session support" contract), the `'session'` event (no-op until bridge flips), and the `{session}` option on `tls.connect()`. Surface tests in `packages/node/tls-native/src/ts/index.gjs.spec.ts` (+5 SessionAccess assertions, now 11 total) and `packages/node/tls/src/session-access.gjs.spec.ts` (+13 surface assertions) lock the contract in BEFORE the native flip — when the GIO struct-layout work resolves (~50-line change in `_resolve_native_session()`), `hasTlsSessionAccess()` returns `true` and every consumer becomes functional transparently with zero API-shape migration. Surface tests: 11 (tls-native) + 13 (tls) = 24 new specs.

> **Current state (v0.4.29, 2026-05-27, un-red `main`):** PR #340 now repairs BOTH the #334 and #336 type-narrowing regressions so `main`'s CI goes green end-to-end. #334's repair fixed the clean-`build` step (two public type surfaces consumers depend on); #336's repair fixes the `check` step — 45 `tsc --noEmit` errors across `tests/integration/{streamx,ts-for-gir,autobahn,webtorrent}` where #336 over-narrowed working `any` spec code to `@types/*`/`unknown` shapes that don't model the real npm-package runtime surface. All restored with concrete narrowing (no `any` reintroduced, no `@types` augmentation, no assertions weakened). VERIFIED airtight: clean full `build` (all `lib/`+`*.tsbuildinfo` nuked) → 0 errors, `check` → 0 errors, `lint` → 0 errors. See `### Completed` for the per-file breakdown.
> **Current state (v0.4.29, 2026-05-27, install data-loss fix):** `gjsify install` no longer fetches/extracts a published npm tarball over a workspace's OWN source tree — a data-loss bug where the native backend `rmSync`+over-extracted a publishable workspace's `src/**`. `workspaceInstall` now symlinks ANY dep whose name matches a local workspace (not just explicit `workspace:` specs; plain semver / `*` / `latest` count too), and `extractOne` gained an `assertNodeModulesDest()` guard that refuses to extract into a `dest` (or symlink-resolved realpath) outside a `node_modules/` directory. External installs / workspace symlinks / lockfile writes / `gjsify pack`'s `files` filter all preserved; native backend stays the default. New regression e2e `tests/e2e/install-workspace-source-safety/` (3 tests, fails-before/passes-after). See `### Completed`.
> **Current state (v0.4.29, 2026-05-27, chokidar integration suite):** New `tests/integration/chokidar/` ports 4 spec files from chokidar 5.0.0's `src/index.test.ts` (basic add/change/unlink/rename events, recursive watching with `depth` boundaries, regex/function `ignored` patterns, `awaitWriteFinish` stabilityThreshold semantics) into `@gjsify/unit` style. **Node: 19/19 green. GJS: 19/19 green.** First real-world end-to-end consumer of `@gjsify/fs`'s `FSWatcher` (`Gio.FileMonitor`-backed) implementation under GJS — chokidar is the file-watching surface every TypeScript-aware dev tool (Vite, Rolldown, esbuild `--watch`, tsc `--watch`) depends on, so a passing port unblocks the future "Vite-dev under GJS" workstream. Root-cause fix landed in same PR: **`FSWatcher` now emits the Node-contract event shape** — `this.emit('change', 'rename', basename)` instead of `this.emit('rename', ...)` on directory entry create / delete / rename. Node's `fs.watch` documents a single `'change'` event with `(eventType, filename)` listener signature where `eventType ∈ {'rename','change'}`; our previous impl used `'rename'` as the event NAME, so any consumer following Node's contract (chokidar, Vite, tsc `--watch`, esbuild `--watch`) silently dropped 16/19 cases. chokidar's npm tarball does not ship its own tests, so the ports cite paulmillr/chokidar@5.0.0 `src/index.test.ts` directly with SPDX + attribution headers per the integration-suite convention.

> **Current state (v0.4.29, 2026-05-27, un-red `main`):** PR #340 now repairs BOTH the #334 and #336 type-narrowing regressions so `main`'s CI goes green end-to-end. #334's repair fixed the clean-`build` step (two public type surfaces consumers depend on); #336's repair fixes the `check` step — 45 `tsc --noEmit` errors across `tests/integration/{streamx,ts-for-gir,autobahn,webtorrent}` where #336 over-narrowed working `any` spec code to `@types/*`/`unknown` shapes that don't model the real npm-package runtime surface. All restored with concrete narrowing (no `any` reintroduced, no `@types` augmentation, no assertions weakened). VERIFIED airtight: clean full `build` (all `lib/`+`*.tsbuildinfo` nuked) → 0 errors, `check` → 0 errors, `lint` → 0 errors. See `### Completed` for the per-file breakdown.
> **Current state (v0.4.29, 2026-05-27, install data-loss fix):** `gjsify install` no longer fetches/extracts a published npm tarball over a workspace's OWN source tree — a data-loss bug where the native backend `rmSync`+over-extracted a publishable workspace's `src/**`. `workspaceInstall` now symlinks ANY dep whose name matches a local workspace (not just explicit `workspace:` specs; plain semver / `*` / `latest` count too), and `extractOne` gained an `assertNodeModulesDest()` guard that refuses to extract into a `dest` (or symlink-resolved realpath) outside a `node_modules/` directory. External installs / workspace symlinks / lockfile writes / `gjsify pack`'s `files` filter all preserved; native backend stays the default. New regression e2e `tests/e2e/install-workspace-source-safety/` (3 tests, fails-before/passes-after). See `### Completed`.
> **Current state (v0.4.29, 2026-05-27, dotenv integration suite):** New `tests/integration/dotenv/` ports the upstream dotenv v17.4.2 suite (`tests/test-parse.js`, `test-parse-multiline.js`, `test-config.js`, `test-populate.js`) into 4 `@gjsify/unit` spec files — small but a load-bearing cross-runtime smoke test for `process.env` mutation (the `@gjsify/process` Proxy round-tripping through `GLib.{get,set,unset}env` on GJS) plus `@gjsify/fs.readFileSync` of `.env` files with `URL`-instance path argument. Mirrors the cosmiconfig template (setup-fixtures.mjs writes the upstream `.env` / `.env.local` / `.env.multiline` fixtures verbatim; `gjsify build --app {node,gjs}` produces both bundles; `test:node` + `test:gjs` run separately). sinon-based stubs (CLI tip rotation, `os.homedir` mock for `~/.env` expansion, fs/parse stubs) are intentionally skipped — they exercise mocking infra, not dotenv or `@gjsify/*` semantics. All keys touched in `process.env` are reset in `try/finally` so suites don't leak state into each other. Real-world consumer test for `process.env` was previously sparse (per-package unit tests in `@gjsify/process` set/delete keys directly; no third-party consumer exercised the surface end-to-end). **Node: 127/127 green. GJS: 127/127 green, 0 skips. No `@gjsify/*` fix required** — the `process.env` Proxy, `readFileSync` with string + `URL` path, and `Buffer.from()` input all round-trip cleanly on both runtimes. > **Current state (post-v0.4.30, 2026-05-28, no-explicit-any wave 2b — long tail → 0 repo-wide):** Eliminated the final 11 `typescript/no-explicit-any` warnings across 8 small packages: `@gjsify/sab-native` (1), `@gjsify/compression-streams` (2), `@gjsify/web-globals` (1), `@gjsify/bridge-types` (2), `@gjsify/url` (1), `@gjsify/formdata` (1), `@gjsify/inspector` (1), `@gjsify/domain` (1). Source fixes: `url.URL #uri` typed `GLib.Uri` (was annotated `any // GLib.Uri`); `domain.members` typed `EventEmitter[]` (was `any[]`); `bridge-window.getComputedStyle` typed against lib.dom (`Element` / `CSSStyleDeclaration`). Two per-line `oxlint-disable-next-line` retained where the public API genuinely uses `any` per the standard: `FormData.forEach`'s `thisArg?: any` (matches lib.dom's intentional choice — the callback's `this` binding type isn't expressible) and `inspector.Session.post`'s `_params`/`result` (protocol-defined arbitrary objects per `@types/node`). Spec deliberate-invalid-input casts (`SharedBuffer.create(1.5 as any)`, `new CompressionStream('invalid-format-xyz' as any)`, `(globalThis as any).File`) converted to structural `as unknown as <T>` / `as { File?: unknown }`. **Repo-wide: 11 → 0** — the entire `typescript/no-explicit-any` lint surface is now clean.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.30, 2026-05-28, no-explicit-any wave 2b — `@gjsify/ws`):** Eliminated all 81 `typescript/no-explicit-any` warnings in `@gjsify/ws` — the npm-`ws`-compat drop-in (WebSocket client + WebSocketServer over Soup.Server + `@gjsify/websocket`). Source (21, all FIXED, no new disables): `websocket.ts` — `_native: any` retyped to a structural `NativeWebSocketLike` interface (the subset we delegate to: `binaryType`, `protocol`/`extensions` readers, typed `addEventListener(type, listener)`, `send(string|ArrayBuffer|ArrayBufferView|Blob)`, `close(code?, reason?)` — chosen structurally so the runtime binding can be either `@gjsify/websocket`'s Soup-backed class or the host's native global without coupling to lib.dom); the four `(ev: any)` native event listeners (`message`/`close`/`error`/`open`) typed to a `NativeEvent` shape with all the fields we read defensively (`data`/`code`/`reason`/`wasClean`/`error`/`message`); `_nativeSend` `payload: unknown` narrowed to `string | ArrayBuffer | ArrayBufferView | Blob` via a typed switch on input shape (`Buffer.isBuffer(data)` → `slice` to ArrayBuffer; ArrayBuffer/view passthrough; primitive coercion); the `_eventTargetListeners` map + `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener`/`_dispatchEvent` (the W3C-style API ws exposes) typed to a `WsListener = (ev: WsListenerEvent) => void` shape covering type/target/data/code/reason/wasClean/error/message — matches @types/ws's `(event: Event|MessageEvent|CloseEvent|ErrorEvent) => void` listener signature structurally. `websocket-server.ts` — `handleUpgrade(req, socket, head, cb)` + its 4 private helpers (`_validateUpgradeHeaders`/`_completeUpgrade`/`_abortHandshake`/`_buildVerifyClientInfoFromReq`) retyped to two new structural `UpgradeRequest` (Node `IncomingMessage`-shaped: `method`/`url`/`headers`/`socket.remoteAddress`/`socket.remotePort`) and `UpgradeSocket` (`Duplex`/`net.Socket`-shaped: `write(chunk, cb?)`, optional `destroy`, optional `_releaseIOStream(): Gio.IOStream | null` for the `@gjsify/net` hook) interfaces — keeps the file free of a hard `@gjsify/http`/`@gjsify/net` import. `stream.ts` — `createWebSocketStream(ws: any, options)` retyped to `(WebSocket | WSLike)` where `WSLike` is a structural ws-compatible class shape (`readyState` + 4 readyState constants + `on`/`once` with `(...args: unknown[])` variadic listeners + `send`/`close`/`terminate`/optional `pause`/`resume`); the per-listener `(msg: Buffer | string, isBinary: boolean)`/`(err: Error)` arrow params widened to `(...args: unknown[])` + per-slot narrowing casts to satisfy the structural EventEmitter `on`/`once` signature without dropping precision. Spec files (60): all 4 specs (`index.spec.ts`/`stream.spec.ts`/`websocket-server.spec.ts`/`websocket-server.spec.ts`) got a file-level `// oxlint-disable typescript/no-explicit-any -- <tailored reason>` header — each reason explains *why* `any` is the right tool there (assert ws's CJS post-load self-references; demonstrate npm-`ws`-shape consumer code with `(req, socket, head)` / `(info, cb)` / `(ws)` / `(msg)` callback shapes that real consumers write without importing internal types; GI introspection boundary on the Soup.Session websocket_connect_async callback). **EXTRA CAUTION OBSERVED — never narrowed public ws types:** the public `WebSocket` instance API (`'message'`/`'open'`/`'close'`/`'error'` event-handler signatures, `send(data, …)` payload union, W3C-style `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` consumer-callback type, `binaryType` setter union, `ClientOptions`/`ServerOptions`/`VerifyClientInfo` interfaces) all stayed concrete or were typed to ws-library-compatible shapes (`WsListener`/`NativeWebSocketLike`/`UpgradeRequest`/`UpgradeSocket`). No `unknown`-narrowing of consumer surfaces (which broke #334/#336); the private internals (`_native`, `_eventTargetListeners`, the upgrade-handshake helpers) carry the structural narrowing — consumers (socket.io, webtorrent, autobahn driver) see exactly the same public surface. No public type narrowed to `unknown`, no `.oxlintrc.json` change, no test assertions weakened, no Soup-boundary justified-disable needed (the GI hooks `add_handler`/`add_websocket_handler`/connection signals already had typed `Soup.ServerMessage`/`Soup.WebsocketConnection`/`GLib.Bytes`/`GLib.Error` params before this PR — kept intact). VERIFIED: `@gjsify/ws` `oxlint` 0 `no-explicit-any`, `tsc --noEmit` 0 real errors (one `Cannot find module '@gjsify/http'` filtered — worktree build-ordering, present on `origin/main`). Repo-wide: ~538 → ~457.

Source (35): `linkable.ts` `_references: any[]` and `_link`/`_unlink`/`_linked(b: any)` retyped to `Linkable` (the base class of every GL resource: `WebGLProgram`/`WebGLBuffer`/`WebGLTexture`/...); `types/extension.ts` `ExtensionFactory` return changed from `any|null` to `ExtensionInstance|null` (`type ExtensionInstance = object` — extension classes have concrete heterogeneous shapes consumers narrow with `instanceof`); MAX_EXT: GLenum } | undefined` etc.) across `webgl-context-base.ts`/`context/state.ts`/`context/drawing.ts`/`context/texture-management.ts`/`context/framebuffer.ts`/`webgl-rendering-context.ts`/`webgl-framebuffer.ts` — gives accurate per-extension typing at each consumer without a giant union type; `utils.ts` `checkObject(object: any)` → `unknown`, `(pixels as any).width/height` → `(pixels as { width?: number })`, `flag<T = Record<string, any>>` → `unknown`; `extensions/oes-texture-half-float.ts` `(context as any)._native2` → structural `{ _native2?: unknown }`; `conformance/setup.ts` `(canvas as any).getContext('webgl2')` → direct typed call (the W3C overload supports the string). Eight justified per-line `// oxlint-disable-next-line typescript/no-explicit-any` remain — every one byte-matches a lib.dom WebGL spec signature whose pname-dependent return value is genuinely `any` per the W3C specification: `getQueryParameter` / `getSamplerParameter` / `getSyncParameter` (interface declarations + impls in `webgl2-context/object-lifecycle.ts`) and `getActiveUniformBlockParameter` / `getActiveUniforms` / `getIndexedParameter` / `getInternalformatParameter` / `getParameter` (in `webgl2-rendering-context.ts`). Same lib-dom-byte-match precedent as the streams wave's `CountQueuingStrategy.size` / `TransformStream.readable`/`.writable`. Spec files (34): `webgl1.spec.ts` (13, file-level disable — dynamic GL-constant name indexing `gl[name]` over `GL_CONSTANT_NAMES` + impl-private `_native` Gwebgl Vala bridge access for `getParameterb`/`getParameterbv`/`getParameterf`/`getParameterfv`/`getParameteri`/`getParameteriv`) and `conformance/buffers.spec.ts` (4, file-level disable — deliberately-invalid `bufferData`/`bufferSubData` args testing INVALID_VALUE error path per the Khronos conformance suite) each got a file-level `oxlint-disable` with a tailored reason. The remaining 17 isolated `(x as any)` patterns FIXED, not silenced: `conformance/state.spec.ts` (9) Float32Array/Int32Array index casts dropped (the typed array types already index numerically) + `(gl as any)[cap]` → `(gl as unknown as Record<string, number>)[cap]`; `conformance/context.spec.ts` (5) per-cast structural narrowings (`Record<string, unknown>` for method-existence loops, `Record<string, number>` for constant loops, `{ getContext?: unknown }` / `{ WebGLRenderingContext?: unknown }`); `conformance/uniforms.spec.ts` (1) typed-array index cast dropped; `conformance/programs.spec.ts` (2) deliberately-null `getActiveAttrib`/`getActiveUniform` calls retyped to `(gl as { getActiveAttrib: (p: WebGLProgram | null, i: number) => unknown })`. No public type narrowed to `unknown` past what lib.dom already specifies, no `.oxlintrc.json` change, no test assertions weakened. Spec — `client.spec.ts` `(http.IncomingMessage as any)()` zero-arg construction → `as unknown as new () => InstanceType<…>` (test divergence from `@types/node`); `(agent as any)` field accesses removed by extending the existing local `interface Agent` augmentation with `protocol`/`maxFreeSockets` (the impl HAS them; `@types/node` doesn't expose). `extended.spec.ts` got a file-level header for the `validateHeaderName/Value(123 as any)` deliberate-invalid pattern. `listen-error.spec.ts` `Promise<any>` → `Promise<NodeJS.ErrnoException & { port?: number }>`; `createBiquadFilter(): any → AudioNode`; `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` `_listener: any` → `((event: Event) => void) \| null`; `gst-player.ts` + `html-audio-element.ts` `_pipeline: any`/`_volumeElement: any`/`(_bus: any, msg: any)`/`(_appsrc: any, ...)` retyped to `Gst.Pipeline`/`Gst.Element`/`Gst.Bus`/`Gst.Message`/`GstApp.AppSrc` (gst+gstapp typelibs are already in `tsconfig.types`; `Gst.parse_launch` returns a typed `Gst.Element` cast to `Gst.Pipeline` to use `get_by_name`). **iframe**: spec — 10× `new IFrameWindowProxy(mockBridge as any)` replaced by a `MockMessageBridge = Pick<MessageBridge, 'sendToWebView' \| 'getLocation'>` helper + `asMessageBridge(mock)` cast through `unknown` (1 well-named conversion vs 10 scattered `as any`s); `(sentData as any).hello` → `{ hello: string }` cast; `((event: Event) => void) as any` listener cast removed (the dom-events `EventListenerOrEventListenerObject` already accepts the function shape). **event-bridge**: source — `getElement: () => { dispatchEvent(event: any): boolean } \| null` typed to dispatch `OurEvent` (the `Event` re-export from `@gjsify/dom-events`); spec — `(canvas as any)` cast removed (now structurally compatible), `(e: any)` filter callbacks typed to a `FakeBridgeEvent = OurEvent & { clientX?, clientY?, … }` union covering the fields the tests read. **canvas2d**: source — `(canvas: any, options?: any) =>` callback in `registerContextFactory` typed via the signature `(canvas: HTMLCanvasElement, options?: unknown) => unknown` already declared in `@gjsify/dom-elements`; `(canvas as any)[CONTEXT_KEY]` → local `type CanvasWithContext = … & { [CONTEXT_KEY]?: CanvasRenderingContext2D }` cast; `_onDraw(_area, cr: any, …)` typed to `Cairo.Context` (the Cairo `cairo` type was already imported in a sibling). **video**: source — `Gst.init(null as any)` → `Gst.init()` (the typed `init(argv?: string[])` accepts no args); `buildMediaStreamPipeline(gstSource: any, gstPipeline: any)` → `Gst.Element` / `Gst.Pipeline | null` plus `GstSourceTrack._gstSource?: unknown` → `Gst.Element` (and `_gstPipeline`/`_gstTee` to their concrete `Gst.*` types). > **Current state (post-v0.4.30, 2026-05-28, no-explicit-any wave 2b — `@gjsify/http2`):** Seventh slice of the post-#334/#336 `no-explicit-any` cleanup. Drives `typescript/no-explicit-any` to **0** in `@gjsify/http2` (the second-largest cluster after web-streams) — **105 warnings eliminated** across the Soup-backed compat layer (Http2ServerRequest/Response + ClientHttp2Session/Stream over Soup.Session ALPN-h2), the native h2c dispatcher path (`@gjsify/http2-native`/nghttp2 bridge), and the two GJS-only specs. Source-file strategy (25 fixes, 0 disables): `connect()`'s callback `socket: any` → `Socket | TLSSocket` via a `ConnectSocket` alias (Node-compatible — currently `null` at runtime since the transport is Soup-managed); `Http2ServerRequest.socket: any` → `Socket | TLSSocket | null` matching Node's public shape (the two write sites in http2-server.ts widen via a structured-cast through `as unknown as NonNullable<typeof req.socket>` with a comment "GJS partial-Socket layer" — the runtime object carries only `{remoteAddress,remotePort,localAddress,localPort,encrypted}` since GJS has no real `net.Socket`); `Http2ServerResponse._write`/`ClientHttp2Stream._write` `chunk: any` → `string | Buffer | Uint8Array` with a typeof-narrow before `Buffer.from()` (same pattern as the #350 hash/hmac retype — straight `string|Buffer|Uint8Array` triggers a Buffer.from overload mismatch on the union, the explicit narrow fixes it); `respondWithFile`/`respondWithFD`/`_respondFromFD`'s 5 inline `statCheck?: (stat: any, headers: any, statOptions: any) => void` consolidated into a single `StatCheck` type alias importing `fs.Stats` + `http.OutgoingHttpHeaders`, with `void | boolean` return so the `if (cont === false)` cancel-send branch type-checks (Node parity); `ServerOptions.{Http1IncomingMessage,Http1ServerResponse}: any` → `new (...args: unknown[]) => unknown` constructor-shape (option fields kept for Node-shape compat — gjsify doesn't dispatch through them); `client-session.ts` `Soup.Session.send_async` callback `_self: any` → `Soup.Session | null` matching `Gio.AsyncReadyCallback<Soup.Session>`'s real signature; `catch (error: any)` → `catch (error: unknown)`; `index.spec.ts`'s isolated `(getPackedSettings as any)()` → `(getPackedSettings as unknown as () => Uint8Array)()`. Spec-file strategy: both `*.gjs.spec.ts` (80 of 105) received file-level `oxlint-disable typescript/no-explicit-any` with tailored reasons — `http2.gjs.spec.ts` (57) for pervasive `(x as any)` casts at the `node:http2` ↔ `@gjsify/http2` impl-private boundary (pushStream callbacks, ServerHttp2Stream internals, options-bag deliberate-invalid construction), `http2-native-dispatch.gjs.spec.ts` (23) for the GI-introspection boundary (dynamic `gi://Gio`/`gi://GLib`/`gi://GjsifyHttp2` imports + raw `headers_received`/`data_received`/`push_promise_received` GObject signal handlers marshalling `GLib.Variant`/`GLib.Bytes` payloads — same precedent as #348 process/streams.ts and #351 http2-native session-bridge spec). Per the AGENTS.md cautionary precedent (`#334`/`#336` cascade), NEVER narrowed any of the public Http2* types (`Http2ServerRequest`/`Http2ServerResponse`/`ClientHttp2Session`/`ClientHttp2Stream`/`ServerHttp2Stream`/`IncomingHttpHeaders`/`OutgoingHttpHeaders`) to `unknown` — they stay byte-for-byte Node-compatible. No `.oxlintrc.json` blanket disables, no test assertions weakened. Verified locally: `oxlint -c .oxlintrc.json packages/node/http2/src` → **0 `no-explicit-any`**; `gjsify workspace @gjsify/http2 check` → 0 real type errors (worktree "Cannot find module '@gjsify/*'" build-ordering artifacts filtered per AGENTS.md). Repo-wide enters at ~538; this slice clears 105 → ~433. See `### Completed`.

Source (24, all FIXED bar two W3C-spec-required justified disables): `tee-multiplexer.ts` (8) — `_tee`/`_pipeline` fields, constructor + accessor signatures retyped to `GstNs.Element`/`GstNs.Pipeline`/`GstNs.Pad` with a new `TeeElement extends GstNs.Element { allow_not_linked: boolean }` interface for the runtime-only GObject property; `media-stream-track.ts` (6) — `_gstSource`/`_gstPipeline`/`_gstTee`/`_teeMultiplexer` plus the `MediaStreamTrackGstInit` shape retyped to `GstNs.Element | null` / `GstNs.Pipeline | null` / `TeeMultiplexer | null` (this STRENGTHENS the public class shape — consumers of `MediaStreamTrack` now read concrete Gst types instead of `any`; the two `RTCRtpSender` local-narrowing intersections that previously re-typed these fields locally became redundant and were dropped); `rtc-rtp-receiver.ts` (3) — `_pipeline: any` → `Gst.Pipeline | null`, constructor `pipeline?: any` → `pipeline?: Gst.Pipeline`, and the `new (ReceiverBridge as any)({…})` cast cleaned to direct `new ReceiverBridge({ pipeline, kind })` (matches the `Partial<ConstructorProps>` GIR-typed signature with the now-concrete pipeline type); `media-devices.ts` (1) — `let gstDevices: any[]` → `Gst.Device[]` (the `Gst.DeviceMonitor.get_devices()` return is `Device[] | null` per GIR); `gst-stats-parser.ts` (1) — duck-type check `(nested as any).n_fields` → `(nested as { n_fields?: unknown }).n_fields` (structural narrow on the unboxed GstStructure); `rtc-peer-connection/data-channel.ts` (1) — `catch (err: any)` → `catch (err)` with `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)` Node-style narrowing; `rtc-peer-connection.ts` + `rtc-data-channel.ts` (2 KEPT) — the local `EventHandler<E> = ((this: <Class>, ev: E) => any) | null` type alias matches W3C Web IDL `EventHandlerNonNull` exactly (lib.dom uses `=> any` on every `GlobalEventHandlers.on*` field; the `any` is required so the setter accepts handlers whose return value is ignored per spec) — carry a per-line `oxlint-disable-next-line` with a "matches lib.dom" reason. `wpt-helpers.ts` (4) — `dc.onerror = (ev: any)` callbacks retyped to `(ev: RTCErrorEvent)` (the precise W3C event type that carries `.error`), `awaitMessage`'s `addEventListener('error', …)` typed against `Event` + `as RTCErrorEvent` narrow at the `.error` read. Spec files (82): 4 each got a file-level `// oxlint-disable typescript/no-explicit-any -- <SPECIFIC tailored reason>` header — `register.spec.ts` (11, `(globalThis as any).<W3C ctor>` existence probes verifying `/register` writes happened), `webrtc.spec.ts` (33, deliberate-invalid construction `(pc as any).addTrack({})` + `catch (e: any)` DOMException-name assertions + `(ev: any)` ICE-candidate callbacks + `(track as any)._teeMultiplexer` impl-private reads), `wpt.spec.ts` (27, same patterns + `(cfg1 as any).iceServers` invalid-config exercise + `as Promise<any>` race-helper adapters + `invalidBinaryTypes: any[]` enum-bound exercise), `wpt-media.spec.ts` (13, same family + `generateAnswer(offer: any): Promise<any>` typed cleanly to `RTCSessionDescriptionInit` inputs/outputs). No public W3C type narrowed to `unknown` (verified per the #334/#336 hard rule), no `.oxlintrc.json` change, no test assertions weakened. The class-level retyping of `MediaStreamTrack._gstSource` (and siblings) was verified compatible with the only out-of-package consumer (`@gjsify/video`'s `video-bridge.ts` intersection type uses `_gstSource?: unknown` which intersects cleanly with the new `GstNs.Element | null`).
