> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-27, un-red `main`):** PR #340 now repairs BOTH the #334 and #336 type-narrowing regressions so `main`'s CI goes green end-to-end. #334's repair fixed the clean-`build` step (two public type surfaces consumers depend on); #336's repair fixes the `check` step — 45 `tsc --noEmit` errors across `tests/integration/{streamx,ts-for-gir,autobahn,webtorrent}` where #336 over-narrowed working `any` spec code to `@types/*`/`unknown` shapes that don't model the real npm-package runtime surface. All restored with concrete narrowing (no `any` reintroduced, no `@types` augmentation, no assertions weakened). VERIFIED airtight: clean full `build` (all `lib/`+`*.tsbuildinfo` nuked) → 0 errors, `check` → 0 errors, `lint` → 0 errors. See `### Completed` for the per-file breakdown.
> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-27, install data-loss fix):** `gjsify install` no longer fetches/extracts a published npm tarball over a workspace's OWN source tree — a data-loss bug where the native backend `rmSync`+over-extracted a publishable workspace's `src/**`. `workspaceInstall` now symlinks ANY dep whose name matches a local workspace (not just explicit `workspace:` specs; plain semver / `*` / `latest` count too), and `extractOne` gained an `assertNodeModulesDest()` guard that refuses to extract into a `dest` (or symlink-resolved realpath) outside a `node_modules/` directory. External installs / workspace symlinks / lockfile writes / `gjsify pack`'s `files` filter all preserved; native backend stays the default. New regression e2e `tests/e2e/install-workspace-source-safety/` (3 tests, fails-before/passes-after). See `### Completed`.

> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-27, Biome → oxc):** `gjsify format` / `gjsify lint` / `gjsify fix` now wrap [oxc](https://oxc.rs/) (`oxlint` 1.66+ + `oxfmt` 0.51) instead of Biome — full replacement, no `--engine` fallback. New `utils/oxc-resolve.ts` resolves the `oxlint`/`oxfmt` npm **Node launchers** (`node_modules/<tool>/bin/<tool>`) and spawns them via `process.execPath` (required because oxlint's JS-plugin host lives in the launcher, not a bare binary). Root `.oxlintrc.json` (recommended→correctness:error, useImportType→typescript/consistent-type-imports, noExplicitAny→typescript/no-explicit-any:warn, useNodejsImportProtocol→unicorn/prefer-node-protocol:error, noUnusedImports→eslint/no-unused-vars:warn) + `.oxfmtrc.json` (printWidth 120, 4-space, singleQuote, semi, trailingComma all, arrowParens always). `gjsify fix` = `oxfmt --write` then `oxlint --fix`; `format --init` writes both config files from new templates baked into the bundle via the static-read-inliner. **CSS/JSON formatting is DROPPED** — oxfmt formats JS/TS(+TOML) only; no replacement formatter for CSS/JSON. New internal oxlint JS plugin `packages/infra/oxlint-plugin-gjsify/` ships a `gjsify/register-class-order` rule (hoists `static` GObject metadata above a `GObject.registerClass` static block, with autofix; not published to npm). e2e ported `tests/e2e/biome/` → `tests/e2e/oxc/` + new `tests/e2e/oxc-plugin/`; `biome.json.tmpl` + `biome-resolve.ts` removed; oxc VS Code extension recommended. `--globals auto` detection stays on acorn (AGENTS.md: oxc's parser must not be repurposed for global detection). oxfmt is pre-1.0 — expect a possible re-reformat per oxfmt minor until 1.0. The codebase-wide lint pass is now clean: `gjsify lint` reports **0 errors** (2.5k `no-explicit-any`/`no-unused-vars` warnings remain, acceptable). All error-level `correctness` findings were FIXED not silenced — autofixes (prefer-node-protocol, consistent-type-imports, spread) plus a manual longtail: `new Array(n)`→`Array.from({length})`, redundant parameter-property assignments dropped, a duplicate top-level `const seen` renamed, dead code/unreachable statements removed, and sqlite's `allowUnknownNamedParameters` enforcement actually implemented (was a stored-but-unused flag). Intentional patterns (NUL/ESC regexes, GObject `static new()` `.d.ts` factories, deliberate class+interface merging, NAPI finally-rethrow, the Excalibur points-setter self-assign, GJS `imports[name]` access) carry per-line `oxlint-disable-next-line` with a reason; `no-this-alias` uses `allowedNames` for the `this`-capture idiom. Earlier (dom-elements, #329): `@gjsify/dom-elements` now reflects the GTK allocation in EVERY `HTMLElement` layout-dim getter — `clientWidth`/`clientHeight`, `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`, and `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` all share one `_allocatedClientWidth`/`_allocatedClientHeight` cache; `notifyElementResize()` writes the new size on the target AND every ancestor (same walk as the resize-subscriber dispatch). Fixes Excalibur.js 0.32's `Screen.FillContainer` reading `canvas.offsetWidth` (FillContainer sets `canvas.style.{width,height} = '100%'` and then reads offset dims) after a `ResizeObserver` fire and getting `0` → blank canvas (the PixelRPG map-editor "map disappears on resize below the tablet breakpoint" bug; the app-side workaround in `engine.ts` can be removed once the map-editor bumps the gjsify version). Adds a new `resize-observer.spec.ts` case locking the contract in across all three getter families (uses a fresh `HTMLElement` wrapper instead of `document.body` to keep the assertion independent of prior tests). 461 dom-elements tests green. Earlier today: `gjsify publish --otp <code>` closes the Node-free manual-bootstrap gap: the `npm-otp` header is now forwarded on the PUT request, so maintainers can do the first publish of a new `@gjsify/*` scoped package without falling back to `npm publish`. Implements npm's `otplease` behavior (refs/npm-cli/lib/utils/auth.js): on a non-TTY (CI) a clear error points at `--otp`; on an interactive TTY the CLI prompts once and retries. Three new e2e cases cover the OTP header assertion, the non-TTY EOTP error path, and the `--otp`-on-first-PUT success path (all using in-process mock servers). AGENTS.md step 2 of the bootstrap procedure updated to use `gjsify publish --access public --otp <code>`; cli-reference.md documents the new `--otp` option. Earlier: `gjsify publish --otp <code>` closes the Node-free manual-bootstrap gap: the `npm-otp` header is now forwarded on the PUT request, so maintainers can do the first publish of a new `@gjsify/*` scoped package without falling back to `npm publish`. Implements npm's `otplease` behavior (refs/npm-cli/lib/utils/auth.js): on a non-TTY (CI) a clear error points at `--otp`; on an interactive TTY the CLI prompts once and retries. Three new e2e cases cover the OTP header assertion, the non-TTY EOTP error path, and the `--otp`-on-first-PUT success path (all using in-process mock servers). AGENTS.md step 2 of the bootstrap procedure updated to use `gjsify publish --access public --otp <code>`; cli-reference.md documents the new `--otp` option.
> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-27, fetch Soup.Session GC fix):** `@gjsify/fetch` now holds ONE process-wide `Soup.Session` at module scope instead of `new Soup.Session()` per `Request`. A per-request session became GC-eligible once the `Response` body was read and could be finalized by SpiderMonkey while a libsoup connection was still registered on its host — libsoup's connection-manager teardown then fired `libsoup-CRITICAL **: runtime check failed: (host->conns == NULL)` on stderr during heavy fetch use (e.g. `@gjsify/npm-registry`'s packument/tarball fetches in `gjsify install`). The shared session is never finalized mid-flight (and reuses keep-alive connections); matches the `@gjsify/timers` GLib/Soup BoxedInstance GC-safety precedent. New GJS-only `soup-session.gjs.spec.ts` regression test (shared-session invariant + subprocess stderr scan). Earlier today (Biome → oxc): `gjsify format` / `gjsify lint` / `gjsify fix` now wrap [oxc](https://oxc.rs/) (`oxlint` 1.66+ + `oxfmt` 0.51) instead of Biome — full replacement, no `--engine` fallback. New `utils/oxc-resolve.ts` resolves the `oxlint`/`oxfmt` npm **Node launchers** (`node_modules/<tool>/bin/<tool>`) and spawns them via `process.execPath` (required because oxlint's JS-plugin host lives in the launcher, not a bare binary). Root `.oxlintrc.json` (recommended→correctness:error, useImportType→typescript/consistent-type-imports, noExplicitAny→typescript/no-explicit-any:warn, useNodejsImportProtocol→unicorn/prefer-node-protocol:error, noUnusedImports→eslint/no-unused-vars:warn) + `.oxfmtrc.json` (printWidth 120, 4-space, singleQuote, semi, trailingComma all, arrowParens always). `gjsify fix` = `oxfmt --write` then `oxlint --fix`; `format --init` writes both config files from new templates baked into the bundle via the static-read-inliner. **CSS/JSON formatting is DROPPED** — oxfmt formats JS/TS(+TOML) only; no replacement formatter for CSS/JSON. New internal oxlint JS plugin `packages/infra/oxlint-plugin-gjsify/` ships a `gjsify/register-class-order` rule (hoists `static` GObject metadata above a `GObject.registerClass` static block, with autofix; not published to npm). e2e ported `tests/e2e/biome/` → `tests/e2e/oxc/` + new `tests/e2e/oxc-plugin/`; `biome.json.tmpl` + `biome-resolve.ts` removed; oxc VS Code extension recommended. `--globals auto` detection stays on acorn (AGENTS.md: oxc's parser must not be repurposed for global detection). oxfmt is pre-1.0 — expect a possible re-reformat per oxfmt minor until 1.0. The codebase-wide lint pass is now clean: `gjsify lint` reports **0 errors** (2.5k `no-explicit-any`/`no-unused-vars` warnings remain, acceptable). All error-level `correctness` findings were FIXED not silenced — autofixes (prefer-node-protocol, consistent-type-imports, spread) plus a manual longtail: `new Array(n)`→`Array.from({length})`, redundant parameter-property assignments dropped, a duplicate top-level `const seen` renamed, dead code/unreachable statements removed, and sqlite's `allowUnknownNamedParameters` enforcement actually implemented (was a stored-but-unused flag). Intentional patterns (NUL/ESC regexes, GObject `static new()` `.d.ts` factories, deliberate class+interface merging, NAPI finally-rethrow, the Excalibur points-setter self-assign, GJS `imports[name]` access) carry per-line `oxlint-disable-next-line` with a reason; `no-this-alias` uses `allowedNames` for the `this`-capture idiom. Earlier (dom-elements, #329): `@gjsify/dom-elements` now reflects the GTK allocation in EVERY `HTMLElement` layout-dim getter — `clientWidth`/`clientHeight`, `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`, and `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` all share one `_allocatedClientWidth`/`_allocatedClientHeight` cache; `notifyElementResize()` writes the new size on the target AND every ancestor (same walk as the resize-subscriber dispatch). Fixes Excalibur.js 0.32's `Screen.FillContainer` reading `canvas.offsetWidth` (FillContainer sets `canvas.style.{width,height} = '100%'` and then reads offset dims) after a `ResizeObserver` fire and getting `0` → blank canvas (the PixelRPG map-editor "map disappears on resize below the tablet breakpoint" bug; the app-side workaround in `engine.ts` can be removed once the map-editor bumps the gjsify version). Adds a new `resize-observer.spec.ts` case locking the contract in across all three getter families (uses a fresh `HTMLElement` wrapper instead of `document.body` to keep the assertion independent of prior tests). 461 dom-elements tests green. Earlier today: `gjsify publish --otp <code>` closes the Node-free manual-bootstrap gap: the `npm-otp` header is now forwarded on the PUT request, so maintainers can do the first publish of a new `@gjsify/*` scoped package without falling back to `npm publish`. > **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-26):** New `@gjsify/vite-plugin-gjsify` infra package lands the **Vite-plugin track** — `gjsifyBrowser()` is a Vite preset that mirrors `gjsify build --app browser` (the same `@girs/*`/`gi://` → empty redirect, browser polyfill aliases, `import`/`browser` conditions, `esnext` target, blueprint + opt-in deepkit), so a dual-target web app's Vite dev/HMR experience matches its `--app browser` production output — deliberately **minus css-as-string** (browser apps want Vite's native CSS pipeline). Hardened `gjsImportsEmptyPlugin`'s `resolveId` with an internal `@girs/`/`gi://` guard so it's portable under Vite (the Rolldown `filter` stays as a fast-path; zero regression to the CLI). New `vite-plugin-gjsify` e2e runs a programmatic `vite build` and asserts no `gi://`/`@girs/` leak + `node:assert`→`@gjsify/assert` resolved. **⚠ NEW PACKAGE — needs manual first-publish + npm Trusted Publisher before the next `release.yml` run (see Open TODOs).** Earlier: `gjsify build --shebang` now emits a target-appropriate shebang for `--app node` (`#!/usr/bin/env node`) as well as `--app gjs` — so a Node CLI bin (e.g. ts-for-gir migrating off `esbuild.ts`) is directly executable; new `shebang-node` e2e. Also: `gjsify build` now produces location-independent GJS bundles. A bundled dep's runtime data-file reads (a dep loading its own i18n / `package.json` / templates via `import.meta.url`) resolve from the bundle's ACTUAL location at run time via `@gjsify/module`'s PnP-aware `createRequire` (`shims/module-resolve.ts` + a one-line bundle-URL anchor banner), instead of a path baked relative to the BUILD location. Fixes published GJS bundles crashing once installed at a different `node_modules` depth (the bundle sits at `node_modules/<pkg>/bin/…`, so the baked `../../../node_modules/<dep>` doubled to `node_modules/node_modules/<dep>` — e.g. ts-for-gir's globally-installed `typedoc` read). The path rewriter (`rewrite-node-modules-paths.ts`) was refactored into per-case functions (runtime-resolve / legacy / PnP-zip / CJS); the new behavior is gated to `--app gjs` ESM via `runtimeResolve` (node + non-ESM keep the legacy build-relative path = zero regression). New e2e `published-bundle-reads` relocates a built bundle to a deeper `node_modules` layout and asserts a dynamic dep read still resolves under GJS 1.88. Earlier (post-compression-streams, #312): the polyfill `CompressionStream` no longer races its lazy zlib load (the `gjsify pack` fix). Earlier (post-install-pnp-guard): `gjsify install` now errors with actionable guidance when it detects Yarn PnP residue (`.pnp.cjs` / `.pnp.loader.mjs`) — it names the residue files and points at the two real fixes (remove the residue, or switch yarn to `nodeLinker: node-modules`) instead of recommending the `GJSIFY_INSTALL_BACKEND=npm` escape hatch, which can't resolve `workspace:` specs (the exact second wall ts-for-gir [#392](https://github.com/gjsify/ts-for-gir/issues/392) hit). Native PnP generation stays out of scope (byte-identical `.pnp.cjs` + cache would mean replicating yarn's libzip/checksum/virtual-locator internals for a single yarn release). Earlier (post-webgl2-method-groups-split): Second slice of the `webgl2-rendering-context.ts` split (sibling to PR #273). Extracts the four method groups PR #273 left as explicit follow-up candidates — `texImage3D` family (7 methods → `webgl2-context/tex-image-3d.ts`), unsigned-integer + non-square matrix uniforms (14 methods → `webgl2-context/uniform.ts`), `clearBuffer{fv,iv,uiv,fi}` family (4 methods → `webgl2-context/clear-buffer.ts`), and instanced-draw + framebuffer-ops (12 methods → `webgl2-context/instanced-and-framebuffer-ops.ts`). 37 methods totalling ~380 LoC moved out of the base. Same `install*Methods(proto)` shape as the PR #273 `object-lifecycle.ts` split: typed `*Methods` interface declaration-merged into `WebGL2RenderingContext` via `declare module`, methods object with `ThisType<WebGL2RenderingContext>`, plus an `install<Group>Methods(proto)` function. File-local `DebugCounters` / `debugGlEnabled()` helper and `OptionalClearBufferNative` interface moved alongside the methods that use them. Base file `webgl2-rendering-context.ts` 1245 → 866 LoC; calls all 5 install functions after class declaration with the deliberate side-effect-import-next-to-named-import pattern so tsc preserves the augmentations in the emitted `.d.ts`. 860 GJS webgl conformance tests still green. Earlier (post-as-any-longtail-1cast): Phase G.5 lands `gjsify upgrade` — a drop-in replacement for `yarn upgrade-interactive` / `npx npm-check-updates`. Interactive prompt by default (index ranges, `a`/`all`, comma+space separators); non-interactive bulk-update modes via `--latest` (allows major), `--minor` (skips major), `--patch` (patches only). Workspace-aware: skips `workspace:` / `file:` / `link:` / `git:` / `git+` / `http(s):` / `npm:` / `*` / `latest` specs and only checks real npm dependencies. Range prefix is preserved on write-back (`^1.2.3` → `^2.0.0`, `~0.4.0` → `~0.5.0`). Registry resolution honors layered `.npmrc` lookup (home → cwd) with `npm_config_registry` env var winning. Parallel fetch with 8-worker concurrency cap via `@gjsify/npm-registry`'s `fetchPackument`. Color-coded diff table (red=major / yellow=minor / green=patch / cyan=prerelease). 7-test e2e suite stands up a mock npm registry in-process via `http.createServer()` — the test runner uses `execFile` (async) rather than `execFileSync` so the in-process mock can actually serve responses (synchronous spawn blocks the event loop and starves the HTTP server). Covers `--latest --dry-run` (no write-back), `--latest` (write with prefix preserved), `--patch` / `--minor` semver gating, `--filter` substring narrowing, `workspace:` ranges skipped, empty-deps graceful exit. Earlier (post-Phase-G.4): Phase G.4 lands `gjsify test` — a build+run+aggregate runner that collapses the per-package `build:test:{gjs,node}` + `test:{gjs,node}` + `test` script boilerplate. Locates `src/test.mts`, builds it in-process via `BuildAction` for each requested runtime (`--runtime gjs|node|all`), runs each output (`runGjsBundle` for GJS, `spawn('node', …)` for Node), prints a per-runtime summary, and aggregates exit codes. mtime-based skip-when-fresh (overridable via `--rebuild`); `--no-build` runs an existing bundle. 5-test e2e suite covers happy path, failing spec, `--rebuild`, `--no-build`, missing entry. Earlier (post-Phase-F.9.7): Phase F.9.7 is the small-but-load-bearing fix surfaced by the easy6502 migration: `gjsify.flatpak.name` config field overrides the derived display name in MetaInfo `<name>` + .desktop `Name=`. Previously `flatpak init` ran the npm `package.json#name` through `friendlyName(pkgName, appId)` to pick a default — works when name is the reverse-DNS app id, breaks when it's an npm shape like `learn6502` vs display `"Learn 6502 Assembly"`. Fix is one optional field + a 1-line `flatpak.name ?? friendlyName(...)` precedence rule in init; full backwards compatibility. Companion: easy6502 (`JumpLink/Learn6502#96`) consumes this in its migration off hand-written manifest + MetaInfo + .desktop, regenerating all four Flathub assets from a single `package.json#gjsify.flatpak` config block (including translator hints for all 12 releases). Earlier in this stretch (post-Phase-F.9.6): Phase F.9.6 extended the F.9 scaffold to cover the full AppStream surface — `description` accepts a `DescriptionBlock[]` union (mixed `<p>` paragraphs and `<ul>/<li>` bullet lists), per-release `<description>` blocks (same shape), translator hints (`<!-- TRANSLATORS: ... -->` emitted before every translatable tag for `xgettext`/`msgfmt --xml --template` to forward into `.po` files), `<developer><email>` + `<name translate="no">`, content_rating attributes (`<content_attribute>` children), kudos, provides (binaries + mimetypes + dbus), supports/requires/recommends (controls + display_length + internet), iconRemote, translateUrl, plus screenshot captions with their own translator hints. Backwards compatible: the simple `description: "string"` form still works. The scaffold now produces MetaInfo XML rich enough to match hand-written Flathub manifests like easy6502 / Workbench, making multi-language Flathub apps generatable from a single `package.json#gjsify.flatpak` config block. Implementation: scaffold.ts rewritten from template-substitution to direct XML construction (~330 LOC builder; the old `metainfo-{app,cli}.xml.tmpl` files are removed). e2e suite extended 13 → 14 tests. Earlier in this stretch (post-Phase-F.9): Phase F.9 extended `gjsify flatpak init` to scaffold a full Flathub-ready asset bundle in one invocation — manifest JSON + MetaInfo XML (`desktop-application` or `console-application`, kind-aware) + `.desktop` entry (apps only) + `flathub.json` policy stub. New `--kind <app|cli>` flag (replaces `--cli-only`, which stays as a deprecated alias); new `gjsify flatpak check` command runs Flathub linters locally (appstreamcli + flatpak-builder-lint). Earlier (post-sab-native-Phase-1): `@gjsify/worker_threads` × `@gjsify/sab-native` Phase 1 landed. `Worker.postMessage(value, [sab])` and `workerData: { sab }` now route `SharedBuffer` instances across the subprocess-Worker boundary. Parent spawns each Worker with a `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socketpair (`FdChannel.makePair()`), passes the child end as inherited fd 3 via `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.take_fd`, and per-message walks the value tree for `SharedBuffer` instances, SCM_RIGHTS-sends each fd tagged with a sequence counter, then writes a JSON line with `{__sab: tag, size: N}` placeholders. The bootstrap counts placeholders per inbound JSON message and synchronously drains exactly that many fds from fd 3 — kernel buffering guarantees the fds are ready, since `sendmsg(SCM_RIGHTS)` always completes before `write(stdin)` in the parent — then materialises each placeholder via `SharedBuffer.fromFd(fd, size)`. Cross-process futex (`FUTEX_WAIT` / `FUTEX_WAKE` without the `_PRIVATE` flag) lets workers signal completion on shared barrier addresses. 4 new worker_threads unit tests + 1 new integration test (4 workers × SHA-256 over 1 MiB SharedBuffer; XOR-folded digest matches the in-process reference). Total: 275/275 GJS unit + 1034/1034 GJS integration + 270/270 Node unit + 1169/1169 Node integration, all green. Earlier (2026-05-18, sab-native Phase 0): the Vala/C bridge itself — `SharedBuffer` (`memfd_create(2)` + `mmap(MAP_SHARED)`, SEQ_CST `__atomic_*`, futex wait/notify), `FdChannel` (socketpair + SCM_RIGHTS), 18 GJS specs green including a real cross-process round-trip via `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.take_fd(fd, 3)`. Wire protocol is byte-order-explicit LE so a buffer written on x86_64 reads identically on s390x. Earlier (2026-05-18, Phase F.8): Phase F.8 added `gjsify uninstall -g <pkg>` as the symmetric inverse of `install -g`, with a 4-test e2e suite. Phase F.5 fully migrated ts-for-gir CI to the Node-free `install.mjs` bootstrap (Fedora 43 container, dnf prereqs, all four workflows; three iteration rounds caught environment quirks at the source). End-to-end users can now install AND uninstall GJS apps with the same one-line `curl … | gjs -m -` story, no Node or npm required. Earlier (2026-05-17, post-Phase-F-MVP): Phase F MVP landed `install.mjs` (root) + `gjsify self-update` + `gjsify generate-installer`, plus a release-workflow asset-upload step. End-users can now bootstrap `@gjsify/cli` (or any GJS-runnable npm package) with a single `curl -fsSL .../install.mjs | gjs -m -` line — no Node or npm required on the user's machine. Three cross-cutting Soup3-stream fixes shipped alongside (Accept-Encoding: identity in npm-registry, PARTIAL_INPUT-as-EOF in utils.readBytesAsync, gjs-wrapped launcher for `.gjs.mjs` bins in install-global). The new flow is gated behind the next gjsify release (which produces the `install.mjs` + `cli.gjs.mjs` + `.sha256` GitHub-release assets); CI migration of gjsify-self + ts-for-gir lands in the follow-up PR. Earlier (2026-05-17, post-Phase-E): Phase E landed `gjsify pack` + `gjsify publish` as first-class commands, replacing the last `npm pack` + `npm publish` shellouts in the gjsify release pipeline. End-to-end verified against the live npm registry in v0.4.37; every `@gjsify/*` tarball now ships with resolved `^0.4.37` dep ranges (no leaked `workspace:^`). Three sub-bugs surfaced + fixed during the v0.4.6 → v0.4.37 hotfix stretch: wire-filename uses unscoped basename per npm convention, URL-encoding uses npm-package-arg's `@${scope}%2f${name}` shape with lowercase `%2f`, auth lookup honors `NPM_CONFIG_USERCONFIG` (where `actions/setup-node@v6` writes the auth-token npmrc). ts-for-gir's cross-repo migration ([gjsify/ts-for-gir#393](https://github.com/gjsify/ts-for-gir/pull/393)) bumped to `@gjsify/cli@^0.4.37` and switched `publish:app`/`publish:types` to `gjsify publish`. Earlier in this stretch (2026-05-16): `@gjsify/http2` is now feature-complete and promoted Partial → Full. The 5-phase plan (PRs #180–#184) replaced Soup's HTTP/2 multiplexer for the cases libsoup can't model — h2c cleartext server (no TLS), wire-level PUSH_PROMISE delivery, native h2c client (`http2.connect(..., { nativeDispatcher: 'force' })`), client-side `'push'` event reception from server-pushed streams, GOAWAY on `server.close()`, RST_STREAM on `stream.close(code)`, GC-pinning of `SessionBridge` instances. The native dispatcher (`Http2NativeDispatcher` + `Http2NativeClientDispatcher`) runs on `Gio.SocketService` + `Gio.SocketClient` with `Gio.PollableInputStream.create_source()` watches because `Gio.Socket.create_source()` is marked `introspectable=0` and `Gio.Socket.receive()` fails caller-allocates introspection. The Vala/C-shim layer in `@gjsify/http2-native` grew a full `nghttp2_session` driver (server + client modes) with a per-stream HEADERS accumulator keyed by `frame->push_promise.promised_stream_id` for PUSH_PROMISE frames (the parent-id keying bug was caught and fixed in Phase 2). Cross-cutting fix in `ClientHttp2Session.request()`: pseudo-headers are now reliably ordered before regular headers per RFC 7540 §8.1.2.1 (Soup tolerates mis-ordered headers since it builds the HPACK frame itself; nghttp2 rejects with PROTOCOL_ERROR + RST_STREAM — fatal against the native dispatcher). 231 GJS tests / 51 Node tests in the http2 package, all green. The Soup-backed paths (HTTP/1.1 server, ALPN-h2 server via `createSecureServer()`, h2-over-HTTPS client via Soup.Session) stay verbatim as the default — opt-in to native via `allowHTTP1: false` (server) or `nativeDispatcher: 'force'` (client).

> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-19, post-Phase-G.3):** Phase G.3 lands `gjsify build --watch` — rolldown's native `watch()` API wired through the CLI, with per-build duration logging, clean SIGINT shutdown, error pass-through, and `--library --watch` rejection. Closes the "Dev loop endet bei Save" gap from the Phase-G plan: developers can now save a TS file and see the bundle update without re-running `gjsify build`. Watch is npm-rolldown-only (the GJS-native engine has no watcher); calling it under the GJS-bundled CLI surfaces a precise pointer at `node lib/index.js build ... --watch`. 2-test e2e suite asserts rebuild-on-change + SIGINT cleanliness. Wire protocol is byte-order-explicit LE so a buffer written on x86_64 reads identically on s390x. Earlier (2026-05-18, Phase F.8): Phase F.8 added `gjsify uninstall -g <pkg>` as the symmetric inverse of `install -g`, with a 4-test e2e suite. Phase F.5 fully migrated ts-for-gir CI to the Node-free `install.mjs` bootstrap (Fedora 43 container, dnf prereqs, all four workflows; three iteration rounds caught environment quirks at the source). End-to-end users can now install AND uninstall GJS apps with the same one-line `curl … | gjs -m -` story, no Node or npm required. Earlier (2026-05-17, post-Phase-F-MVP): Phase F MVP landed `install.mjs` (root) + `gjsify self-update` + `gjsify generate-installer`, plus a release-workflow asset-upload step. End-users can now bootstrap `@gjsify/cli` (or any GJS-runnable npm package) with a single `curl -fsSL .../install.mjs | gjs -m -` line — no Node or npm required on the user's machine. Three cross-cutting Soup3-stream fixes shipped alongside (Accept-Encoding: identity in npm-registry, PARTIAL_INPUT-as-EOF in utils.readBytesAsync, gjs-wrapped launcher for `.gjs.mjs` bins in install-global). The new flow is gated behind the next gjsify release (which produces the `install.mjs` + `cli.gjs.mjs` + `.sha256` GitHub-release assets); CI migration of gjsify-self + ts-for-gir lands in the follow-up PR. Earlier (2026-05-17, post-Phase-E): Phase E landed `gjsify pack` + `gjsify publish` as first-class commands, replacing the last `npm pack` + `npm publish` shellouts in the gjsify release pipeline. End-to-end verified against the live npm registry in v0.4.37; every `@gjsify/*` tarball now ships with resolved `^0.4.37` dep ranges (no leaked `workspace:^`). Three sub-bugs surfaced + fixed during the v0.4.6 → v0.4.37 hotfix stretch: wire-filename uses unscoped basename per npm convention, URL-encoding uses npm-package-arg's `@${scope}%2f${name}` shape with lowercase `%2f`, auth lookup honors `NPM_CONFIG_USERCONFIG` (where `actions/setup-node@v6` writes the auth-token npmrc). ts-for-gir's cross-repo migration ([gjsify/ts-for-gir#393](https://github.com/gjsify/ts-for-gir/pull/393)) bumped to `@gjsify/cli@^0.4.37` and switched `publish:app`/`publish:types` to `gjsify publish`. Earlier in this stretch (2026-05-16): `@gjsify/http2` is now feature-complete and promoted Partial → Full. The 5-phase plan (PRs #180–#184) replaced Soup's HTTP/2 multiplexer for the cases libsoup can't model — h2c cleartext server (no TLS), wire-level PUSH_PROMISE delivery, native h2c client (`http2.connect(..., { nativeDispatcher: 'force' })`), client-side `'push'` event reception from server-pushed streams, GOAWAY on `server.close()`, RST_STREAM on `stream.close(code)`, GC-pinning of `SessionBridge` instances. The native dispatcher (`Http2NativeDispatcher` + `Http2NativeClientDispatcher`) runs on `Gio.SocketService` + `Gio.SocketClient` with `Gio.PollableInputStream.create_source()` watches because `Gio.Socket.create_source()` is marked `introspectable=0` and `Gio.Socket.receive()` fails caller-allocates introspection. The Vala/C-shim layer in `@gjsify/http2-native` grew a full `nghttp2_session` driver (server + client modes) with a per-stream HEADERS accumulator keyed by `frame->push_promise.promised_stream_id` for PUSH_PROMISE frames (the parent-id keying bug was caught and fixed in Phase 2). Cross-cutting fix in `ClientHttp2Session.request()`: pseudo-headers are now reliably ordered before regular headers per RFC 7540 §8.1.2.1 (Soup tolerates mis-ordered headers since it builds the HPACK frame itself; nghttp2 rejects with PROTOCOL_ERROR + RST_STREAM — fatal against the native dispatcher). 231 GJS tests / 51 Node tests in the http2 package, all green. The Soup-backed paths (HTTP/1.1 server, ALPN-h2 server via `createSecureServer()`, h2-over-HTTPS client via Soup.Session) stay verbatim as the default — opt-in to native via `allowHTTP1: false` (server) or `nativeDispatcher: 'force'` (client).

> **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-19, post-Phase-F.10.{2,3}):** Phases F.10.2 + F.10.3 land alongside F.10.1 (separate PR) to complete the Flathub publishing loop. `gjsify flatpak diff` reports version drift between the local git state and the per-app Flathub tracking-repo manifest (exit 1 + actionable hint on drift, exit 0 + `✅ in sync` on match). `gjsify flatpak release vX.Y.Z` orchestrates the full release flow end-to-end: regen assets (`flatpak init --force`) → lint (`flatpak check`) → tag + push → open the Flathub PR (`flatpak sync-flathub`). Ordering invariant: init + check must succeed before any tag is created. Both commands support `--dry-run` for safe planning. Earlier (post-Phase-F.9.7): Phase F.9.7 is the small-but-load-bearing fix surfaced by the easy6502 migration: `gjsify.flatpak.name` config field overrides the derived display name in MetaInfo `<name>` + .desktop `Name=`. Previously `flatpak init` ran the npm `package.json#name` through `friendlyName(pkgName, appId)` to pick a default — works when name is the reverse-DNS app id, breaks when it's an npm shape like `learn6502` vs display `"Learn 6502 Assembly"`. Fix is one optional field + a 1-line `flatpak.name ?? friendlyName(...)` precedence rule in init; full backwards compatibility. Companion: easy6502 (`JumpLink/Learn6502#96`) consumes this in its migration off hand-written manifest + MetaInfo + .desktop, regenerating all four Flathub assets from a single `package.json#gjsify.flatpak` config block (including translator hints for all 12 releases). Earlier in this stretch (post-Phase-F.9.6): Phase F.9.6 extended the F.9 scaffold to cover the full AppStream surface — `description` accepts a `DescriptionBlock[]` union (mixed `<p>` paragraphs and `<ul>/<li>` bullet lists), per-release `<description>` blocks (same shape), translator hints (`<!-- TRANSLATORS: ... -->` emitted before every translatable tag for `xgettext`/`msgfmt --xml --template` to forward into `.po` files), `<developer><email>` + `<name translate="no">`, content_rating attributes (`<content_attribute>` children), kudos, provides (binaries + mimetypes + dbus), supports/requires/recommends (controls + display_length + internet), iconRemote, translateUrl, plus screenshot captions with their own translator hints. Backwards compatible: the simple `description: "string"` form still works. The scaffold now produces MetaInfo XML rich enough to match hand-written Flathub manifests like easy6502 / Workbench, making multi-language Flathub apps generatable from a single `package.json#gjsify.flatpak` config block. Implementation: scaffold.ts rewritten from template-substitution to direct XML construction (~330 LOC builder; the old `metainfo-{app,cli}.xml.tmpl` files are removed). e2e suite extended 13 → 14 tests. Earlier in this stretch (post-Phase-F.9): Phase F.9 extended `gjsify flatpak init` to scaffold a full Flathub-ready asset bundle in one invocation — manifest JSON + MetaInfo XML (`desktop-application` or `console-application`, kind-aware) + `.desktop` entry (apps only) + `flathub.json` policy stub. New `--kind <app|cli>` flag (replaces `--cli-only`, which stays as a deprecated alias); new `gjsify flatpak check` command runs Flathub linters locally (appstreamcli + flatpak-builder-lint). Earlier (post-sab-native-Phase-1): `@gjsify/worker_threads` × `@gjsify/sab-native` Phase 1 landed. `Worker.postMessage(value, [sab])` and `workerData: { sab }` now route `SharedBuffer` instances across the subprocess-Worker boundary. Parent spawns each Worker with a `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socketpair (`FdChannel.makePair()`), passes the child end as inherited fd 3 via `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.take_fd`, and per-message walks the value tree for `SharedBuffer` instances, SCM_RIGHTS-sends each fd tagged with a sequence counter, then writes a JSON line with `{__sab: tag, size: N}` placeholders. The bootstrap counts placeholders per inbound JSON message and synchronously drains exactly that many fds from fd 3 — kernel buffering guarantees the fds are ready, since `sendmsg(SCM_RIGHTS)` always completes before `write(stdin)` in the parent — then materialises each placeholder via `SharedBuffer.fromFd(fd, size)`. Cross-process futex (`FUTEX_WAIT` / `FUTEX_WAKE` without the `_PRIVATE` flag) lets workers signal completion on shared barrier addresses. 4 new worker_threads unit tests + 1 new integration test (4 workers × SHA-256 over 1 MiB SharedBuffer; XOR-folded digest matches the in-process reference). Total: 275/275 GJS unit + 1034/1034 GJS integration + 270/270 Node unit + 1169/1169 Node integration, all green. Earlier (2026-05-18, sab-native Phase 0): the Vala/C bridge itself — `SharedBuffer` (`memfd_create(2)` + `mmap(MAP_SHARED)`, SEQ_CST `__atomic_*`, futex wait/notify), `FdChannel` (socketpair + SCM_RIGHTS), 18 GJS specs green including a real cross-process round-trip via `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.take_fd(fd, 3)`. Wire protocol is byte-order-explicit LE so a buffer written on x86_64 reads identically on s390x. Earlier (2026-05-18, Phase F.8): Phase F.8 added `gjsify uninstall -g <pkg>` as the symmetric inverse of `install -g`, with a 4-test e2e suite. Phase F.5 fully migrated ts-for-gir CI to the Node-free `install.mjs` bootstrap (Fedora 43 container, dnf prereqs, all four workflows; three iteration rounds caught environment quirks at the source). End-to-end users can now install AND uninstall GJS apps with the same one-line `curl … | gjs -m -` story, no Node or npm required. > **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-19, post-Phase-F.10.1):** Phase F.10.1 lands `gjsify flatpak sync-flathub` — automated end-to-end sync of the per-app `flathub/<app-id>` tracking-repo manifest. After cutting a release in the upstream repo, a single `gjsify flatpak sync-flathub` resolves the latest git tag + commit, clones the Flathub tracking-repo into `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/gjsify/flathub-sync/`, surgically edits `modules[0].sources[<i>]` to pin the new `tag` + `commit`, injects an `x-checker-data` block for the Flathub auto-update bot, branches + commits + pushes, and opens the PR via `gh`. Idempotent (already-at-version short-circuits), preserves source indent (2-space matches Flathub convention), default-branch-aware (`master` vs `main`). New `gjsify.flatpak.flathubRepo` config field overrides the default `flathub/<appId>` derivation. 4-test e2e suite stubs `git` + `gh`. > **Current state (v0.4.37, 2026-05-19, post-Phase-G.1):** Phase G.1 added three new CLI commands wrapping Biome's native binary — `gjsify format` (whitespace), `gjsify lint` (report + safe-fix), `gjsify fix` (biome's `check --write` combined pass). Resolution skips Biome's Node-launcher, spawns `node_modules/@biomejs/cli-<platform>-<arch>/biome` directly via `spawn(stdio: 'inherit')`. Same Node-free pattern as the existing `gjsify gettext/gresource/gsettings` wrappers. `--init` writes a recommended `biome.json` tuned for GJS projects (JS/TS 4-space, JSON+CSS 2-space, single quotes, line width 120, recommended linter + 4 GJS-specific opt-outs for `@girs/*` patterns). Workspace-aware: walks up from cwd to workspace root for both biome binary AND biome.json. New e2e suite `tests/e2e/biome/` (8 tests). Backwards compatible — closes the Node-tools dependency for downstream consumers (easy6502: drops prettier; ts-for-gir: drops direct biome devDep). Earlier (2026-05-18, sab-native Phase 0): the Vala/C bridge itself — `SharedBuffer` (`memfd_create(2)` + `mmap(MAP_SHARED)`, SEQ_CST `__atomic_*`, futex wait/notify), `FdChannel` (socketpair + SCM_RIGHTS), 18 GJS specs green including a real cross-process round-trip via `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.take_fd(fd, 3)`. Wire protocol is byte-order-explicit LE so a buffer written on x86_64 reads identically on s390x. Earlier (2026-05-18, Phase F.8): Phase F.8 added `gjsify uninstall -g <pkg>` as the symmetric inverse of `install -g`, with a 4-test e2e suite. Phase F.5 fully migrated ts-for-gir CI to the Node-free `install.mjs` bootstrap (Fedora 43 container, dnf prereqs, all four workflows; three iteration rounds caught environment quirks at the source). End-to-end users can now install AND uninstall GJS apps with the same one-line `curl … | gjs -m -` story, no Node or npm required. Earlier (2026-05-17, post-Phase-F-MVP): Phase F MVP landed `install.mjs` (root) + `gjsify self-update` + `gjsify generate-installer`, plus a release-workflow asset-upload step. End-users can now bootstrap `@gjsify/cli` (or any GJS-runnable npm package) with a single `curl -fsSL .../install.mjs | gjs -m -` line — no Node or npm required on the user's machine. Three cross-cutting Soup3-stream fixes shipped alongside (Accept-Encoding: identity in npm-registry, PARTIAL_INPUT-as-EOF in utils.readBytesAsync, gjs-wrapped launcher for `.gjs.mjs` bins in install-global). The new flow is gated behind the next gjsify release (which produces the `install.mjs` + `cli.gjs.mjs` + `.sha256` GitHub-release assets); CI migration of gjsify-self + ts-for-gir lands in the follow-up PR. Earlier (2026-05-17, post-Phase-E): Phase E landed `gjsify pack` + `gjsify publish` as first-class commands, replacing the last `npm pack` + `npm publish` shellouts in the gjsify release pipeline. End-to-end verified against the live npm registry in v0.4.37; every `@gjsify/*` tarball now ships with resolved `^0.4.37` dep ranges (no leaked `workspace:^`). Three sub-bugs surfaced + fixed during the v0.4.6 → v0.4.37 hotfix stretch: wire-filename uses unscoped basename per npm convention, URL-encoding uses npm-package-arg's `@${scope}%2f${name}` shape with lowercase `%2f`, auth lookup honors `NPM_CONFIG_USERCONFIG` (where `actions/setup-node@v6` writes the auth-token npmrc). ts-for-gir's cross-repo migration ([gjsify/ts-for-gir#393](https://github.com/gjsify/ts-for-gir/pull/393)) bumped to `@gjsify/cli@^0.4.37` and switched `publish:app`/`publish:types` to `gjsify publish`. Earlier in this stretch (2026-05-16): `@gjsify/http2` is now feature-complete and promoted Partial → Full. The 5-phase plan (PRs #180–#184) replaced Soup's HTTP/2 multiplexer for the cases libsoup can't model — h2c cleartext server (no TLS), wire-level PUSH_PROMISE delivery, native h2c client (`http2.connect(..., { nativeDispatcher: 'force' })`), client-side `'push'` event reception from server-pushed streams, GOAWAY on `server.close()`, RST_STREAM on `stream.close(code)`, GC-pinning of `SessionBridge` instances. The native dispatcher (`Http2NativeDispatcher` + `Http2NativeClientDispatcher`) runs on `Gio.SocketService` + `Gio.SocketClient` with `Gio.PollableInputStream.create_source()` watches because `Gio.Socket.create_source()` is marked `introspectable=0` and `Gio.Socket.receive()` fails caller-allocates introspection. The Vala/C-shim layer in `@gjsify/http2-native` grew a full `nghttp2_session` driver (server + client modes) with a per-stream HEADERS accumulator keyed by `frame->push_promise.promised_stream_id` for PUSH_PROMISE frames (the parent-id keying bug was caught and fixed in Phase 2). Cross-cutting fix in `ClientHttp2Session.request()`: pseudo-headers are now reliably ordered before regular headers per RFC 7540 §8.1.2.1 (Soup tolerates mis-ordered headers since it builds the HPACK frame itself; nghttp2 rejects with PROTOCOL_ERROR + RST_STREAM — fatal against the native dispatcher). 231 GJS tests / 51 Node tests in the http2 package, all green. The Soup-backed paths (HTTP/1.1 server, ALPN-h2 server via `createSecureServer()`, h2-over-HTTPS client via Soup.Session) stay verbatim as the default — opt-in to native via `allowHTTP1: false` (server) or `nativeDispatcher: 'force'` (client).
>
> **Previous (v0.4.0-pre, 2026-05-12, post-Phase A):** Phase A landed `output.keepNames` as the top-level minify-keepNames path so the GJS-CLI bundle now shrinks within ~12% of the Node-CLI's output for the yargs integration fixture (down from 19% delta). The previous nested `minify: { mangle: { keepNames: {...} } }` shape worked under npm rolldown's TS API but tripped `rolldown_common::deserialize_minify` on the native facade's JSON path. New 5th self-host e2e test enforces size-equivalence within 15% + semantic equivalence (both bundles produce 52 yargs tests passing). Earlier in this stretch: **Phase D-3 self-host loop closed.** `gjsify build packages/infra/cli/src/index.ts --app gjs` produces a 6.3 MB GJS bundle that runs under stock `gjs 1.88` and bundles real npm projects end-to-end — the yargs integration suite (52 tests) builds + runs to green under `gjs -m` only, no Node anywhere in the loop. Five concrete blockers fixed across the bundler-pick + auto-globals + plugin-gjsify packages: `parseAsync()` for top-level yargs, lazy-load of npm rolldown (Rust crate can't load under GJS), engine-aware `AnalysisBundler` injection for the `--globals auto` iterative pass, `createRequire(import.meta.url).resolve(...) → pathToFileURL(...)` for native facade loading under GJS, six JS-API ↔ Rust-deserializer shape normalizations (`input` → InputItem[], `output: {}` flattened, `transform.{define,inject}` lifted, inject map → tagged-enum array, `sourcemap: false` dropped, `minify: { mangle: ... }` → plain `true`). Plus: input-side shebang strip plugin so the CLI's own `#!/usr/bin/env node` doesn't land mid-chunk; `unicorn-magic` ESM shim mirroring the upstream Node entry; `resolveConsoleShim()` walks `node_modules` from `_shimDir`. Permanent regression coverage in `tests/e2e/self-host/` (4 tests, ~17 s) wired into the `test:e2e` aggregator. Earlier in this v0.4.0-pre stretch: Workstream B (`@gjsify/tls`) promoted from Partial → near-Full: cert-chain extraction (Node-shaped `getPeerCertificate({detailed:true})` walking `Gio.TlsCertificate.get_issuer()`), full RFC 6125 §6.4.3 hostname matching (wildcard prefix/suffix, xn-- A-label exact match, *.tld rejection, error code `ERR_TLS_CERT_ALTNAME_INVALID`), mTLS via `Gio.TlsConnection.set_certificate()` on both client + server, `TlsAuthenticationMode.REQUESTED/REQUIRED` for `requestCert`+`rejectUnauthorized`, custom-CA validation via `cert.verify()` against parsed `caCertificates`, custom `checkServerIdentity` override, ALPN advertise/negotiate via `set_advertised_protocols`/`get_negotiated_protocol`, `addContext`+`SNICallback` plumbing (best-effort — Gio doesn't surface ClientHello server_name pre-handshake; tracked in Open TODOs), `createSecureContext` accepts string/Buffer/Uint8Array/array PEM with cert+key concatenation and CA-bundle splitting via `splitPemBlocks`, ctx.context Node-compat self-reference. New specs `cert.spec.ts` + `tls.gjs.spec.ts` (RFC 6125 depth/prefix/IDN, error.code, GJS-only Gio.TlsCertificate plumbing). Tests: 132 → 169 (+37). 65 `as any` removed from `tls/src/index.spec.ts` (all replaced by `unknown` + type guards / `fakeCert(...)` helper). Workstream C (`@gjsify/worker_threads`) added `transferList` support to `MessagePort.postMessage()` for both `ArrayBuffer` (zero-copy via SM140 `ArrayBuffer.prototype.transfer()` — sender detaches) and `MessagePort` (in-process channel hand-off — source port detaches, receiver gets a usable port wired to the surviving channel partner). `SharedArrayBuffer` pass-through verified on Node (same backing store; cross-thread `Atomics.{store,load,notify}` visibility) and gracefully skipped on GJS — Mozilla disables the SAB constructor in stock GJS, and the cross-process subprocess-Worker path needs a `@gjsify/sab-native` Vala mmap bridge that's deferred to STATUS.md "Open TODOs → SharedArrayBuffer cross-process sharing". Implementation: `packages/gjs/utils/src/structured-clone.ts` extended with a transfer context + view-snapshot pre-walk (TypedArray/DataView byteOffset/length captured BEFORE buffer transfer detaches the source views); `packages/node/worker_threads/src/message-port.ts` rewritten to validate the transfer list (rejects detached, duplicate, non-transferable, SAB-in-list, self-transfer, closed-port-transfer with `DataCloneError`/code 25 per HTML spec), substitute MessagePort placeholders before clone, and re-attach receiver-side ports after. New integration suite `tests/integration/worker-stress/` with two specs: `transferlist-stress` (bulk 16 MiB transfer + 4-channel fan-out + 5-hop port chain — Node 727 MiB/s, GJS 235 MiB/s baseline) and `sab-parallel-hash` (4 worker threads computing SHA-256 over a 1 MiB SAB, Atomics barrier, Node-only). 32 new unit tests + 5 integration tests in worker_threads itself; full counts now 270 Node / 264 GJS unit + 1169 Node / 1033 GJS integration. `as any` removed from `worker_threads/src/index.spec.ts` (30 → 0 — added `BroadcastChannelW3C` / `MessagePortW3C` helper aliases for the W3C surface that `@types/node` types more tightly than the runtime). Earlier in this v0.4.0-pre stretch: Workstream D (`@gjsify/webgl` source refactor) split the 4164-line `webgl-context-base.ts` into focused composition modules under `packages/framework/webgl/src/ts/context/` (`state.ts`, `buffer-binding.ts`, `texture-management.ts`, `framebuffer.ts`, `shader-program.ts`, `drawing.ts`). Each module declares a typed `*Methods` interface (declaration-merged into `WebGLContextBase`) plus an `install*Methods(proto)` registration function called from the base file after the class declaration — sidesteps the circular-import trap that prototype-merge mixins would otherwise hit at module-load time. The base file is now ≈590 lines, owning fields, abstract `_gl`, the constructor, `_init`, `getParameter`, `getExtension`, `getSupportedExtensions`, and the foundational `_check{Owns,Valid,Wrapper}` helpers shared by every split module. Public API unchanged — `WebGLRenderingContext` and `WebGL2RenderingContext` (and every external consumer) work without modification. `as any` reduced from 74 to 44 across `packages/framework/webgl/src/`; webgl-context-base.ts went 2 → 0, webgl2-rendering-context.ts went 29 → 1 (a doc comment), with the remaining 44 split between intentional negative-test casts in conformance/spec files and globalThis bootstrap in `webgl-bridge.ts`. Six bare `// TODO`/`// FIXME` comments in the original file replaced by structured `STATUS.md "Open TODOs": …` comments alongside the now-tracked entries (drawingBufferColorSpace colorimetry, multi-FBO texture detach, MAX_RENDERBUFFER_SIZE caching, optional drawing-buffer pre-allocation). All 860 GJS WebGL tests pass, the Three.js post-processing showcase rebuilds clean. Earlier: two post-v0.3.17 `gjsify showcase` hotfixes. (a) Pre-flight `checkGwebgl` removed — it tried to `require.resolve('@gjsify/webgl')` from `process.cwd()` or the CLI's own `node_modules`, both empty under `npx @gjsify/cli showcase three-postprocessing-pixel`, so it always failed for webgl-using showcases. Since `showcase` delegates to `gjsify dlx`, which fetches the showcase + transitive deps (incl. `@gjsify/webgl` with the `gwebgl` Vala prebuild) into the npm cache where the PR #94 native-package walker picks it up, the pre-flight was checking something `dlx` was about to install — leftover from when the CLI tarball bundled showcase packages. (b) `gjsify showcase <name>` now pins the spawned dlx spec to the CLI's own version (`<pkg>@<cli-version>`). Without the pin, `gjsify dlx` cached whatever version it first resolved and served it for 7 days (default TTL), so users running `npx @gjsify/cli@0.3.17 showcase express-webserver` got the cached `@gjsify/example-node-express-webserver@0.3.16` install — pre-PR #94 shape, missing the `@gjsify/http-soup-bridge` runtime dep — and the bundle errored with `Typelib file for namespace 'GjsifyHttpSoupBridge' not found`. Pinning makes the cache key change every release, so each CLI version always runs its matching showcase tarball. Two new regression tests in `tests/e2e/cli-only/run.mjs` lock the showcase-command shape, plus a new `tests/e2e/dlx-version-pin/` suite (4 tests) that drives `gjsify dlx` end-to-end against an in-process mock npm registry — verifies that `<pkg>@v0.0.1` and `<pkg>@v0.0.2` land in different cache directories, transitive native-typelib deps install correctly, and `computeNativeEnvForBundle` walks them. Together these would have caught the regression that necessitated the v0.3.18/0.4.37 hotfix releases without needing to publish to npm to test. Below: three coupled changes landed in one PR. (1) `gjsify dlx` / `gjsify run` native-prebuild detection now walks the bundle's own `node_modules` tree on top of CWD — fixes `gjsify showcase express-webserver` failing with `Typelib file for namespace 'GjsifyHttpSoupBridge' not found` because transitive Vala typelibs (`@gjsify/http-soup-bridge`) were never reached. The two overlapping detectors (`detectNativePackages` + transitive-blind `resolveNativePackages`) collapse into one algorithm with two startpoints; env composition is split into the testable pure helper `computeNativeEnvForBundle(bundlePath, cwd)`. (2) Showcase shape: `@gjsify/example-node-express-webserver` now declares `@gjsify/http-soup-bridge` as a runtime `dependency` (was missing — only present at build time, so `npm install` from the published tarball never shipped the typelib). (3) `gjsify build` minifies by default; opt-out via `--no-minify` or `bundler.output.minify: false`. The hard-coded `minify: false` overrides in the app/library orchestrators are removed so user-config now wins. New e2e suite `dlx-native-prebuilds/` (2 tests). Earlier in this v0.4.0-pre stretch: `cssAsStringPlugin` lightningcss bundling/lowering + `css-bundling` e2e suite.
>
> **Previous (v0.4.0-pre, 2026-05-07):** PR2 of the Flatpak-toolchain line landed `gjsify flatpak {init,build,deps,ci}` subcommands — manifest scaffold from `gjsify.flatpak` config, flatpak-builder + flatpak-node-generator wrappers, GitHub-Actions workflow scaffold. New e2e suite `flatpak/` with 7 tests via stubbed binaries. PR1 had landed the bundler-side primitives (`gjsify gsettings` subcommand, `defineFromPackageJson` / `defineFromEnv` config keys, `loaders` text-shorthand, package-name plugin entries in `bundler.plugins`, env-template shebang strings) gated behind 5 new e2e suites. Consumers — `easy6502/packages/app-gnome` (Vite→`gjsify build` migration) and `@ts-for-gir/cli` (drop `scripts/build-gjs.mjs` wrapper) — follow next. Earlier: the gjsify build pipeline produces a single self-contained GJS bundle from arbitrary npm-published packages. **The bundler engine has been migrated from esbuild to Rolldown** (Vite 8's production bundler) — same CLI surface (`gjsify build --app gjs|node|browser --globals auto …`), Rollup-shaped plugins under the hood that also run under Vite for the sister GJS apps. `@gjsify/rolldown-plugin-gjsify` rewrites `import.meta.url` / `__dirname` / `__filename` for both regular `node_modules` and Yarn-PnP zip-resident files, and inlines statically-resolvable `readFileSync(new URL(<lit>, import.meta.url), "utf8")` / `readdirSync(URL)` / `existsSync(URL)` / `JSON.parse(readFileSync(...))` compositions at build time. `--globals auto` retains its iterative multi-pass "after tree-shaking" detection (bundler-agnostic; engine swap preserves the load-bearing invariant). `@gjsify/module`'s `createRequire` is PnP-aware — `require.resolve("<pkg>/package.json")` works in PnP-built bundles. `@gjsify/cli` ships a native install backend (`@gjsify/semver` + `@gjsify/npm-registry` + `@gjsify/tar`) so `gjsify dlx` and `gjsify install` no longer require Node or npm at runtime. First real-world consumer: ts-for-gir's GJS bundle ([gjsify/ts-for-gir#378](https://github.com/gjsify/ts-for-gir/pull/378)) runs unmodified npm packages (TypeScript compiler, TypeDoc, shiki, yargs, ejs, ~100 transitive zip-resident deps) on `gjs -m` with zero ts-for-gir-side workarounds. End-to-end coverage: 9 e2e suites green (`create-app`, `cli-only`, `cli-only-pnp`, `standalone-plugin`, `native-install`, `cli-config`, `inline-static-reads`, `library-multi-build`, `terminal-native`). Pillar tests: Node 291/291, GJS 227/227 (3 ignored). The full v0.3.x history lives in [Release timeline](#release-timeline) below; per-release detail in [`CHANGELOG.md`](./CHANGELOG.md).

## Release timeline

- **v0.4.37** — `@gjsify/module` PnP-aware `createRequire`. Parses `.pnp.cjs`'s `RAW_RUNTIME_STATE` directly (no Yarn-manifest execution), so `require.resolve("<pkg>/package.json")` works in PnP-built bundles for the first time. Removed the last reason ts-for-gir had to embed its EJS templates at build time.
- **v0.3.12** — `cli,esbuild-plugin-gjsify` library multi-build fix: `format`/`jsExtension` were being passed the wrong sibling outdir/extension, so the ESM and CJS halves of a `--library` build collided in the CJS directory. New e2e suite: `library-multi-build/`.
- **v0.3.11** — Three rewriter fixes that let PnP-built bundles run portably without `nodeLinker: node-modules`: (1) banner-shebang hoist keeps `#!/usr/bin/env -S gjs -m` at byte 0 even when esbuild's own banner runs first; (2) `createRequire(<URL>)` of zip-resident anchors becomes a thrown-on-use stub (esbuild has already inlined every dep — the createRequire result is dead code in our bundles); (3) `import.meta.url` URL rewrite is skipped for source files inside Yarn-PnP virtual zips so the bare token resolves to the bundle's own URL at runtime.
- **v0.3.10** — Static-read inliner: build-time evaluation of `readFileSync` / `readdirSync` / `existsSync` / `JSON.parse(readFileSync(...))` against `import.meta.url`-relative URLs, replaces calls with literal contents → bundle is portable to any directory. New e2e suite: `inline-static-reads/` (1/1).
- **v0.3.9** — Config precedence + outfile safety + PnP-zip rewriter warning. `package.json#gjsify` now merges with `.gjsifyrc.*`/`gjsify.config.*` (was first-match-wins). `gjsify build` refuses to default `--outfile` to a `.ts` source path. New e2e suite: `cli-config/` (2/2).
- **v0.3.8** — Native install backend default. `@gjsify/semver` (77/77), `@gjsify/npm-registry` (33/33), `@gjsify/tar` (27/25 Node/GJS) compose into `install-backend-native.ts` — BFS resolver, parallel tarball download with sha512 SRI, flat `node_modules/` + `.bin/` symlinks. New e2e suite: `native-install/`.
- **v0.3.7** — PnP-rewriter onLoad ordering fix: the `__filename`/`__dirname` rewriter is composed INTO the pnp plugin's onLoad (esbuild stops at the first match). Extracted `getPnpPlugin` from `@gjsify/cli` into reusable `@gjsify/resolve-npm/pnp-relay` with a `transformContentsFactory` callback. New e2e suite: `cli-only-pnp/` (5/5).
- **v0.3.6** — pnpapi namespace unwrap, esbuild peer dep, lazy-deepkit. `cli-only-pnp` suite (4/4).
- **v0.3.5** — `gjsify dlx` GJS-bundle runner; PnP zip read; two-hop relay shape; shebang config; `@gjsify/webassembly` Promise-API polyfill (15 tests).
- **v0.3.0–0.3.4** — Yarn PnP support, `excludeGlobals`, fetch bridge-free, UNDECLARED_DEPENDENCY fall-through, portable `import.meta.url`.

## Summary

gjsify implements Node.js, Web Standard, and DOM APIs for GJS (GNOME JavaScript / SpiderMonkey 140).
The project comprises **41 Node.js modules** (+1 meta, +5 native bridges), **18 Web API packages** (+1 meta, +1 native bridge, +3 Adwaita assets), **2 DOM packages**, **6 framework bridge packages**, **3 GJS infrastructure packages**, and **16 build/infra tools**.

| Category | Total | Full | Partial | Stub |
|----------|-------|------|---------|------|
| Node.js APIs | 41 | 33 (80%) | 5 (12%) | 3 (7%) |
| Node.js native bridges | 5 | 5 (terminal, sab, tls, http-soup, http2) | — | — |
| Node.js meta | 1 | 1 | — | — |
| Web APIs | 18 | 16 (89%) | 2 (11%) | — |
| Web native bridge | 1 | 1 (webrtc-native) | — | — |
| Web meta | 1 | 1 | — | — |
| Browser UI / Adwaita assets | 3 | 3 (adwaita-web, adwaita-fonts, adwaita-icons) | — | — |
| DOM | 2 | 2 (dom-elements, canvas2d-core) | — | — |
| Framework bridges | 6 | 6 (bridge-types, canvas2d, event-bridge, iframe, video, webgl) | — | — |
| GJS Infrastructure | 3 | 3 (runtime, unit, utils) | — | — |
| Build/Infra Tools | 16 | 16 | — | — |
| Showcases | 8 | 8 | — | — |
| Integration test suites | 22 | 22 | — | — |

**Web platform coverage** (vs. the relevant W3C/WHATWG standards, not just our own package list): ≈54 % of all surveyed standards implemented full or partial, with ~20 % out of scope by design for desktop GTK apps (Service Worker, FS Access, Web Bluetooth, …). See `website/src/data/web-standards.ts` for the canonical category list.

**Test coverage:** 10,650+ test cases in 113+ spec files (each test runs on both Node.js and GJS). (86 new `@gjsify/child_process` parity tests in v0.4.37's audit.) CI via GitHub Actions (Node.js 24.x + GJS on Fedora 43/44 — minimum supported runtime: GJS 1.86 / SpiderMonkey 140). Integration suites (`gjsify foreach test:integration`) are opt-in and exercise curated upstream tests from acorn, autobahn, axios, cosmiconfig, deepkit-type-compiler, dotenv, execa, fast-glob, gettext-parser, lightningcss, mcp-inspector-cli, mcp-typescript-sdk, minify-xml, pkg-types, rolldown-native, rollup-pluginutils, socket.io, streamx, ts-for-gir, undici, webtorrent, worker-stress, and yargs.

## Node.js Packages (`packages/node/`)

### Fully Implemented (35)

| Package | GNOME Libs | Tests | Description |
|---------|-----------|-------|-------------|
| **assert** | — | 117 | AssertionError, deepEqual, throws, strict mode |
| **async_hooks** | — | 130 | AsyncLocalStorage (run, enterWith, snapshot, exit), AsyncResource (bind, runInAsyncScope, triggerAsyncId), createHook |
| **buffer** | — | 317 | Buffer via Blob/atob/btoa, alloc, from, concat, encodings, fill, indexOf/lastIndexOf, slice/subarray, copy, int/float read/write, swap16/32/64, equals, compare |
| **child_process** | Gio, GLib | 210 (2 specs) | exec/execSync, execFile/execFileSync, spawn/spawnSync via Gio.Subprocess; cwd/env via Gio.SubprocessLauncher; `spawn()` now provides `child.stdout`/`child.stderr` as Readable streams (GioInputStreamReadable); `ensureMainLoop()` in spawn/exec/execFile so GLib async callbacks dispatch in any GJS context (fixes GJS-from-GJS deadlock). **Node-parity audit (v0.4.37)**: env value coercion (`null`/number/boolean/array → `${value}` string, `undefined` dropped, prototype-chain iteration, empty-env wipes parent env), cwd accepts `URL`/`Buffer`, `argv0`/`detached`/`uid`/`gid`/`windowsHide`/`windowsVerbatimArguments` options accepted (argv0 via `/bin/sh -c 'exec -a'` shim, detached via `setsid`), exec/execFile/execSync return `Buffer` for `encoding: 'buffer'`/`null`, exec/spawn/spawnSync honour `timeout` + `killSignal` (spawnSync via `/usr/bin/timeout` wrapper since `communicate()` blocks the GLib mainloop), exec honours `maxBuffer` (throws `ERR_CHILD_PROCESS_STDIO_MAXBUFFER`) + `AbortSignal`, spawn/spawnSync validate option/argv types synchronously, ENOENT-for-missing-binary surfaces as `err.code='ENOENT'`/`err.errno=-2`/`err.syscall='spawn <file>'`/`err.path`/`err.spawnargs` (via `GLib.SpawnError.matches()` detection + Error-wrap so `err instanceof Error` holds), `kill()` maps named signals (`SIGINT`/`SIGHUP`/etc.) to POSIX numbers |
| **console** | — | 124 | Console class with stream support, format specifiers, table, dir, time/timeLog, count, group, assert, trace, stdout/stderr routing |
| **constants** | — | 27 | Flattened re-export of `os.constants` (errno, signals, priority, dlopen) + `fs.constants` + legacy crypto constants — the deprecated Node `constants` alias |
| **crypto** | GLib | 571 (13 specs) | Hash (SHA256/384/512, MD5, SHA1, known vectors), Hmac (extended edge cases), randomBytes/UUID/Int (v4 format, uniqueness), PBKDF2, HKDF, scrypt, AES (CBC/CTR/ECB/GCM), DH, ECDH, Sign/Verify, publicEncrypt/privateDecrypt, **KeyObject (JWK import/export)**, **X509Certificate**, timingSafeEqual, getHashes/getCiphers/getCurves, constants |
| **dgram** | Gio, GLib | 143 | UDP Socket via Gio.Socket with bind, send, receive, multicast, connect/disconnect/remoteAddress, broadcast, TTL, ref/unref, IPv6, EventEmitter |
| **diagnostics_channel** | — | 137 | Channel, TracingChannel, subscribe/unsubscribe |

### Native Bridges

| Package | GNOME Libs | Description |
|---------|-----------|-------------|
| **@gjsify/terminal-native** (Vala) | Linux/Posix (Vala VAPIs) | Optional Vala prebuild providing real Linux terminal syscalls to `@gjsify/tty` and `@gjsify/process`. `Terminal` class: `is_tty(fd)` → `Posix.isatty()`, `get_size(fd, out rows, out cols, out xpixel, out ypixel)` → `ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ)`, `set_raw_mode(fd, enable)` → `tcgetattr/tcsetattr` (ICANON+ECHO). `ResizeWatcher` class: `resized(rows, cols)` GObject signal wired to SIGWINCH via `GLib.Unix.signal_add()`. TypeScript wrapper: synchronous `imports.gi.GjsifyTerminal` with try/catch — safe when typelib not installed, no crash. Consumers see native behaviour when installed, GLib/env fallback otherwise. Ships as `.so`+`.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-x86_64/`. CI builds pending (add to `.github/workflows/prebuilds.yml`). |
| **@gjsify/http-soup-bridge** (Vala) | Soup 3.0 | Vala/GObject library consumed by `@gjsify/http`. Wraps `Soup.Server` + `SoupServerMessage` and exposes JS only through plain GObject classes whose lifetime SpiderMonkey GC cannot race against. Solves two libsoup GC crashes: (1) `BoxedBase::finalize → g_source_unref` SIGSEGV from deferred-GC on in-flight Soup messages, (2) `g_main_context_unref` assertion from shared `GMainContext` ref imbalance. Contains `Server` (wraps `Soup.Server`, emits `request-received` / `upgrade` / `error-occurred` signals), `Request` (read-side snapshot — method, url, headers, `get_body()`), `Response` (write side, owns `SoupServerMessage` C-side including all pause/unpause bookkeeping), and a peer-close watcher (`g_socket_create_source(IN|HUP|ERR)` + non-blocking `MSG_PEEK` probe — capability unreachable from JS because `Gio.Socket.receive_message` is not introspectable). Ships as prebuilt `.so` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-{x86_64,aarch64,ppc64,s390x,riscv64}/`; CI (`.github/workflows/prebuilds.yml`) rebuilds on Vala source changes (native runners for x86_64/aarch64; QEMU via `uraimo/run-on-arch-action` for ppc64/s390x/riscv64). |
| **@gjsify/http2-native** (Vala + C shim) | libnghttp2 ≥ 1.40 | Vala/GObject library consumed by `@gjsify/http2`. Provides nghttp2 primitives unreachable through libsoup's high-level GIR API. Contains `FrameEncoder` (HPACK header-block encoder + raw frame builders for DATA / HEADERS / PUSH_PROMISE / SETTINGS / WINDOW_UPDATE / PING / RST_STREAM / GOAWAY — backed by a tiny C shim around `nghttp2_hd_deflate_*` since nghttp2 has no upstream Vala VAPI), `StreamIdAllocator` (server-side even-id sequencer per RFC 7540 §5.1.1, plus client-id tracking for GOAWAY), and **`SessionBridge`** (full `nghttp2_session` driver — server + client modes via `nghttp2_session_{server,client}_new`, `mem_recv`/`mem_send`, complete submit family (`submit_settings/response/request/data/push_promise/goaway/rst_stream`); per-stream HEADERS accumulator + GLib.Bytes-owned DATA carrier; events drain through `gjsify_http2_event_*` getters and re-emit on the main loop via `GLib.Idle.add()` — same hop pattern as `@gjsify/webrtc-native`). Header signals carry `GLib.Variant("a(ss)")` instead of `string[]` so GJS's marshaller doesn't trip the `G_VALUE_HOLDS_POINTER` assertion. All buffer ownership stays C-side via `GLib.Bytes` so SpiderMonkey GC cannot race nghttp2 allocations — same pattern as `@gjsify/webrtc-native` / `@gjsify/http-soup-bridge`. TS wrapper loads the typelib lazily via try/catch — packages fall back to JS-only counters when the prebuild is unavailable. Ships as `.so` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-{x86_64,aarch64,ppc64,s390x,riscv64}/`; CI (`.github/workflows/prebuilds.yml`) rebuilds on Vala source changes. |
| **@gjsify/sab-native** (Vala + C shim) | Linux libc (memfd_create, mmap, futex, socketpair, SCM_RIGHTS) | Vala/GObject library providing cross-process shared memory + atomics for `@gjsify/worker_threads`. Closes the SharedArrayBuffer gap in stock GJS (Mozilla disables the SAB constructor without COOP/COEP opts; even with SAB on, subprocess workers cannot share backing stores by design — SM allocates SABs from the per-runtime malloc heap). Two GObject classes: **`SharedBuffer`** (wraps a `memfd_create(2)` + `mmap(MAP_SHARED)` region, exposes typed accessors `get_u8/u32_le/i32_le/u64_le` + write counterparts, bulk `read_bytes/write_bytes` via `GLib.Bytes` with refcount-pinned mmap lifetime, SEQ_CST atomics `atomic_{add,sub,load,store,xchg,cmpxchg}_i32` via GCC `__atomic_*` builtins, and `futex_wait/futex_wake` via raw `syscall(SYS_futex, FUTEX_{WAIT,WAKE}_PRIVATE)` for cross-process wait/notify) + **`FdChannel`** (Unix-domain `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socketpair + `sendmsg/recvmsg` with `SCM_RIGHTS` for fd-transfer; SOCK_CLOEXEC on both ends; 4-byte big-endian tag payload mapping each fd to the JSON-side placeholder index). Two construction paths: `SharedBuffer.create(size)` allocates fresh anonymous memory; `SharedBuffer.from_fd(fd, size)` mmaps a fd received over `SCM_RIGHTS`. Fd dup'd on `from_fd` so sender can close their copy. Wire protocol is byte-order-explicit LE across all multi-byte accessors so a buffer written on x86_64 reads identically on s390x. TS façade (`SharedBuffer`, `atomics` namespace, internal `fdChannel`) loads typelib via synchronous `imports.gi.GjsifySabNative` with try/catch — `hasNativeSab()` predicate. 18 GJS specs green including a real `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.take_fd(fd, 3)` cross-process round-trip + SCM_RIGHTS fd transfer round-trip. Ships as `.so` + `.gir` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-{x86_64,aarch64,ppc64,s390x,riscv64}/`. |
| **@gjsify/tls-native** (Vala) | GnuTLS 3.x (`gnutls_ocsp_*`) | Optional Vala prebuild providing GnuTLS capabilities not exposed by `Gio.TlsConnection`. **Phase 1 (this release): OCSP-response parsing.** Vala's bundled `gnutls.vapi` (0.56) covers the core handshake / X.509 / pubkey surface but has no `gnutls_ocsp_*` declarations — `@gjsify/tls-native` ships a sibling `gnutls-ocsp.vapi` declaring the minimal OCSP surface and a `Tls.parse_ocsp_response(uint8[])` static method that returns an `OcspResponseInfo` GObject (response status, producedAt, certStatus, thisUpdate, nextUpdate, revocationTime, revocationReason — all RFC 6960 §4.2.1 fields). The split `.vapi` (declarations) + `.vala` (impl) avoids the duplicate `typedef gnutls_xxx_t` collision that hits when the `cname = "struct gnutls_xxx_int"` pattern appears in a `.vala` source. TS wrapper: `hasNativeTls()` predicate, `parseOcspResponse(bytes): OcspResponseInfo \| null`, `OcspCertStatus` / `OcspResponseStatus` symbolic enum-style constants. 8 GJS specs green (typelib loads, parser rejects empty/garbage/truncated input, symbolic constants match RFC values). Builds against `gnutls-devel` (Fedora) / `libgnutls28-dev` (Debian). Phase 2 candidates (session resumption + channel binding) still blocked on extracting the live `gnutls_session_t` from a running `Gio.TlsConnection`. Ships as `.so` + `.gir` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-x86_64/` (multi-arch via CI to follow). |

multiple buffers per call; rejects detached / duplicate / non-transferable / SAB entries with `DataCloneError`/code 25), **`postMessage(value, transferList)` for `MessagePort`** (in-process channel hand-off — source port detaches, receiver gets a usable port wired to the surviving channel partner; supports nested transfer chains, rejects self-transfer / closed-port transfer), **`SharedArrayBuffer` pass-through in same-process MessageChannel** (Node only — same backing store; sender writes visible to receiver via `Atomics.{load,add}`), **cross-process `SharedBuffer` in `Worker.postMessage` and `workerData`** (via [`@gjsify/sab-native`](#native-bridges): parent's `FdChannel.makePair()` at spawn passes the child end as inherited fd 3 via `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.take_fd`; the bootstrap counts placeholders per inbound message and synchronously drains exactly that many fds from fd 3 — kernel buffering guarantees the fds are ready since the parent always writes them before the stdin line — then rebuilds each placeholder via `SharedBuffer.fromFd(fd, size)`) | `SharedArrayBuffer` constructor unavailable in stock GJS (Mozilla disables it; ## Web API Packages (`packages/web/`)

All 20 packages have real implementations (plus 1 meta). New in this cycle: `@gjsify/xmlhttprequest` (split out of fetch), `@gjsify/domparser` (excalibur-tiled), `@gjsify/webrtc`, `@gjsify/webrtc-native`, `@gjsify/adwaita-fonts`, `@gjsify/adwaita-icons`, `@gjsify/web-polyfills`, `@gjsify/webassembly` (Promise-API polyfill).

|---------|-----------|-------|----------|
| **abort-controller** | — | 23 (2 specs) | AbortController, AbortSignal (.abort, .timeout, .any) |
