| **xmlhttprequest** | Soup 3.0, GLib | covered via fetch `on('Gjs', …)` | XMLHttpRequest (full `responseType`: arraybuffer / blob + temp-file / json / text / document). FakeBlob with `_tmpPath`. `URL.createObjectURL`/`revokeObjectURL` are first-class methods on `@gjsify/url`'s URL class — this package just owns the blob-file plumbing and the XHR class |
| **formdata** | — | 49 | FormData, File, multipart encoding |
| **streams** | — | 345 | ReadableStream (incl. **`type:'bytes'`**), WritableStream, TransformStream, **ReadableByteStreamController** + **`autoAllocateChunkSize`**, **ReadableStreamBYOBReader** (`read(view, {min?})` preserves view-constructor), **ReadableStreamBYOBRequest** (`view`/`respond`/`respondWithNewView`), polymorphic close/error/cancel for default + BYOB readers, byte-stream `tee()`, TextEncoderStream, TextDecoderStream, ByteLengthQueuingStrategy, CountQueuingStrategy (WHATWG Streams polyfill for GJS) |
| **webcrypto** | — | 486 | SubtleCrypto (digest, AES-CBC/CTR/GCM, HMAC, ECDSA, RSA-PSS, RSA-OAEP, PBKDF2, HKDF, ECDH, generateKey, importKey/exportKey, deriveBits/deriveKey), CryptoKey |
| **web-globals** | — | 66 | Unified re-export surface for all Web API packages. Root export is pure named re-exports; side effects (registering URL, URLSearchParams, Blob, File, FormData, performance, PerformanceObserver + chaining every sub-package's `/register`) live in `@gjsify/web-globals/register`. Users opt in via the `--globals` CLI flag or an explicit `import '@gjsify/web-globals/register'`. |
| **websocket** | Soup 3.0, Gio, GLib | 27 | WebSocket, MessageEvent, CloseEvent (W3C spec) |
| **webaudio** | Gst 1.0, GstApp 1.0 | 32 | AudioContext (decodeAudioData via GStreamer decodebin, createBufferSource, createGain, currentTime via GLib monotonic clock), AudioBuffer (PCM Float32Array storage), AudioBufferSourceNode (GStreamer appsrc→audioconvert→volume→autoaudiosink), GainNode (AudioParam with setTargetAtTime), AudioParam, HTMLAudioElement (canPlayType, playbin playback). **Phase 1 — covers Excalibur.js** |
| **gamepad** | Manette 0.2 | 19 | Gamepad (navigator.getGamepads polling via libmanette event-driven signals), GamepadButton (pressed/touched/value), GamepadEvent (gamepadconnected/gamepaddisconnected on globalThis), GamepadHapticActuator (dual-rumble with strong/weak magnitude). Button mapping: Manette→W3C standard layout (17 buttons incl. triggers-as-buttons). Axis mapping: 4 stick axes + trigger axes→button values. Lazy Manette.Monitor init, graceful degradation without libmanette. |
| **webrtc** | Gst 1.0, GstWebRTC 1.0, GstSDP 1.0 | 302 (4 specs) | **Phase 1–4 — Data Channel + Media + Stats & Advanced.** RTCPeerConnection (offer/answer, ICE trickle, STUN/TURN config, addTransceiver, addTrack, removeTrack, getStats, restartIce, setConfiguration), RTCDataChannel (string + binary send/receive, bufferedAmount, binaryType), RTCRtpSender (track, getParameters/setParameters, replaceTrack, getCapabilities, getStats delegation), RTCRtpReceiver (track with muted→unmuted via ReceiverBridge, jitterBufferTarget, getStats delegation), RTCRtpTransceiver (mid, direction, stop, setCodecPreferences), MediaStream, MediaStreamTrack (GStreamer source integration, enabled→valve), getUserMedia (pipewiresrc/pulsesrc/v4l2src fallback), MediaDevices, **RTCDTMFSender** (spec-compliant tone/duration/gap, `tonechange` event), **RTCCertificate** (generateCertificate, W3C expiry), **RTCDtlsTransport / RTCIceTransport / RTCSctpTransport** (thin proxies), **RTCStatsReport** (GstStructure → W3C camelCase conversion via `gst-stats-parser.ts`). Outgoing pipeline: source→valve→convert→encode(opus/vp8)→payloader→capsfilter→webrtcbin. End-to-end bidirectional audio verified. Registers via `@gjsify/webrtc/register` (granular subpaths) — `--globals auto` picks them up. Requires GStreamer ≥ 1.20 with gst-plugins-bad + libnice-gstreamer. |
| **webrtc-native** | Gst 1.0, GstWebRTC 1.0, GstSDP 1.0 | — | Vala/GObject library consumed by `@gjsify/webrtc`. Exposes three main-thread signal bridges: `WebrtcbinBridge` (wraps webrtcbin's `on-negotiation-needed` / `on-ice-candidate` / `on-data-channel` + `notify::*-state`), `DataChannelBridge` (wraps GstWebRTCDataChannel's `on-open` / `on-close` / `on-error` / `on-message-string` / `on-message-data` / `on-buffered-amount-low` + `notify::ready-state`), `PromiseBridge` (wraps `Gst.Promise.new_with_change_func`). Each bridge connects on the C side (never invokes JS on the streaming thread) and re-emits via `GLib.Idle.add()` on the main context. Ships as prebuilt `.so` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-{x86_64,aarch64,ppc64,s390x,riscv64}/`; CI (`.github/workflows/prebuilds.yml`) rebuilds on Vala source changes (native runners for x86_64/aarch64; QEMU via `uraimo/run-on-arch-action` for ppc64/s390x/riscv64). |
| **webstorage** | — | 41 | Storage, localStorage, sessionStorage (W3C Web Storage) |
| **webassembly** | — | 15 | WebAssembly Promise-API polyfill — `compile`, `compileStreaming`, `instantiate`, `instantiateStreaming`, `validate` wrap SpiderMonkey's working synchronous `new WebAssembly.Module(buffer)` / `new WebAssembly.Instance(module, imports)` constructors. Granular `/register/promise` subpath. Auto-injected by `--globals auto` whenever the bundle references `WebAssembly.<method>` (via new `METHOD_MARKERS` in `detect-free-globals.ts`). |

### WebRTC Status

**Implemented (Phase 1 + 1.5 — Data Channel end-to-end):**
- RTCPeerConnection: constructor, createOffer, createAnswer, setLocalDescription, setRemoteDescription, addIceCandidate, close, createDataChannel, getConfiguration
- State getters: signalingState, connectionState, iceConnectionState, iceGatheringState, localDescription, remoteDescription, currentLocal/RemoteDescription, pendingLocal/RemoteDescription, canTrickleIceCandidates
- RTCPeerConnection events: negotiationneeded, icecandidate, icegatheringstatechange, iceconnectionstatechange, connectionstatechange, signalingstatechange, datachannel
- RTCDataChannel: send (string / ArrayBuffer / ArrayBufferView / Blob), close, readyState, bufferedAmount, bufferedAmountLowThreshold, binaryType ('arraybuffer' default, 'blob' lazy via globalThis.Blob), id, label, ordered, protocol, negotiated, maxPacketLifeTime, maxRetransmits
- RTCDataChannel events: open, close, message, error, bufferedamountlow, closing
- RTCSessionDescription (with Gst↔JS round-trip via GstSDP), RTCIceCandidate (W3C fields + candidate-line parser), RTCError (extends DOMException), RTCErrorEvent, RTCPeerConnectionIceEvent, RTCDataChannelEvent

**Implemented (Phase 2 — Media API Surface):**
- RTCPeerConnection.addTransceiver(kind, init) — creates real GstWebRTC transceivers, returns RTCRtpTransceiver
- RTCPeerConnection.getSenders / getReceivers / getTransceivers — return live lists
- RTCPeerConnection.removeTrack(sender) — validates sender, resets track
- RTCPeerConnection `track` event — fires on pad-added with RTCTrackEvent (receiver, track, streams, transceiver)
- RTCRtpTransceiver: mid, direction (read/write, mapped to GstWebRTC enum), currentDirection, stop(), setCodecPreferences(codecs)
- RTCRtpSender: track, dtmf, transport, getParameters/setParameters, replaceTrack (stub), getCapabilities(kind)
- RTCRtpReceiver: track (stub MediaStreamTrack, muted), jitterBufferTarget (0–4000ms range), getParameters, getCapabilities(kind)
- MediaStream: id, active, getTracks/getAudioTracks/getVideoTracks, getTrackById, addTrack/removeTrack, clone, addtrack/removetrack events
- MediaStreamTrack: id, kind, label, enabled, muted, readyState, contentHint, clone, stop
- MediaStreamTrackEvent, RTCTrackEvent
- Globals via `@gjsify/webrtc/register/media`: MediaStream, MediaStreamTrack, RTCTrackEvent

**Implemented (Phase 2.5 — Incoming Media Pipeline):**
- ReceiverBridge (Vala): manages muted source → decodebin → tee switching entirely in C to handle decodebin's streaming-thread `pad-added` signal
- RTCRtpReceiver._connectToPad wires webrtcbin output → ReceiverBridge → media-flowing signal → track unmute
- Track transitions from muted to unmuted when decoded media replaces the muted source
- Pipeline cleanup on RTCPeerConnection.close() disposes receiver bridges

**Implemented (Phase 3 — Outgoing Media + getUserMedia):**
- RTCPeerConnection.addTrack(track, ...streams) — creates transceiver, wires outgoing pipeline via request_pad_simple
- getUserMedia({ audio, video }) — wraps GStreamer sources (pipewiresrc → pulsesrc → autoaudiosrc → audiotestsrc fallback; pipewiresrc → v4l2src → autovideosrc → videotestsrc fallback)
- MediaDevices class with getUserMedia, enumerateDevices (stub), getSupportedConstraints
- navigator.mediaDevices registration via `@gjsify/webrtc/register/media-devices`
- Outgoing pipeline: source → valve → audioconvert/videoconvert → encoder (opusenc/vp8enc) → payloader (rtpopuspay/rtpvp8pay) → capsfilter → webrtcbin sink pad
- MediaStreamTrack GStreamer integration: _gstSource, _gstPipeline, enabled→valve.drop, stop()→NULL+dispose
- RTCRtpSender._wirePipeline builds explicit encoder chains (no Vala bridge needed — all main-thread)
- RTCRtpSender.replaceTrack with atomic source swap (unlink old, link new, sync state)
- Capsfilter with RTP caps ensures createOffer generates m= lines immediately
- End-to-end: pcA.addTrack(getUserMedia audio) → pcB receives track event, track unmutes
- Single-PC-per-track limitation (multi-PC fan-out via tee deferred to future)

**Implemented (Phase 4 — Stats & advanced):**
- RTCPeerConnection.getStats() — emits `get-stats` signal on webrtcbin, parses `GstStructure` → `RTCStatsReport` (Map<string, RTCStats>) via `gst-stats-parser.ts` (snake_case → camelCase conversion). `getStats(track)` validates selector against live senders/receivers and rejects with `InvalidAccessError` for unknown tracks. `sender.getStats()` / `receiver.getStats()` delegate via a stats callback wired in `addTrack`/`addTransceiver`.
- RTCPeerConnection.restartIce() — sets ICE restart flag, triggers `negotiationneeded` if negotiation is underway
- RTCPeerConnection.setConfiguration() — validates and applies ICE server updates; rejects immutable fields (`bundlePolicy`, `rtcpMuxPolicy`) with `InvalidModificationError`
- RTCDTMFSender — full spec-compliant tone insertion: DTMF char validation (0-9 A-D # * ,), `duration` clamping (40–6000ms), `interToneGap` (≥30ms), `toneBuffer` reader, `tonechange` event dispatch, `commaDelay` (2 s), `insertDTMF()` overwrites pending queue. Tested against `refs/wpt/webrtc/RTCDTMFSender-insertDTMF.https.html`
- RTCCertificate — `generateCertificate(algorithm)` validates ECDSA/RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5 params, returns certificate with 30-day expiry. `getFingerprints()`, `expires` getter. W3C API surface matches spec; actual DTLS cert is GStreamer-internal.
- RTCDtlsTransport, RTCIceTransport, RTCSctpTransport — thin W3C proxy classes (state, iceTransport getter, maxMessageSize, maxChannels). Exposed from `@gjsify/webrtc` index.

**Still deferred (post-Phase 4):**
- `icecandidateerror` event — stub (getter returns null, setter no-op); requires mapping webrtcbin ICE failure signals
- `peerIdentity` / `getIdentityAssertion` — stub (rejects with TypeError); identity provider integration not planned
- `setLocalDescription()` without explicit argument — callers must pass a `RTCSessionDescriptionInit`
- MediaStreamTrack constraints (`applyConstraints`, `getConstraints`, `getCapabilities` per-device)
- `enumerateDevices` with GStreamer Device Monitor
- Multi-PC-per-track fan-out via tee multiplexer

**Notes on spec behaviour (verified against WPT):**
- RTCDataChannel.binaryType defaults to `'arraybuffer'` — this IS the W3C spec default (§6.2: *"The initial value is 'arraybuffer'"*), distinct from WebSocket which defaults to `'blob'`. Invalid assignments are silently ignored per WPT [RTCDataChannel-binaryType.window.js](refs/wpt/webrtc/RTCDataChannel-binaryType.window.js) (matches Firefox / Chrome / Safari).
- Setting `binaryType` to `'blob'` requires `globalThis.Blob` (provide via `@gjsify/buffer/register`); otherwise the setter throws `NotSupportedError`.

**Current deviation from W3C spec:**
- `setLocalDescription()` without a description argument (implicit createOffer/createAnswer re-use) is not implemented — callers must pass an explicit `RTCSessionDescriptionInit`.

**How the GJS streaming-thread issue is solved (Phase 1.5):**

Webrtcbin emits `on-negotiation-needed`, `on-ice-candidate`, `on-data-channel` (and its `Gst.Promise` change_func callbacks) from GStreamer's internal streaming thread. GJS/SpiderMonkey blocks any JS callback invoked from a non-main thread (critical log: *"Attempting to call back into JSAPI on a different thread. … it has been blocked."*) to prevent VM corruption. An in-JS `GLib.idle_add` workaround doesn't help because the callback body itself never runs.

**`@gjsify/webrtc-native`** solves this on the C side: three Vala GObject classes (`WebrtcbinBridge`, `DataChannelBridge`, `PromiseBridge`) connect to the underlying GStreamer signals and `Gst.Promise` change_func, capture their args, then use `GLib.Idle.add()` to re-emit mirror signals on the main GLib context. JS consumers (`@gjsify/webrtc`) connect to those mirror signals and always run on the main thread.

Two subtleties in the bridge design:
1. `WebrtcbinBridge.on_data_channel_cb` wraps the incoming channel in a `DataChannelBridge` *on the streaming thread* before the idle hop — so the bridge's own signal handlers are connected before any `on-message-*` callbacks can fire on the same thread. Without this eager wrap, the first few messages from the remote peer would race the JS-side setup and get dropped.
2. The `GstWebRTCDataChannelState` C enum is **1-based** (`CONNECTING=1 … CLOSED=4`) but the auto-generated TypeScript declaration omits the initialiser and infers 0-based values. `RTCDataChannel` maps against the real 1-based runtime values.

Tests: **302 total across 4 spec files** (`webrtc.spec.ts` 87, `wpt.spec.ts` 96, `wpt-media.spec.ts` 109, `register.spec.ts` 10), including the full loopback (two local peers, offer/answer, ICE trickle, data-channel open/send/receive/echo).

**System prerequisites:**
- GStreamer ≥ 1.20 with **gst-plugins-bad** (for webrtcbin) AND **libnice-gstreamer** (for ICE transport — webrtcbin's state-change to PLAYING fails without it)
- Fedora:   `dnf install gstreamer1-plugins-bad-free gstreamer1-plugins-bad-free-extras libnice-gstreamer1`
- Ubuntu/Debian: `apt install gstreamer1.0-plugins-bad gstreamer1.0-nice`
- Verify:   `gst-inspect-1.0 webrtcbin && gst-inspect-1.0 nicesrc`

Tests that exercise `webrtcbin` (construction, close, loopback, getStats) auto-skip with a clear message if the nice plugin is missing; the remaining 18 tests (RTCSessionDescription, RTCIceCandidate parsing, register-subpath wiring) cover the platform-agnostic code paths.

| **dom-elements** | GdkPixbuf, `@gjsify/canvas2d-core` | 461 | Node(ownerDocument→document, event bubbling via parentNode), Element(setPointerCapture, releasePointerCapture, hasPointerCapture, **`_onResize`/`_fireResizeSubscribers` for backing-widget resize**, **`_allocatedClientWidth`/`_allocatedClientHeight` for GTK-allocation cache**), HTMLElement(getBoundingClientRect, **dataset/DOMStringMap**, **`clientWidth`/`clientHeight` / `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` / `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` all return the cached allocation written by `notifyElementResize()` instead of a hard-coded 0** — fixes Excalibur.js `Screen.FillContainer` reading `canvas.offsetWidth` (FillContainer sets `canvas.style = '100%'` then reads offset dims) and getting 0→0×0 blank canvas), HTMLCanvasElement (base DOM stub; auto-registers `'2d'` context factory via `@gjsify/canvas2d-core`), HTMLImageElement (**data: URI support**), HTMLMediaElement, HTMLVideoElement, Image, Document(body→documentElement tree), Text, Comment, DocumentFragment, DOMTokenList, MutationObserver, **ResizeObserver (real impl — wired into bridges' GTK resize signal via `notifyElementResize()`; fires on direct target + walks ancestors so `observe(canvas.parentElement)` catches Excalibur.js `FillContainer` mode; also caches the allocation on target + ancestors so subsequent `clientWidth`/`clientHeight` reads return the live size)**, IntersectionObserver, Attr, NamedNodeMap, NodeList. Auto-registers `globalThis.{Image,HTMLCanvasElement,document,self,devicePixelRatio,scrollX,scrollY,pageXOffset,pageYOffset,alert,AbortController,AbortSignal,fetch,Request,Response,Headers}` |
| **canvas2d-core** | Cairo, PangoCairo | 89 (8 specs: clearing, color, composite, drawimage, imagedata, state, text, transform) | **Headless** CanvasRenderingContext2D (HSL/HSLA color parsing, shadowBlur approximation, drawImage paint+clip, composite operations), CanvasGradient, CanvasPattern, Path2D, ImageData, color parser. NO GTK dependency — usable in pure-Cairo contexts. Extracted from `@gjsify/canvas2d` to break the dom-elements ↔ canvas2d circular dependency and to give downstream code a GTK-free 2D surface |
| **bridge-types** | — | — | DOMBridgeContainer(interface), BridgeEnvironment(isolated document+body+window per bridge), BridgeWindow(rAF, performance.now, viewport) |
| **canvas2d** | `@gjsify/canvas2d-core`, Cairo, GdkPixbuf, PangoCairo, Gtk 4 | — | Re-exports `canvas2d-core` surface + **FontFace** (pixel-perfect font rendering via PangoCairo) + Canvas2DBridge→Gtk.DrawingArea (GTK widget wrapper) |
| **webgl** | gwebgl, Gtk 4, Gio | 12 | WebGLRenderingContext (1.0), WebGL2RenderingContext (2.0, overrides texImage2D/texSubImage2D/drawElements for GLES3.2 compat, native FBO completeness delegation, GLSL 1.0 compatibility for versionless shaders, **clearBufferfv/iv/uiv/fi**, **premultipliedAlpha support**), HTMLCanvasElement (GTK-backed), WebGLBridge (Gtk.GLArea subclass, rAF, resize re-render, **eager context init**), Extensions. **Source split (Workstream D):** the 4164-line `src/ts/webgl-context-base.ts` is split into focused modules under `src/ts/context/` — `state.ts` (enable/disable/blend/stencil/clear/error stack), `buffer-binding.ts` (bindBuffer/bufferData/bufferSubData), `texture-management.ts` (bindTexture/texImage2D/texSubImage2D/pixelStorei + tracker), `framebuffer.ts` (bindFramebuffer/framebufferTexture2D/renderbufferStorage + completeness checks), `shader-program.ts` (compileShader/linkProgram/useProgram + uniform setters), `drawing.ts` (drawArrays/drawElements/viewport/scissor + vertex attribs). The base file (≈590 lines now) keeps fields, abstract `_gl`, constructor, `_init`, `getParameter`, `getExtension`, `getSupportedExtensions`, plus the foundational `_checkOwns` / `_checkValid` / `_checkWrapper` helpers. Each split module exports a typed `*Methods` interface (declaration-merged into `WebGLContextBase`) and an `install*Methods(proto)` registration function — the base file calls `installAllContextMethods(WebGLContextBase.prototype)` after the class declaration to sidestep the circular-import trap that prototype-merge mixins otherwise hit at module-load time. `as any` reduced from 74 → 44 across `packages/framework/webgl/src/`; the source-side counts dropped from 2 to 0 in `webgl-context-base.ts` and from 29 to 1 (in a doc comment) in `webgl2-rendering-context.ts`. All 860 GJS tests pass; the Three.js post-processing showcase rebuilds clean. |
| **event-bridge** | Gtk 4.0, Gdk 4.0 | — | attachEventControllers(): GTK4 controllers→DOM MouseEvent/PointerEvent/KeyboardEvent/WheelEvent/FocusEvent, **window-level keyboard listeners** |
| **iframe** | WebKit 6.0 | — | HTMLIFrameElement, IFrameBridge→WebKit.WebView, postMessage bridge |
| **video** | Gst 1.0, Gtk 4.0 | — | VideoBridge→Gtk.Picture(gtk4paintablesink). Supports srcObject(MediaStream from getUserMedia/WebRTC) + src(URI via playbin). Phase 1 |

Each port must add a SPDX header to the SCSS partial citing `refs/adwaita-web/adwaita-web/scss/_<name>.scss` and/or `refs/libadwaita/src/stylesheet/widgets/_<name>.scss` per the AGENTS.md Source Attribution rules.

### WebGL Known Issues

Issues discovered while porting Three.js demos. Non-fatal but should be addressed for full compatibility.

| Issue | Severity | Details | Affected Demos |
|-------|----------|---------|----------------|
| `EXT_color_buffer_float` extension missing | Medium | Three.js requests this extension for `HalfFloatType` render targets. Not implemented in extension registry. Rendering works but with fallback quality. | LDraw, Pixel Post-Processing |
| WebGL1 `setError` calls too strict for WebGL2 | Low | Base class validation (texImage2D, renderbufferStorage, etc.) uses WebGL1 format/type rules. WebGL2 allows more combinations (R8, RG8, RGBA16F, DEPTH_COMPONENT24, etc.). Non-fatal — native GL still executes the calls. | All WebGL2 demos |
| WebGL1 framebuffer color attachment validation too strict | Low | Base `_preCheckFramebufferStatus` only accepts RGBA/UNSIGNED_BYTE or RGBA/FLOAT. WebGL2 override delegates to native driver. WebGL1 with extensions (OES_texture_half_float) still rejects valid formats. | Post-processing with WebGL1 |

### Missing Web APIs

Not yet implemented (but potentially relevant for GJS projects):

| API | Priority | Notes |
|-----|----------|-------|
| ~~**ECDSA sign/verify**~~ | ✓ Done | Implemented in Phase 19 (RFC 6979 + FIPS 186-4) |
| ~~**RSA-PSS / RSA-OAEP**~~ | ✓ Done | Implemented in Phase 19 (RFC 8017, MGF1) |
| **URL/URLSearchParams (global)** | Low | Exists in @gjsify/url, missing as global export |
| **Blob/File (global)** | Low | Partially native in GJS; globals package could re-export |
| **structuredClone** | Low | Natively available in SpiderMonkey 128 |
| **Performance (global)** | Low | @gjsify/perf_hooks exists; Web export missing |

| **resolve-npm** | Central alias registry (60+ mappings) | Full |
| **empty** | Stub module for platform exclusion | Full |
| **lightningcss-native** | Phase D-2 POC. Vala+Rust cdylib bridge to lightningcss for GJS. Two GObject methods: `Engine.transform()` (in-memory CSS string → CSS string) and `Engine.bundle()` (entry CSS file → bundled CSS, resolves `@import` chains via lightningcss's filesystem-backed `FileProvider`). Both accept browserslist queries directly. Backs `cssAsStringPlugin` under `--app gjs` (when its `hasNativeLightningcss()` probe succeeds, the plugin transparently routes through this package; otherwise falls back to the existing npm `lightningcss` path). Ships Rust `cdylib` (`libgjsify_lightningcss.so`, ~8.3 MB) + Vala bridge (`libgjsifylightningcss.so`, ~34 KB) + GIR/typelib in `prebuilds/linux-x86_64/`. POC-only: no source-map input, no css-modules, no analyze-dependencies — those are Phase B work. | POC |
| **lightningcss-wasm** | Phase D-2 POC (WASM track companion to `lightningcss-native`). Vendors `lightningcss-wasm@1.32.0`'s NAPI-on-WASM bundle (15.8 MiB `.wasm` + the napi-wasm runtime + the asyncify async helper). Pure-JS loader instantiates the WASM via SpiderMonkey 140's synchronous `new WebAssembly.{Module,Instance}` constructors; reads bytes via `node:fs` (resolves to `@gjsify/fs` under `--app gjs`); resolves `__getrandom_v03_custom` via `globalThis.crypto.getRandomValues` (`@gjsify/webcrypto` under `--app gjs`). **No WASI shim required** — napi-wasm is a pure-JS NAPI host. Smoke tested under stock `gjs -m`: 0.81 ms for nesting flatten + minify of a 90 B fixture. Decision matrix: **3-5× slower than native** on transforms, ~960× slower cold init — kept in tree as a fallback for unsupported architectures. | POC |
| **rolldown-native** | Phase D-2 POC. Vala+Rust cdylib bridge to rolldown for GJS. Wraps `rolldown::Bundler::generate()` (async, tokio) in a per-call current-thread runtime so JS sees a sync `bundle()` call. JSON-encoded `BundlerOptions` in (camelCase, matches rolldown's serde shape), JSON-encoded BundleOutput out (`{warnings, output: (Chunk\|Asset)[]}`). Ships Rust cdylib (`libgjsify_rolldown.so`, ~13 MB) + Vala bridge + GIR/typelib in `prebuilds/linux-x86_64/`. Smoke tested under GJS 1.88: 17 ms cold for a 3-file ESM bundle with constant-inlining + tree-shake; 25-byte minified output for a single-export entry; clear UnresolvedEntry diagnostics on missing inputs. POC-only: no JS plugins (Phase B), no watch/HMR, no incremental builds. **Build constraint**: depends on `refs/rolldown` submodule via cargo path-dep — rolldown 0.1.0 on crates.io has broken transitive deps (rolldown_fs/oxc_resolver_fs trait mismatch). End consumers receive only the prebuilt `.so` + `.typelib` and never see the submodule. | POC |

| **@gjsify/utils** | Gio wrappers, process info, encoding, ensureMainLoop | Full |
| **@gjsify/runtime** | Platform-independent runtime detection (isGJS, isNode, runtimeName) | Full |

## GNOME Library Usage

| GNOME Lib | Used In |
|-----------|---------|
| **Gio 2.0** | fs, net, dns, child_process, dgram, tls, module, fetch, xmlhttprequest |
| **GLib 2.0** | crypto, url, os, process, dns, child_process, dgram, tls, module, timers, xmlhttprequest (temp files) |
| **Soup 3.0** | http, https, fetch, websocket, eventsource, xmlhttprequest |
| **Gda 6.0** | sqlite (libgda SQLite provider) |
| **Gtk 4.0** | canvas2d (DrawingArea), webgl (GLArea), video (Picture), event-bridge |
| **Gdk 4.0** | event-bridge (key/modifier state) |
| **GdkPixbuf 2.0** | dom-elements (HTMLImageElement) |
| **WebKit 6.0** | iframe (WebKit.WebView) |
| **Cairo + PangoCairo** | canvas2d-core, canvas2d (FontFace) |
| **gwebgl 0.1** | webgl (Vala extension) |
| **Gst 1.0** | webaudio, webrtc, video (all GStreamer pipelines) |
| **GstApp 1.0** | webaudio (appsrc/appsink for PCM I/O) |
| **GstWebRTC 1.0** | webrtc (webrtcbin element) |
| **GstSDP 1.0** | webrtc (SDP message parse/serialize via `SDPMessage.new_from_text` + `as_text`) |
| **Manette 0.2** | gamepad (libmanette monitor + devices) |
| **`@gjsify/webrtc-native` (Vala)** | webrtc (main-thread signal bridges for webrtcbin / data channels / Gst.Promise) |
| **`@gjsify/http-soup-bridge` (Vala)** | http (libsoup server bridge — GC-safe SoupServerMessage lifetime, SSE/long-poll peer-close detection via `g_socket_create_source`) |
| **`@gjsify/http2-native` (Vala + C shim)** | http2 (libnghttp2 HPACK encoder + raw frame builder for PUSH_PROMISE / DATA / HEADERS, server-side even-id allocator, h2c connection-preface detection — buffers C-side via GLib.Bytes) |
| **`@gjsify/terminal-native` (Vala)** | tty, process (optional terminal syscalls — Posix.isatty, ioctl TIOCGWINSZ, termios raw mode, SIGWINCH; GLib/env fallback when not installed) |
| **`@gjsify/lightningcss-native` (Vala + Rust cdylib)** | Phase D-2 POC bridge to the `lightningcss` crate. Rust shim (`src/rust/`, `lightningcss = "1"` from crates.io) exposes a one-shot extern `gjsify_lightningcss_transform()`; C glue (`src/vala/gjsify-lightningcss-glue.c`) translates the malloc'd Rust struct into `GBytes` + `GError`; Vala wraps that as `GjsifyLightningcss.Engine.transform()`. Used today only by future Phase B `cssAsStringPlugin` integration; the existing build pipeline still pulls npm `lightningcss` until the WASM-vs-FFI decision matrix lands. |
| **`@gjsify/rolldown-native` (Vala + Rust cdylib)** | Phase D-2 POC bridge to the `rolldown` crate. Rust shim (`src/rust/`, path-dep on `refs/rolldown` due to crates.io 0.1.0 having broken transitive deps) wraps `rolldown::Bundler::generate()` in a per-call current-thread tokio runtime + JSON I/O at the FFI boundary; C glue (`src/vala/gjsify-rolldown-glue.c`) translates the malloc'd Rust JSON string into `GBytes` + `GError`; Vala wraps that as `GjsifyRolldown.Bundler.bundle()`. Used today only by future Phase B `gjsify build` engine swap; the existing pipeline still calls npm `rolldown` until plugin callbacks (Phase B Hard Item) ship via the Vala signal-bridge pattern from `@gjsify/webrtc-native`. |

## Priorities / Next Steps

### Completed

- ~~**install-perf consolidation — packument gzip + shared cache primitive**~~✓ — Follow-up to #452/#455/#456. **Packument gzip:** `buildHeaders` gained a per-call-site `acceptEncoding`; `fetchPackumentConditional` requests `gzip` (~4× smaller — 770 KB → 186 KB / 10 `@girs/*` packuments) + `compress: false`, then `decodeJsonBody` buffer-then-gunzips (gzip-magic sniff + full-buffer `DecompressionStream`, the proven `@gjsify/tar` `.tgz` pattern) because `@gjsify/fetch`'s streaming `DecompressionStream` trips libsoup `G_IO_ERROR_PARTIAL_INPUT` on npm-CDN gzip (empirically reconfirmed). Cross-platform via the magic-byte sniff (Node/undici may pre-decode; GJS yields raw gzip). `fetchTarball` stays `identity` (already-gzipped `.tgz`; transport-gzip breaks the raw-bytes SRI check). **Shared cache primitive:** new `install-cache-fs.ts` (`gjsifyCacheRoot`/`atomicWrite`/`readCacheFile`) de-dups the XDG-root + atomic-write + read-or-MISS logic both caches re-implemented; tarball cache's twice-inlined SRI base64→hex unified into a local `parseSri`. Pure extraction, public signatures unchanged. Install A/B lockfile byte-identical to the `identity` baseline; re-resolve still yields packument 304s + tarball hits. npm-registry 88 (+10) + cli 320 (+6 cache-fs) + e2e 2/2 green; tsc/oxfmt/oxlint clean.

- ~~**`gjsify install` cold-install speed — packument metadata cache (PR 3 of the install-perf series)**~~✓ — On-disk packument cache with ETag revalidation so a re-resolve (lockfile miss) skips re-downloading unchanged packuments (fetched `accept-encoding: identity`, ~60–98 KB each). `@gjsify/npm-registry` gains `fetchPackumentConditional()` (sends `If-None-Match`; 304 → `{status:'not-modified'}` no body, 200 → `{status:'fresh', packument, etag}`); `fetchPackument()` delegates to it, behaviour-preserving. New CLI `install-packument-cache.ts` stores `{etag, packument}` under `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/gjsify/metadata/v1/`, keyed by (registry, name) so scope-registry overrides can't cross-contaminate; `GJSIFY_PACKUMENT_CACHE=0` disables. `resolveDeps`' `fetchPkg` revalidates and reuses the cached body on 304. Verified live: a second resolve of a 7-package `@girs/*` graph produced 7 × 304 (zero body re-downloads). npm-registry 78 (+4) + cli 307 (+6) green; tsc/oxfmt/oxlint clean. **Install-perf series complete.**
- ~~**`gjsify install` cold-install speed — parallel resolve (PR 2 of the install-perf series)**~~✓ — `resolveDeps()` was a fully serial BFS (`await fetchPackument` per edge → N sequential ~RTT round-trips, the dominant cold-install cost). Rewritten into a wave-based BFS: drain the current queue level, prefetch every not-yet-cached packument in that level in parallel (bounded to `DEFAULT_CONCURRENCY` 16, via new `prefetchPackuments`), then apply hoist/nest placement serially in the identical FIFO order. Because children always append to the queue end, the drained level is visited in the same order → placement decisions and the lockfile are **byte-for-byte unchanged** (verified by diffing `gjsify-lock.json` from the serial vs parallel bundle on a real 9-spec → 24-entry `@girs/*` graph). `packumentCache` keeps it to ≤1 fetch per unique name; optional-dep 404s reject into the cache and are re-surfaced + skipped by the per-edge `try/catch`. New `tests/e2e/native-install` conflict case (`app → dep@^1 + mid@^1`, `mid → dep@^2` ⇒ `dep@2` nests under `mid`, `dep@1` hoists) guards the order-sensitive nesting path. Builds on PR 1's lifted Soup conn cap. `@gjsify/cli` 295 + e2e 2/2 green; tsc/oxfmt/oxlint clean.
- ~~**`gjsify install` cold-install speed — quick wins (PR 1 of an install-perf series)**~~✓ — Measured the cold-install bottleneck on the map-editor monorepo (687 tarballs): the shared `Soup.Session` in `@gjsify/fetch` was capped at libsoup's default `max-conns-per-host=2`, serialising every bulk fetch to two-at-a-time **even over the negotiated HTTP/2 connection** (16 packuments measured at ~3.0s vs ~0.24s once the cap is lifted — a **12.8× difference**, same h2 connection). Three changes: (1) `getSharedSession()` now constructs with `maxConnsPerHost: 16` + `maxConns: 64` (construct-only props in libsoup 3), env-overridable via `GJSIFY_FETCH_MAX_CONNS_PER_HOST` / `GJSIFY_FETCH_MAX_CONNS` — matches npm's `maxsockets` (15) and pnpm's `network-concurrency` (16), browser-competitive, harmless for light fetch users. (2) `install-backend-native.ts` download pool default `8 → 16` (only translates to real concurrency now that the Soup cap was raised). (3) **npm `_cacache` interop** — `getForeignCachedTarball()` reads npm's content-addressable store (same SRI key, raw `.tgz` bytes) as a second-chance source after gjsify's own store and before the network, writing through to our store on a hit; honours `GJSIFY_NPM_CACHE` (path override / `0` disables), `npm_config_cache`, then `~/.npm`. pnpm/yarn/bun stores are deliberately NOT read (per-file/zip layouts don't map to a tarball-by-integrity lookup). A user who has run `npm install` before turns a cold `gjsify install` near-warm with zero network round-trips. Also fixed a stale unused `SAMPLE_HEX` test constant (wrong digest, never exercised until the new cacache tests used it). `@gjsify/cli` 295 tests green (+4 cacache tests), `@gjsify/fetch` 79 GJS tests green (GC-safety guarantees intact), tsc + oxfmt + oxlint clean. Follow-ups tracked in Open TODOs: parallelize the serial resolve BFS (PR 2), packument metadata cache with ETag revalidation (PR 3).

- ~~**`Open TODOs > BLOCKER`: first-publish + Trusted Publisher for `@gjsify/vite-plugin-gjsify` (stale-entry cleanup)**~~✓ — The BLOCKER entry was added before the package's first npm release. As of v0.4.37 (2026-05-29), `@gjsify/vite-plugin-gjsify` is published on npmjs.com at `latest: 0.4.37` (verified via `curl https://registry.npmjs.org/@gjsify%2fvite-plugin-gjsify`) and the v0.4.37 `release.yml` run succeeded end-to-end (all packages published via OIDC + Trusted Publisher, no alphabetical-loop break). The BLOCKER's failure-mode condition ("next release.yml run breaks if first-publish is skipped") is no longer applicable — first publish + Trusted Publisher must have been bootstrapped in an earlier maintainer action. Removing the entry keeps `Open TODOs` reflecting only actually-deferred work. (If a future audit shows the Trusted Publisher config IS missing despite v0.4.37 publishing successfully, the next release.yml run will surface the same `404 — OIDC token exchange error - package not found` symptom and the entry can be re-added with that updated context.)
- ~~**`@gjsify/child_process` — Node-parity audit (14 silent-semantic-mismatch fixes + 86 new tests)**~~✓ — Systematic differential audit of `@gjsify/child_process` against `refs/node/lib/child_process.js` + `refs/node-test/parallel/test-child-process-*.js` + `refs/deno/ext/node/polyfills/child_process.ts`. Same class of bug the env-undefined-nulls-stdin discovery (#407) caught accidentally; this PR fixes the rest deterministically with a permanent regression net. **14 bugs fixed** in `packages/node/child_process/src/index.ts`: (1) `env` values are now coerced via `${value}` template (so `null` → `'null'`, numbers → decimal, booleans → `'true'`/`'false'`, arrays → `'a,b,c'`), and `undefined` is dropped — matches `refs/node-test/parallel/test-child-process-env.js`. (2) env iterates the prototype chain via `for…in` (was `Object.entries`, which dropped inherited keys). (3) `env: {}` now wipes the parent env via `launcher.set_environ([])` (was leaking parent env). (4) `cwd` accepts `URL` (file://) via duck-typing on `.href`/`.protocol` (instanceof URL is unreliable across @gjsify/url realms) and throws synchronously on non-`file:` URLs; also accepts `Buffer`/`Uint8Array`. (5) `argv0` override via `/bin/sh -c 'exec -a "$0" "$@"'` wrapper (Gio doesn't expose `set_child_setup`); throws synchronously on non-string. (6) `detached` wraps argv with `/usr/bin/setsid` for the new-process-group semantics Node documents on POSIX. (7) `exec`/`execFile`/`execSync` honour `encoding: 'buffer'` and `encoding: null` (return `Buffer` instead of string); switched `_exec` from `communicate_utf8_async` to `communicate_async` to get raw bytes. (8) `exec` honours `timeout` + `killSignal` (kills child after N ms); (9) `exec` honours `maxBuffer` (throws `ERR_CHILD_PROCESS_STDIO_MAXBUFFER` on overflow, truncates to cap); (10) `exec` honours `AbortSignal` (matches the spawn-side AbortSignal handling that was already there). (11) `spawn` honours `timeout` + `killSignal`. (12) `spawnSync` honours `timeout` via `/usr/bin/timeout` argv wrapper (`communicate()` blocks the GLib main loop so `GLib.timeout_add` won't fire — wrapping argv is the only sync-safe path; detects exit 124/137 + maps to `ETIMEDOUT`). (13) ENOENT normalisation for missing binaries: spawn-time GIO errors now decode to `err.code='ENOENT'`/`err.errno=-2`/`err.syscall='spawn <file>'`/`err.path=<file>`/`err.spawnargs=<args>` via `e.matches(GLib.SpawnError, GLib.SpawnError.NOENT)` detection — critical for execa-class consumers that pattern-match on `code === 'ENOENT'`. The detection function explicitly does NOT wrap the GLib error in `new Error(String(e))` (which would discard the `.matches()` helper), but DOES create a fresh Error at the end so `err instanceof Error` holds (GLib_SpawnError doesn't inherit from Error on GJS). (14) `spawn`/`spawnSync` validate options-not-object + non-string argv0 + non-Buffer/string `input` synchronously (Node throws TypeError for these — was being swallowed by spawnSync's error-to-result-field path). Plus: `kill()` accepts named signals (`SIGINT`/`SIGHUP`/`SIGABRT`/`SIGUSR1`/`SIGUSR2`/`SIGTERM`/`SIGKILL`) and maps to POSIX numbers; `signalCode` reports the requested `killSignal` (was hardcoded `'SIGTERM'`); cwd validation moved BEFORE the spawn try/catch so TypeError throws synchronously (matches Node's API contract). New `packages/node/child_process/src/parity.spec.ts` adds **86 new tests** organised into 13 describe blocks covering each gap; test count 124 → 210, all green on both Node and GJS. `@gjsify/integration-execa` 44/44 still green. Lint clean, tsc `--noEmit` clean.
- ~~**MCP TypeScript SDK integration suite expansion (108 → 281 tests, +7 spec files)**~~✓ — Ports `inMemory.test.ts`, `shared/stdio.test.ts`, `shared/uriTemplate.test.ts`, `shared/toolNameValidation.test.ts`, `client/stdio.test.ts`, plus elicitation / sampling / cancellation / progress flows derived from `server/elicitation.test.ts` + `shared/protocol.test.ts`, all at SDK v1.29.0. **Node: 281/281 green; GJS: 281/287 green** (6 failures are pre-existing flaky `streamable-http.spec.ts` timeouts under libsoup long-poll SSE pause — confirmed against a bare `origin/main` baseline, NOT introduced by this expansion). Targets the architectural pattern that exposed PR #407's `@gjsify/child_process` env-undefined silent-data-loss bug: subprocess + stdio JSON-RPC. New specs: **`in-memory-transport.spec.ts`** (10 tests — pair, both directions, auth-info, close-propagation, send-after-close, pre-start queue, error/notification round-trip), **`stdio-buffer.spec.ts`** (8 tests — `ReadBuffer` line framing including byte-by-byte split + mid-codepoint UTF-8 chunking + `serializeMessage`/`deserializeMessage` round-trip), **`uri-template.spec.ts`** (28 tests — RFC 6570 operators, matching, edge cases, **CVE-2026-0621 ReDoS regression cases** on exploded list operators), **`tool-name-validation.spec.ts`** (21 tests — valid/invalid chars, length cap, advisory warnings with `console.warn` capture), **`stdio-subprocess.spec.ts`** (4 tests — `StdioClientTransport` over `/usr/bin/tee` with start/close/pid/env round-trip — regression coverage for PR #407), **`server-initiated-requests.spec.ts`** (4 tests — bidirectional sampling + elicitation via `setRequestHandler(CreateMessageRequestSchema|ElicitRequestSchema)` plus error propagation), **`cancellation-progress.spec.ts`** (5 tests — `AbortSignal`/`extra.signal.aborted` flow, `extra.sendNotification('notifications/progress')` round-trip, `McpError`/`RequestTimeout`). All new tests pass on both runtimes; the only failures observed (`streamable-http.spec.ts`) were already failing on `origin/main`.
> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, Welle 3-F browser-polyfill wiring):** Four-commit follow-up to Welle 2-T (PR #388) and the staged browser-polyfill PRs #392 – #396. Lands the wiring that makes the foundation operational while keeping CI green. (1) `packages/infra/rolldown-plugin-gjsify/src/app/browser.ts` now consumes `ALIASES_NODE_FOR_BROWSER` from `@gjsify/resolve-npm` — a `for…of` loop generates both the bare (`stream`) and `node:*` (`node:stream`) form for every entry, eliminating per-target hand-curation. The legacy `browserPolyfillAliases` Record is emptied to `{}` (its `process: '@gjsify/empty'` and `assert: '@gjsify/assert'` entries shadowed the curated table — `process` now correctly routes to `@gjsify/process`). (2) New meta-pkg `packages/node/browser-polyfills/` (`@gjsify/browser-node-polyfills`) — browser counterpart to `@gjsify/node-polyfills`. Curated 28 workspace deps covering every Node polyfill that ships a browser-runnable build (post-PR-#392-#396 slot state). Triplet `{gjs: polyfill, node: native, browser: polyfill}`. `globals.mjs` wires `@gjsify/buffer/register` today; grows as per-package `/register` granular subpaths land. (3) `packages/infra/resolve-npm/lib/globals-map.mjs` gains `BROWSER_NATIVE_IDENTS` (Set of every browser-native identifier from `GJS_GLOBALS_GROUPS.{web,dom}` + shared Node-group), `BROWSER_GLOBALS_MAP` (strict subset of `GJS_GLOBALS_MAP` still needing browser-side polyfills — today: just `Buffer`), and `getGlobalsMapFor(target)` helper. `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` `BROWSER_NATIVE_RE_EXPORTS` no longer carries a TODO empty placeholder; sources the curated set. (4) PR-B (`--check` default flip to strict) is PARKED behind a follow-up — 26 packages on the integration base lack `src/test.browser.{mts,ts}` entries; forcing the flip would put every CI run into permanent failure. Forward-compatible `--quick` flag wired today (no-op until the future flip); the rollback path is documented inline so the eventual flip becomes a one-constant change. T-Plan sections 2 (PR-D), 4 (PR-F), 5 (PR-G) landed; section 1 (PR-B) prep-only.

> Last updated: 2026-05-30 — Welle 3-D: in-memory browser polyfill for `@gjsify/fs` (memfs-inspired), runtime slot `browser:"partial"`.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, `@gjsify/fs` browser polyfill — welle 3-D):** New `packages/node/fs/src/browser.ts` barrel + `src/browser/{volume,sync,async,promises,promises-entry,stream,types,errors,constants,path-utils}.ts` sub-modules ship an in-memory POSIX-shaped file system that the `--app browser` bundler picks up via the package's new top-level `"browser":"./lib/esm/browser.js"` field + the `"."` exports `"browser"` condition + new `./browser` / `./browser/promises` / `./browser/{volume,sync,async,stream}` subpaths. Architecture is a single global `__defaultVolume = new Volume()` whose tree is a `Map<string, FileNode | DirNode | SymlinkNode>` keyed by path segment, with all sync (`readFileSync`/`writeFileSync`/`appendFileSync`/`statSync`/`lstatSync`/`existsSync`/`accessSync`/`readdirSync`/`mkdirSync`/`rmdirSync`/`rmSync`/`unlinkSync`/`renameSync`/`copyFileSync`/`linkSync`/`symlinkSync`/`readlinkSync`/`realpathSync`/`chmodSync`/`chownSync`/`truncateSync`/`utimesSync`/`mkdtempSync`/`openSync`/`closeSync`/`readSync`/`writeSync`) routed straight at it. Callback (`fs.X(...,cb)`) and promise (`fs.promises.X()` / `import 'fs/promises'`) APIs wrap the sync impls via `queueMicrotask` so Node's "always async" callback contract holds, errors become `{code:'ENOENT',errno:-2,syscall,path}` shaped from `src/browser/errors.ts`, and the three surfaces share one volume — a `writeFile()` is visible to the next `readFileSync()`. `Stats` / `BigIntStats` / `Dirent` are the proper Node-prototype classes (`isFile()`/`isDirectory()`/`isSymbolicLink()`), backed by a shared `KindTests` abstract base. `createReadStream` / `createWriteStream` are minimal Node-shape streams (`on`/`once`/`pipe`/`destroy`/async iterator) over `queueMicrotask`-pumped chunks (no `@gjsify/stream` dep — the bundle stays self-contained). `Volume` is also exported as a class so test code can build sandbox volumes (`new Volume()` → `fromJSON()` / `toJSON()` memfs parity helpers). Slot is `partial` — known gaps documented in the file header: no OPFS / IndexedDB persistence (page reload wipes the volume), `FSWatcher` is a stub (registers events, never fires), symlinks are stored but never followed by `realpath` / `stat`, `chmod` / `chown` alter mode bits but enforce nothing, file descriptors are minimal (no `readv` / `writev` / `fsync` / `fdatasync`), no recursive `cp`. The runtimes triplet is now `{gjs:"polyfill", node:"none", browser:"partial"}` — `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` got a `has_browser_polyfill` signal (detects `src/browser.ts`) so the auto-suggestion matches the new declared shape (drift 0). Future iteration: bridge the volume to OPFS for persistence + thread mutator events through `FSWatcher` (chokidar / vite-watch parity).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, `@gjsify/crypto` browser polyfill — wave 3-C):** XL lift from `.gjsify-browser-audit-R1.md` § "Major lifts" #1 lands. `packages/node/crypto/src/browser.ts` (212 LoC barrel) + `src/browser/{random,hash,hmac,kdf,cipher,sign,stubs}.ts` (1229 LoC across seven feature-grouped modules, 1441 LoC total) ship a WebCrypto-first browser polyfill behind the new `"browser"` field + `exports."."` `"browser"` condition (Rolldown/Vite `mainFields:['browser','module','main']` + `conditionNames:[…,'browser']` resolve it automatically; the GJS index.ts is bypassed in `--app browser` builds). Strategy: native re-export wherever WebCrypto already exposes the primitive (`webcrypto`→`globalThis.crypto`, `subtle`→`globalThis.crypto.subtle`, `randomBytes/Fill/UUID/Int` over `getRandomValues`/`randomUUID`); WebCrypto-backed async classes for Hash/Hmac/Cipher/Sign with explicit `digestAsync()`/`finalAsync()`/`signAsync()` entries and structured `ENOTSUP_SYNC_*` errors on the sync surface (no pure-JS sha-256/aes impls vendored — the bundle stays tight and the async path is first-class). PBKDF2 + HKDF via `subtle.deriveBits`; AES-CBC/CTR/GCM Cipher/Decipher with `setAAD`/`getAuthTag`/`setAuthTag` GCM helpers. Classic DH/ECDH/RSA-OAEP/scrypt/KeyObject/X509Certificate throw ENOTSUP unconditionally (no WebCrypto pendant), each with a per-API hint pointing at the closest `subtle.*` call. `runtimes.browser` flips `none` → `partial`. `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` learns a new `has_browser_polyfill` source-signal (`src/browser.ts` presence) so GJS-bound packages that opt into a dedicated browser entry suggest `browser:partial` instead of the default `none`. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `audit-runtimes --check` clean (79 declarable packages match).

> Last updated: 2026-05-30 — Welle 3-B: browser-polyfill entries for the stream / http / https / zlib family (sequel to Welle 3-A which shipped process / os / dns / ws / module).

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, Welle 3-B — stream/http/https/zlib browser polyfills):** Second implementation batch of the cross-runtime browser-polyfill wave. Ships dedicated `src/browser.ts` entries for the stream-family Node polyfills alongside their existing GJS-bound `src/index.ts` impls. **`@gjsify/stream`** — EventEmitter + `queueMicrotask`-backed `Readable` / `Writable` / `Duplex` / `Transform` / `PassThrough` + `Stream` (legacy `.pipe()`) + `pipeline` + `finished`, declaring the full stream-browserify surface in 505 LOC. Slot stays `browser:"polyfill"` but now ships a proper browser entry instead of bundling the GJS impl. **`@gjsify/http`** — `request()` / `get()` ride on top of native `fetch()`, response bodies stream through an EventEmitter-shaped `IncomingMessage`; `createServer` / `Server` / `ServerResponse` / `OutgoingMessage` throw ENOTSUP (no listening socket in a browser). 392 LOC. Slot `browser:"none"→"partial"`. **`@gjsify/https`** — thin wrapper re-exporting `@gjsify/http`'s browser entry with an additional `Agent` stub (browser `fetch()` handles TLS transparently for `https://` URLs). 54 LOC. Slot `browser:"polyfill"→"partial"`. **`@gjsify/zlib`** — top-level callback APIs (`gzip`/`gunzip`/`deflate`/`inflate`/`deflateRaw`/`inflateRaw`/`brotliCompress`/`brotliDecompress`) ride on native `CompressionStream` / `DecompressionStream`; matching Transform-stream classes (`Gzip`/`Gunzip`/`Deflate`/`Inflate`/`DeflateRaw`/`InflateRaw`/`BrotliCompress`/`BrotliDecompress`) layered over `@gjsify/stream/browser`; sync variants throw with an ENOTSUP-hint pointing at the async API (CompressionStream is async-only). Brotli requires Chrome 117+; older browsers will surface a runtime error from the native ctor. 242 LOC. Slot `browser:"none"→"partial"`. `audit-runtimes.mjs` learns one extra wiring step: the pure-TS branch now also honours `has_browser_src + browser_src_is_partial` to recognise the `@gjsify/https` shape (pure-TS index but partial browser). `tsc --noEmit` clean across all four modified packages; `node scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs --check` passes (79 declarable packages match, 0 drift). Browser-build smoke-test deferred — depends on `ALIASES_NODE_FOR_BROWSER` from PR #388 being merged so `gjsify build --app browser` can resolve `node:*` specifiers to `@gjsify/<X>/browser` routes.

> **Current state (post-v0.4.33, 2026-05-30, Welle 3-A browser polyfill entries):** First implementation batch of the cross-runtime browser-polyfill wave (sequel to Welle 2 — refs vendored in #386, slot flips in #387, tooling foundation in #388). Six new dedicated `src/browser.ts` entries land alongside the existing GJS-bound `src/index.ts` impls in `packages/node/{process,os,dns,ws,module}` plus a `src/test.browser.mts` re-export entry for `@gjsify/buffer`. Each `browser.ts` is a self-contained SPDX-MIT-licensed ESM module exporting a runtime-coherent surface — `@gjsify/process` ships the canonical defunctzombie/node-process subset (nextTick via queueMicrotask, env={}, cwd='/', browser=true, EventEmitter no-op surface, stdin/stdout/stderr stubs); `@gjsify/os` mirrors os-browserify + the modern Node 22 additions (availableParallelism, userInfo, getPriority/setPriority); `@gjsify/dns` is a `partial` slot — `lookup()` resolves to loopback, every real-resolution method throws ENOTSUP with a Node-shaped err `{code, errno, syscall, hostname}`; `@gjsify/ws` re-exports `globalThis.WebSocket` for the client path + ENOTSUP-throws `WebSocketServer` / `Server` / `createWebSocketStream` for the server path (so npm `ws` consumers' static prop reads survive bundle init even when the runtime branch never fires); `@gjsify/module` ships `vite-plugin-node-polyfills`'s `builtinModules=[]` / `isBuiltin()=false` / `createRequire(url) → require-stub` shape. Companion cross-runtime fix: `@gjsify/console` + `@gjsify/timers` had `globals.mjs` files re-exporting from `node:*`, which fail to resolve in the browser bundle — both now route through `globalThis.*` (with a Promise-based setImmediate polyfill in `@gjsify/timers` for the browser path), flipping both slots from `browser:"polyfill"` to `browser:"native"`. `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` learned to read these new signals (`has_browser_src`, `browser_src_is_partial`, `globals_mjs_browser_safe`) so `--check` continues to pass at 79/79 declarable packages with zero drift. `package.json` shape per browser-entry package: top-level `"browser": "./lib/esm/browser.js"` for legacy bundler conditionals + `exports."."."browser"` + new `./browser` subpath export. TS check clean across all 5 modified packages individually (process/os/dns/ws/module). Browser-build smoke-test deferred to a follow-up (depends on `ALIASES_NODE_FOR_BROWSER` from #388 being merged).

- ~~**TypeScript range cleanup — workspace-wide `typescript: "^5.9.3"`, declaration vs lockfile drift resolved**~~✓ — Long-standing inconsistency where root + ~135 non-integration workspaces declared `typescript: "^6.0.3"` while the lockfile actually resolved to `5.9.3` (because three `tests/integration/{deepkit-type-compiler,loro-crdt,typescript-tsc}` packages pin upstream-fixed `^5.4.5` / `^5.7.3` ranges, and 5.9.3 is the highest version satisfying every range — `^6.0.3` does not match 5.9.3). Surfaced during PR #385 (`@gjsify/tsc`) when `gjsify upgrade --check` failed on the newly-declared `^5.9.3` range, then bumping it to `^6.0.3` snapped the lockfile to 6.0.3 and broke `@gjsify/dom-elements` on TS 6's stricter `unknown`-narrowing. Two options evaluated: (A) match reality — change root + non-integration declarations from `^6.0.3` → `^5.9.3`, lockfile unchanged at 5.9.3, pure declaration cleanup with zero behaviour change; (B) bump lockfile to 6.0.3 and fix the dom-elements TS-6 cascade plus any other TS 5→6 deprecations. Picked Option A per the "stability and correctness > new features" guidance. Mechanical replacement across 201 `package.json` files (`git grep -l '"typescript": "\^6\.0\.3"' \| xargs sed -i …`); integration packages untouched (their `^5.x` pins are intentional and already carved out by CI's `--exclude-workspace '@gjsify/integration-*'` glob). Post-cleanup: `gjsify install` regenerated lockfile (still resolves to 5.9.3 at the root — only `@ts-for-gir/language-server` retains a nested `6.0.3` as a transitive resolution, unrelated to workspace decls); `gjsify upgrade --check --exclude-workspace '@gjsify/integration-*'` clean (96 deps consistently declared); `gjsify run check` workspace-wide tsc green (after rebuilding `@gjsify/npm-registry` `lib/` — the `whoami` export from PR #390 was source-only on main, build artifacts caught up here). AGENTS.md `## Constraints` gained a TypeScript-version-invariant paragraph documenting the workspace-wide range, the integration carve-out, and the procedure for future bumps (update decls + verify lockfile + run check in the same PR).

"polyfill"` so the declared triplet matches the actual signal-based heuristic. The `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` heuristic was upgraded in lockstep to recognise the four new equivalence classes: `WEB_API_NODE_NATIVE` (the 8 R2 §5.1 packages), `WEB_API_NODE_NONE` (the 4 R2 §5.2 packages — browser-only Web APIs with no Node pendant), `NODE_API_BROWSER_NATIVE` (`url` + `perf_hooks` — node-api packages whose Node-native value IS the browser-native value via `globalThis`), `NODE_API_NO_BROWSER_SENSE` (`cluster`/`inspector`/`readline` + `child_process`/`dgram`/`fs`/`net`/`tls`/`v8`/`globals`), `NODE_API_GJS_ONLY` (the native-bridge prebuilds `tls-native`/`sab-native`/`terminal-native`/`http2-native`/`http-soup-bridge`), and `NODE_API_LEGACY_NONE` (`process` — pre-existing, deferred to a separate PR). `audit-runtimes --check` ends green at 79 declarable packages, 0 drift. `tsc --noEmit` clean across every modified package (`@gjsify/url`, `@gjsify/perf_hooks`, `@gjsify/path`, `@gjsify/cluster`, `@gjsify/inspector`, `@gjsify/readline`, `@gjsify/dom-elements`, `@gjsify/canvas2d-core`, `@gjsify/abort-controller`, `@gjsify/dom-exception`, `@gjsify/formdata`, `@gjsify/message-channel`, `@gjsify/webassembly`, `@gjsify/webcrypto`, `@gjsify/web-streams`, `@gjsify/compression-streams`, `@gjsify/gamepad`, `@gjsify/webaudio`, `@gjsify/webrtc`, `@gjsify/xmlhttprequest`). **Deferred to follow-up PR** (R2 §6 routing-mechanik for node-api packages whose `globals.mjs` re-exports `node:<pkg>` — that path can't bundle into a browser build): `@gjsify/console` browser-slot lift (heutiges `globals.mjs` ist `export * from 'node:console'` — refactor to `globalThis.console`-Pfad with `Console`-Klassen-Fallback notwendig); `@gjsify/timers` browser-slot lift (`setImmediate` fehlt im Browser, müsste partial werden); `@gjsify/process` node-Slot lift (Slot bleibt `node:"none"` mit Heuristik-Akzeptanz via `NODE_API_LEGACY_NONE`); `@gjsify/crypto` partial/partial (R2 §6.2 — separate PR mit cross-runtime `node:crypto` + `globalThis.crypto.subtle` Hybrid-Re-Export).
