Default `--check` unchanged. (2) `packages/infra/resolve-npm/lib/index.mjs` exports the 56-entry `ALIASES_NODE_FOR_BROWSER` table (Node built-ins + `node-fetch` / `ws` / `isomorphic-ws` → `@gjsify/<X>` or `@gjsify/empty`); unwired (PR-D flips `app/browser.ts` once R1 wave is in). (3) `tests/browser/scripts/discover-bundles.mjs` joins `packages/node` to the existing `packages/{web,dom}` discovery; each `BundleInfo` now carries a `pillar` field used in the Playwright spec title prefix.

- ~~**`pre-commit` git hook — auto-rebuild + auto-stage stale `@gjsify/{cli,tsc}` GJS bundles**~~✓ — Removes a recurring contributor footgun. `.github/workflows/main.yml` runs `gjs -m packages/infra/{cli,tsc}/dist/<bundle>.gjs.mjs --version` against both committed GJS bundles and fails CI if the reported version does not match `packages/infra/cli/package.json` (CLI) or `TYPESCRIPT_VERSION` in `packages/infra/tsc/src/index.ts` (tsc). The freshness gate has been red on multiple PRs in the recent wave (#385, #389, #390, #391, #399, #400 …) — every red run wastes ~90 min CI minutes × 2 platforms, and new contributors hit it the first time they edit anything under `packages/infra/{cli,tsc}/src/`. **Fix:** a plain `.githooks/pre-commit` bash hook (no husky/lefthook dep) detects staged changes under `packages/infra/cli/src/` + `packages/infra/cli/package.json` / `packages/infra/tsc/src/` + `packages/infra/tsc/package.json` via `git diff --cached --name-only --diff-filter=ACMR --`, rebuilds the matching bundle(s) (`gjsify workspace @gjsify/cli build && build:gjs-bundle` for CLI; `gjsify workspace @gjsify/tsc build` for tsc), and re-stages the refreshed `dist/<bundle>.gjs.mjs` so the contributor's commit transparently includes the bundle update. Resolves `gjsify` via three fallbacks (workspace-local `node_modules/.bin/gjsify` → PATH → committed `cli.gjs.mjs` GJS bundle) so the hook still works in a freshly-cloned repo and across the bootstrap CLI's various install layouts. **Bypass:** standard `git commit --no-verify` plus a `SKIP_GJSIFY_HOOKS=1` env var (for CI / scripted commits that must not rebuild). **Install:** new `scripts/install-git-hooks.mjs` sets `core.hooksPath=.githooks` (idempotent, no-op when already set; `--uninstall` restores the git default; `--quiet` suppresses success log). Auto-installed by `gjsify install` via a new `maybeInstallGitHooks()` post-check in `packages/infra/cli/src/commands/install.ts` — gated on `existsSync(scripts/install-git-hooks.mjs) && existsSync(.git)` so it only fires inside the gjsify monorepo itself (consumer projects with `@gjsify/cli` as a dep are skipped silently). New e2e suite `tests/e2e/git-hooks-cli-bundle-staleness/run.mjs` (3 tests) validates: (1) the hook is a no-op for unrelated staged changes, (2) it auto-rebuilds + auto-stages `dist/cli.gjs.mjs` when `packages/infra/cli/src/` is touched, (3) `SKIP_GJSIFY_HOOKS=1` skips the rebuild path. AGENTS.md `## Git hooks` section added; `package.json#scripts.install-git-hooks` exposed for manual invocation.
- ~~**Browser-polyfill refs added (9 submodules under `refs/`) + Source Attribution rows**~~✓ — Welle 2-S of the cross-runtime expansion plan vendors the 9 MUST-ADD browser-polyfill reference submodules per `.gjsify-submodule-plan-S.md` §1: `refs/node-stdlib-browser` (canonical aggregator + npm-name → browser-polyfill mapping table), `refs/path-browserify` (drop-in `node:path` polyfill), `refs/process` (defunctzombie/node-process — canonical browser `process` shim), `refs/stream-browserify` (slim `node:stream` re-export wrapper companion to `refs/readable-stream/`), `refs/buffer-browserify` (feross/buffer — industry-standard `Buffer` class), `refs/pako` (zlib in pure JS, de-facto for `browserify-zlib`), `refs/browserify-zlib` (`node:zlib`-compatible streaming wrapper around pako), `refs/memfs` (in-memory Node `fs` impl), `refs/wa-sqlite` (WebAssembly SQLite with modular OPFS/IndexedDB/Memory VFS layers). Source: Welle-1 audits R1 (browser polyfills) / S (submodule plan) / C (credits inventory). Source Attribution table in `AGENTS.md` (+ symlinked `CLAUDE.md`) gains the 9 corresponding pipe-rows; `## References — refs/` gains a new `### Browser polyfills` subsection with one-line purpose blurbs per submodule. Unblocks the upcoming Browser-polyfill wave that needs to consult these as references for `@gjsify/{path,buffer,stream,zlib,fs,sqlite,process}` on the Web axis.

- ~~**`@gjsify/zlib` Zstd stubs for undici v7 (unblocker)**~~✓ — Closes the `Open TODOs > Medium priority — @gjsify/zlib Zstd stubs for undici v7` follow-up. undici@7's runtime-feature detector reads `zlib.createZstdDecompress` at module-init (Node 23.8+ Zstd API, [nodejs/node#56777](https://github.com/nodejs/node/pull/56777)); without the export, `import undici` throws under GJS even when no Zstd response ever arrives. Added 6 named exports mirroring the existing brotli stub pattern: `createZstdCompress` / `createZstdDecompress` (in `packages/node/zlib/src/transform-streams.ts`) + `zstdCompress` / `zstdDecompress` (callback form) + `zstdCompressSync` / `zstdDecompressSync` (sync form) in `packages/node/zlib/src/index.ts`. Every stub throws (or callback-emits) an `Error` tagged `code: 'ERR_UNSUPPORTED_OPERATION'` — matching Node's behavior on a build without Zstd, so any conditional consumer code that checks the error code routes through the same "no zstd available" branch as on Node. Also wired into the default export object (`zlib.createZstdDecompress` etc.) so the CJS-shape consumers (undici included) find the symbol. 7 new mock-driven tests in `packages/node/zlib/src/index.spec.ts` under a new `Zstd stubs` describe block: named-export presence, every entry-point throws `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_OPERATION` (sync direct, async via callback), default-export-completeness. tsc `--noEmit` clean. Real Zstd would land via `Gio.ZlibCompressor`'s sibling APIs in glib-networking 2.84+ or a libzstd-1.5 Vala prebuild — neither is on the immediate horizon (no GTK ecosystem ships Content-Encoding: zstd today).
- ~~**`gjsify install` — per-fetch + overall timeouts (no more silent hangs on slow CDN)**~~✓ — Closes the root cause of an observed `gjsify install` hang where a reachable-but-slow npm registry CDN (TCP connect OK, response body never finishes) caused a single packument fetch to await indefinitely — 10+ min, 0% CPU, STAT `Ss` = sleeping on an await that never settles, no error message, no progress. Two layers of timeout coverage now bound the worst case: (1) **Per-request timeout in `@gjsify/npm-registry`** — `FetchOptions.timeoutMs` (default 30000 ms, set 0 to disable) added to `fetchWithRetry`; each attempt gets a fresh `AbortController` whose `setTimeout` fires after `timeoutMs` and aborts the in-flight `fetch`. `composeSignals(callerSignal, timeoutSignal)` via `AbortSignal.any` propagates BOTH to the fetch impl (caller signal kept distinct from our timeout signal so the two can be told apart on the catch path). When the fetch throws, we classify via signal state — `timeoutController.signal.aborted && !init.signal?.aborted` → treat as transient and continue the retry loop (slow CDN recovers); `init.signal?.aborted` → propagate as canonical `AbortError` (caller wants out, no retry); anything else → existing `isRetryableError` path. State-based classification (rather than `err.name === 'AbortError'`) is required because GJS's Soup-backed fetch surfaces abort-triggered errors as plain `Error` with no `AbortError` name marker — a name-only test fails the GJS retry path. When ALL retries exhaust because of per-request timeouts, a new typed `RegistryTimeoutError extends Error` is thrown with a clear message: `@gjsify/npm-registry: GET <url> timed out after Xs × N attempt(s) (total ~Ys). This usually means the registry CDN is slow or unreachable.` — replaces the inscrutable `signal is aborted without reason` the raw `AbortSignal` path would otherwise surface. `fetchPackument` + `fetchTarball` thread `opts.timeoutMs` (and `opts.signal`) through. (2) **Overall install timeout in the CLI** — new `gjsify install --timeout <ms>` flag (default 300000 ms = 5 min wall-clock; set 0 to disable). `installCommand`'s handler now wraps the native install path in an `AbortController` + matching `setTimeout(abort, args.timeout)`; the signal threads through `InstallOptions.signal` → `installPackagesNative` → `resolveDeps` (every `fetchPackument` call) AND `downloadAndExtractAll` → `extractOne` (every `fetchTarball` call). On timeout, every in-flight registry fetch aborts in unison; the handler catches the surfacing error, recognises it via `isAbortedFromOverallTimeout` (name===AbortError | message includes `install-overall-timeout` | name===RegistryTimeoutError), prints `gjsify install: timed out after Xs — likely a registry slowdown. Re-run, or override with --timeout <ms> (set --timeout 0 to disable the overall budget).` and exits 1. The two layers compose: a single fetch can retry up to 4 times within the 30s per-request budget; the overall 5 min budget caps the worst case across every fetch in the install. Coverage: 6 new mock-driven tests in `packages/infra/npm-registry/src/timeout.spec.ts` (fast → success / persistent timeout → RegistryTimeoutError × 3 attempts, message check / timeout-then-success retry / timeoutMs:0 disable / caller-abort vs timeout disambiguation / default-30s sanity) wired into `test.mts`; 1 new e2e suite `tests/e2e/install-timeout/run.mjs` that runs the real `gjsify install --timeout 800` against a slow mock-registry that never finishes the response body, asserts non-zero exit < 10s with a `/timed out|RegistryTimeoutError/i` message and an empty `node_modules/`. npm-registry test count: 62 → 68 on both Node + GJS; CLI bundle (`packages/infra/cli/dist/cli.gjs.mjs`) refreshed in the same commit.
- ~~**`gjsify whoami` — npm-registry auth verification subcommand**~~✓ — Surfaces the `whoami(registry, npmrc)` helper from `@gjsify/npm-registry` (introduced as the `gjsify publish` 404-diagnostic backbone in PR #390) as a first-class CLI command. Goal: maintainers verify `~/.npmrc` auth via `gjsify whoami` instead of the `curl + Authorization: Bearer …` dance documented in AGENTS.md. Four observable branches: live token → `Logged in as: <username>` / `Registry: <url>` on stdout, exit 0; dead/revoked token → clear "token appears dead or revoked (registry returned an empty whoami response)" message on stderr with the `npm login` refresh path + an AGENTS.md "New @gjsify/* package: first-publish + Trusted Publisher bootstrap" pointer, exit 1; no `_authToken` configured at all → "no npm token configured" message on stderr with the `npm login` install path, exit 1, never hits the network; network error / non-2xx / non-JSON body → the underlying error message + registry URL on stderr, exit 1. Optional `--json` emits a single-line `{"username": "...", "registry": "..."}` (live) / `{"error": "dead-token"|"no-token-configured"|"<network-message>", "registry": "..."}` (failure) instead of free-form text — useful for CI scripts that gate behavior on `username`. Optional `--registry <url>` overrides the scope-aware registry lookup from the parsed npmrc (account-scoped, not package-scoped — `whoami` verifies the user's *default* registry). New shared util `packages/infra/cli/src/utils/load-npmrc.ts` (`loadNpmrc(cwd)`, `hasAnyAuthToken(npmrc)`, `hasAnyCredential(npmrc)`) extracted from `publish.ts`'s local copy; both `publish` and `whoami` now share one npmrc-resolution path that mirrors npm CLI's `globalconfig → userconfig → projectconfig` precedence + `${VAR}` env-expansion (so `actions/setup-node`'s `$NPM_CONFIG_USERCONFIG` writes still reach CI publishes). 9 mock-driven tests in `packages/infra/cli/src/whoami-command.spec.ts` covering all four branches × text + `--json` (live token text + JSON, dead token text + JSON, no-token text + JSON, network error text + JSON, registry 401 → branch-4). AGENTS.md `## Build` block updated with the new command; AGENTS.md "New @gjsify/* package: first-publish + Trusted Publisher bootstrap" updated with a `gjsify whoami` pre-flight check before the publish PUT (curl one-liner retained as a fallback for environments where `gjsify` isn't on PATH yet).

- ~~**`gjsify workspace <name> <script> --with-dependencies` — topological pre-build of transitive workspace deps**~~✓ — Closes the single biggest local-dev pain point in this monorepo: `gjsify workspace @gjsify/<pkg> build` cascade-failing N times in a row as each intermediate workspace dep's `lib/` is missing. New `--with-dependencies` flag (short alias `-d`, foreach-style synonyms `-t` / `--topological`) on `gjsify workspace` walks the target package's transitive workspace-dep closure via a DFS over `dependencies` + `optionalDependencies` whose specifier starts with `workspace:` (plus `devDependencies` under `--include-dev`), feeds the closure into the shared `@gjsify/workspace`'s `buildDependencyGraph` + `topologicalSort` (same Kahn's-algorithm code path that drives `gjsify foreach -t`), then runs the same script in each dep in dependency-order, finally in the target itself. Intermediate deps that don't declare the script are skipped silently (`--if-present` semantics — mirrors `gjsify foreach`'s script-presence filter). Default is stop-on-first-failure; `--continue-on-error` keeps processing the remaining list and exits 1 with a summary of failed workspaces. Without the flag the command behaves exactly as before (no surprises for existing users — the original `runOne(target)` path is preserved verbatim). New e2e suite `tests/e2e/workspace-with-dependencies/` builds a synthetic 5-workspace monorepo with a 4-deep transitive chain (`utils` ← `core` ← `plugin` ← `cli`) + an unrelated 5th workspace, then asserts (1) without the flag only the target builds (regression guard), (2) `--with-dependencies` builds the entire closure in topological order (mark-file mtimes), (3) the `-d` short alias works identically, (4) `--topological` / `-t` are documented synonyms, (5) deps without the script are skipped, (6) a failing intermediate dep stops the cascade with non-zero exit, (7) `--continue-on-error` keeps going past a failed dep, (8) missing-workspace errors stay clear. The 5th workspace (`@test/unrelated`) explicitly verifies that only the transitive closure is touched — not every workspace declaring the script. Replaces the manual `gjsify workspace A build && gjsify workspace B build && …` chains in the root `build:infra` script's idiom going forward; the existing `build:infra` line is NOT modified in this PR because it mixes `build` and `build:types` calls in the same chain — a non-trivial cleanup that requires extracting per-script orderings (separate follow-up). CLI bundle refreshed in the same commit (CI version-check + bundle staleness check rely on it). 8/8 e2e tests green; lint clean; tsc `--noEmit` clean.
- ~~**`gjsify publish` — dead-token-vs-missing-package diagnostic on `404 Not Found`**~~✓ — Closes a UX gap where a stale `_authToken` in `~/.npmrc` would surface as an opaque `404 Not Found` and send the maintainer down the wrong path (Trusted-Publisher-bootstrap setup) instead of refreshing the token. `gjsify publish` now probes `GET /-/whoami` with the same Authorization header whenever the publish PUT returns 404 with body `"Not Found"` / empty / `{"error":"Not Found"}`. The probe disambiguates: body `{}` (status 200) → the token is revoked/expired, emit a clear `npm login` hint plus the curl verification snippet; body `{"username": "..."}` → the package genuinely doesn't exist yet, point at the AGENTS.md first-publish-bootstrap section + `npm access ls-packages`. Diagnostic kicks in only on the plain token-auth PUT signature — skipped under `--otp` (the 401 EOTP path has its own clear surface) and `--trusted`/OIDC (those have dedicated error shapes in `OidcExchangeError`). Best-effort: any network failure / non-2xx whoami / non-JSON body falls back to the existing generic error message. New helper module `packages/infra/cli/src/utils/publish-diagnose.ts` (`diagnose404` + `is404DiagnosticCandidate`), backed by a new `whoami(registry, npmrc)` exported from `@gjsify/npm-registry` (reusable for a future `gjsify whoami` command). 16 mock-driven tests in `packages/infra/cli/src/publish-diagnose.spec.ts` (dead-token reason, live-token reason, fetch-throws fallback, registry trailing-slash handling, OIDC path skip).

- ~~**`@gjsify/cli` GJS-bundle cross-cwd `rolldown` resolve + `@gjsify/rolldown-plugin-gjsify` force-inlined `/register` subpaths for `--app gjs`**~~✓ — Two related root-cause fixes against the GJS-bundled CLI's `--app gjs` build path. (1) **Multi-anchor npm-package resolution.** `loadNpmRolldown` (and the new `tryLoadNative` fallback) previously did a bare `await import('rolldown')` / `createRequire(import.meta.url).resolve('@gjsify/rolldown-native')`. GJS's native ESM loader has no node_modules walker, and the bundle-URL anchor walks only ONE parent chain — when the bundle is invoked from a cwd OUTSIDE the install dir (e.g. `gjs -m /opt/gjsify/dist/cli.gjs.mjs build … --app gjs` from `/tmp/sandbox/` whose own `node_modules` is the only place rolldown lives), the lookup throws `ImportError: Module not found: rolldown`. New helper `packages/infra/cli/src/utils/resolve-npm-package.ts` tries multiple createRequire anchors in priority order — `GJSIFY_NODE_PATH` (NODE_PATH-like env override) → caller cwd → workspace root (`findWorkspaceRoot`) → bundle URL → parent-dir walk capped at 8 levels. First hit wins; returns null when every anchor misses (caller falls back to the bare specifier so the host runtime's native error path surfaces). Wired into `bundler-pick.ts` `loadNpmRolldown`, `runWatch`'s inline rolldown import, AND `tryLoadNative` for `@gjsify/rolldown-native`. Regression coverage: 6 new unit tests in `packages/infra/cli/src/utils/resolve-npm-package.spec.ts` (cwd / null-on-miss / bundleUrl fallback / GJSIFY_NODE_PATH-wins / parent-dir walk / malformed-specifier graceful return) + new e2e suite `tests/e2e/cli-bundle-cross-cwd-resolve/run.mjs` (3 tests against a synthetic install/cwd split). (2) **`@gjsify/<pkg>/register[/<feature>]` MUST NEVER be externalized for `--app gjs`.** New `isRegisterSubpath(id)` predicate in `packages/infra/rolldown-plugin-gjsify/src/app/gjs.ts` enforces the invariant in `setupForGjs`'s externals callback — it short-circuits to `false` (force-inline) before the user-external check fires, regardless of `bundler.external` from `package.json#gjsify`. Pattern-matches by SHAPE (`*/register` or `*/register/<feature>` source-specifier OR resolved on-disk `<pkg>/lib/esm/register/<feature>.js` path), so the carve-out scales to every package added by the tree-shakeable-globals convention without an explicit allow-list. Rationale: GJS's native ESM loader has no node_modules walker AND does not follow `package.json#exports` maps for bare specifiers, so an externalized `import '@gjsify/buffer/register/buffer'` throws `Module not found` at runtime even when `<pkg>/lib/esm/register/buffer.js` is physically on disk via the exports map — inlining is the only safe option until upstream GJS gains an exports-map-aware resolver. Predicate exported from `@gjsify/rolldown-plugin-gjsify` and regression-tested with 6 new specs in `packages/infra/cli/src/auto-globals.spec.ts` covering all four match shapes (bare specifier / fully-qualified / granular feature / resolved disk path) plus the negative-match cases (`unregister`, query-string suffixes, `cairo`, plain `@gjsify/<pkg>` root) that must still flow through the normal externals path. AGENTS.md `### Tree-shakeable globals — /register subpath convention` updated with a note documenting the invariant and the externals carve-out. CLI Node-side test suite: 165 tests pass (was 156); refreshed `packages/infra/cli/dist/cli.gjs.mjs` shipped alongside the source fix per the standard bundle-staleness CI gate.
- ~~**`@gjsify/tsc` — smoke tests + CI bundle-staleness check**~~✓ — Productionizes the just-shipped `@gjsify/tsc` package with the two pieces missing from the original PR #385. (1) New GJS-only smoke spec `packages/infra/tsc/src/index.gjs.spec.ts` exercises the committed `dist/tsc.gjs.mjs` bundle end-to-end via `Gio.Subprocess` (`gjs -m <bundle> …`): `--version` must equal `Version ${TYPESCRIPT_VERSION}`, `-p <tmpdir>` against a clean fixture (one `const x: number = 42;` line) must exit 0, and `-p <tmpdir>` against a buggy fixture (`const x: number = "string";`) must exit 2 with a `TS2322` diagnostic mentioning `bad.ts` on stdout. Fixtures are throwaway directories in `GLib.dir_make_tmp` populated via `GLib.file_set_contents` + cleaned up via `Gio.File.delete()` per spec. Test bundle assembled via `gjsify build src/test.mts --app gjs --outfile dist/test.gjs.mjs`; new `package.json` scripts mirror the standard workspace shape (`build:test:gjs`, `test:gjs`, `test`). (2) New CI step `Verify committed @gjsify/tsc GJS bundle is functional + up-to-date` in `.github/workflows/main.yml` — exact mirror of the pre-existing `@gjsify/cli` bundle staleness check, runs BEFORE the Build step so it tests the committed bundle (not a freshly-rebuilt one). Greps `TYPESCRIPT_VERSION = '<X>'` out of `packages/infra/tsc/src/index.ts`, diffs against `dist/tsc.gjs.mjs --version`'s `Version <X>` output, fails with a `::error::` group pointing the contributor at `gjsify workspace @gjsify/tsc build` if drift is found. Catches both "bundle was forgotten on a typescript devDep bump" and "constant was forgotten on a rebuild". Added `@gjsify/unit` as a devDependency of `@gjsify/tsc`.
- ~~**`gjsify tsc` CLI subcommand wiring @gjsify/tsc into the main CLI**~~✓ — New `gjsify tsc [tscArgs..]` subcommand in `@gjsify/cli` (`packages/infra/cli/src/commands/tsc.ts`) delegates to the just-released `@gjsify/tsc` bundle: resolves `@gjsify/tsc/bundle` via `createRequire(pathToFileURL(<workspace-root>/__gjsify_tsc__.js))` (workspace-root anchored, falls back to cwd via `findWorkspaceRoot()`), then spawns `gjs -m <bundlePath> [tscArgs..]` with `stdio: 'inherit'` and forwards the child's exit code (`process.exit(code ?? 1)`). Mirrors `gjsify run`'s native-env composition — auto-prepends `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` + `GI_TYPELIB_PATH` from `detectNativePackages(cwd)` + `buildNativeEnv()` so any future typescript-the-bundle code path that reaches a native bridge (today: none — tsc is pure JS) still finds the right typelibs. Equivalent to invoking the `gjsify-tsc` bin from the `@gjsify/tsc` package directly; this command just spares the user from discovering the bin path or remembering the bare package name. Missing-dep branch prints a clear install hint (`Install with: gjsify install --save-dev @gjsify/tsc`) and exits 1; missing-`gjs` branch prints a GJS install hint. `@gjsify/tsc` added as a workspace runtime `dependency` of `@gjsify/cli` (`package.json#dependencies`) so the resolve succeeds out of the box for any `gjsify install`-managed workspace. Registered in `commands/index.ts` + wired into the yargs chain in `src/index.ts` alongside `barrels`. CLI rebuilt; `packages/infra/cli/dist/cli.gjs.mjs` refreshed in the same commit (CI version-check + bundle staleness check both rely on it). VERIFIED: `gjsify tsc --version` → `Version 5.9.3` (matches the published bundle's pinned `TYPESCRIPT_VERSION`); `gjsify tsc -p <tsconfig>` exits 0 on a clean project; `gjsify run check` clean, `gjsify run lint` clean. The first user-visible wiring of the v0.4.37 `@gjsify/tsc` POC into the main CLI surface.
- ~~**`@gjsify/tsc` — TypeScript compiler running directly under GJS (Node-free `tsc` bin)**~~✓ — First proof-of-concept for the long-standing "Node-free build chain" goal. New `packages/infra/tsc/` package (`@gjsify/tsc`) bundles upstream `typescript@5.9.3`'s `lib/_tsc.js` CLI entry to a single 3.4 MiB GJS module via `gjsify build --app gjs` and ships it as the `gjsify-tsc` bin. The bundle runs **directly under SpiderMonkey 140 via GJS** — no Node, no `npm run`, no subprocess detour: `gjsify-tsc --version` reports `Version 5.9.3`; `gjsify-tsc -p tsconfig.json` exits 0 on a clean project and exits 2 with proper `file(line,col): error TS<code>:` diagnostics on a buggy one; `gjsify-tsc --diagnostics` and `--extendedDiagnostics` produce the same Files/Lines/Identifiers/Symbols/Types/Memory/Parse/Bind/Check/Emit perf blocks tsc prints under Node (181 MB / 0.38 s for a standard project with `lib.d.ts`). Bundle is **committed** to `dist/tsc.gjs.mjs` and ships in the npm tarball via `files: ["dist/tsc.gjs.mjs"]` — same precedent as `packages/infra/cli/dist/cli.gjs.mjs`, re-included via a paired `!packages/infra/tsc/dist/tsc.gjs.mjs` exception in root `.gitignore`. Rebuild step is `gjsify workspace @gjsify/tsc build` → `scripts/build-bundle.mjs` runs `gjsify build node_modules/typescript/lib/_tsc.js --app gjs --shebang` using the workspace-local `node_modules/.bin/gjsify` Node-CLI (the global GJS-bundle CLI currently can't resolve `rolldown` when invoked from sub-packages — separate bug, tracked). `typescript` itself stays a `devDependency` of `@gjsify/tsc` only; the bundled output has no runtime `typescript` dep — consumers install `@gjsify/tsc` and immediately have a Node-free `tsc`. `package.json#gjsify.runtimes` declared `{gjs: "polyfill", node: "none", browser: "none"}` — strictly a GJS-only artifact; on Node, downstream uses upstream `typescript` directly. `TYPESCRIPT_VERSION = "5.9.3"` pinned in `src/index.ts` alongside a `TSC_BUNDLE_PATH` constant for programmatic consumers; the actual programmatic TypeScript Compiler API (`createProgram`/`createCompilerHost`) re-exports land in a future `./library` subpath built from `typescript/lib/typescript.js`. Polyfills auto-injected by `--globals auto`: the typescript dist `_tsc.js` reaches `fs`/`path`/`os`/`process`/`perf_hooks`/`util`/`child_process` — all covered by the existing `@gjsify/*` family. Source attribution: typescript-dist is Apache-2.0 (Microsoft Corporation); the gjsify wrapper code (the metadata stub + the build orchestrator + the README) is original gjsify code under MIT.
- ~~**`@gjsify/querystring` — per-concern split of the 1165-LoC `src/index.ts` monolith**~~✓ — Extracted the deno_std + Node.js `lib/querystring.js` port into 5 leaf modules under `packages/node/querystring/src/internal/`: **`tables.ts`** (745 LoC, the four pre-computed `Int8Array` / `string[]` lookup tables — `hexTable`, `isHexTable`, `noEscape`, `unhexTable`), **`encode.ts`** (101 LoC — `encodeStr`, `qsEscape`, `ERR_INVALID_URI`), **`decode.ts`** (82 LoC — `unescapeBuffer`, `qsUnescape`), **`parse.ts`** (255 LoC — `parse`, `ParseOptions`, file-local `charCodes` + `addKeyVal`), **`stringify.ts`** (122 LoC — `stringify`, `StringifyOptions`, `stringifyPrimitive` + the two `encodeStringified*` helpers). Dependency graph is a strict DAG: `tables` ← `encode` ← `stringify`, `tables` ← `decode` ← `parse`; no circular imports. `src/index.ts` reduced from **1165 → 68 LoC** — pure imports + named re-exports + the legacy `decode`/`encode`/`escape`/`unescape` aliases + the default-export object that matches `module.exports` shape for CJS consumers. Public API surface byte-identical (every spec import — `qs.parse`, `qs.stringify`, `qs.escape`, `qs.unescape`, `qs.unescapeBuffer`, `qs.decode`, `qs.encode`, `qs.ERR_INVALID_URI`, `ParsedUrlQuery` type — resolves through the same names). `tsc --noEmit` clean; spec untouched (63 tests across 16 describes); no `package.json#exports` change; no new external runtime deps. Mirrors the per-concern split shape proven in PR #309 (webgl2-rendering-context.ts → 4 method-group modules) — adapted for a procedural/util surface (named export groups, no `install*(proto)` install functions).
- ~~**`@gjsify/webgl` — per-concern split of `context/shader-program.ts` (1141 → 51 + 4 new modules)**~~✓ — Third slice of the long-running `@gjsify/webgl` per-concern split, sibling to PR #309 + PR #273 (which split `webgl2-rendering-context.ts`). Decomposes the 1141-LoC monolithic `packages/framework/webgl/src/ts/context/shader-program.ts` into four per-concern sub-modules under `packages/framework/webgl/src/ts/context/shader-program/`, using the same `install*Methods(proto)` pattern: **`shader-lifecycle.ts`** (10 methods, 274 LoC) — `createShader`/`deleteShader`/`compileShader`/`shaderSource`/`getShaderInfoLog`/`getShaderParameter`/`getShaderPrecisionFormat`/`getShaderSource` + the GLSL `_checkShaderSource` tokenizer pass (`glsl-tokenizer/string` import lands here) and the `_wrapShader` version/preamble injector (handles GLSL ES 1.0 vs 3.0 + GdkPaintable extension undefs); **`program-lifecycle.ts`** (14 methods, 300 LoC) — `createProgram`/`deleteProgram`/`useProgram`/`linkProgram`/`validateProgram`/`attachShader`/`detachShader`/`bindAttribLocation`/`getAttachedShaders`/`getProgramInfoLog`/`getProgramParameter` + the `_switchActiveProgram`/`_fixupLink`/`_deleteLinkable` helpers (the `MAX_UNIFORM_LENGTH`/`MAX_ATTRIBUTE_LENGTH` spec constants moved alongside `_fixupLink`/`bindAttribLocation` since they're only consumed by those methods); **`introspection.ts`** (5 methods, 240 LoC) — `getActiveAttrib`/`getActiveUniform`/`getAttribLocation`/`getUniformLocation`/`getUniform` (the `getUniformLocation` array-uniform branching block with cases A/B/C for `basename` / `basename[0]` / `basename[N>0]` access patterns stays intact); **`uniforms.ts`** (19 methods, 410 LoC) — the full `uniform[1234][fi]` scalar / `uniform[1234][fi]v` vector / `uniformMatrix[234]fv` square-matrix setter battery + the `_checkLocation`/`_checkLocationActive`/`_checkUniformValid`/`_checkUniformValueValid`/`_checkUniformMatrix` validation chain. Each module declares a typed `<Group>Methods` interface, augments `ShaderProgramMethods` via `declare module '../shader-program.js' { interface ShaderProgramMethods extends <Group>Methods {} }`, declares the methods object with `ThisType<WebGLContextBase>`, and exports an `install<Group>Methods(proto)` function. Base file `shader-program.ts` 1141 → 51 LoC — now just the aggregate empty `ShaderProgramMethods` interface (populated by the four `declare module` augmentations), the `declare module '../webgl-context-base.js' { interface WebGLContextBase extends ShaderProgramMethods {} }` link to the consuming class, and the composing `installShaderProgramMethods(proto)` that calls all four `install<Group>Methods(proto)` in order. Each `install<Group>Methods` is imported twice — once as a named import (for the call site) and once as a bare side-effect import (so tsc preserves the augmentation in the emitted `.d.ts`; same technique as the PR #309 + PR #273 splits and the canvas2d-core split in PR #262). No `private` field had to be demoted because the original file already used `_`-prefixed package-internal fields throughout (`_gl`, `_shaders`, `_programs`, `_activeProgram`, `_extensions`, `_textureUnits`, `_checkOwns`, `_checkWrapper`, `_saveError`, `_restoreError`, `_validGLSLIdentifier`, `_getGlslVersion`) — all reachable through the `this: WebGLContextBase` typing in every extracted method. Public surface preserved verbatim: `installShaderProgramMethods` + the `ShaderProgramMethods` type alias still ship from `context/shader-program.ts`; the four focused `install<Group>Methods` are also re-exported from the root so a consumer that wants to install one group without the rest can reach the focused entry points without traversing internal paths. `context/index.ts` (the barrel that wires every method group into `WebGLContextBase.prototype`) needed no changes. **Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean; 860 GJS webgl conformance tests green (identical to pre-split baseline).** With this PR the `@gjsify/webgl` framework's three monolithic context files are all split per-concern: the WebGL1 base (`webgl-context-base.ts` via `context/{state,buffer-binding,texture-management,framebuffer,shader-program,drawing}.ts`) and the WebGL2 subclass (`webgl2-rendering-context.ts` via `webgl2-context/{object-lifecycle,tex-image-3d,uniform,clear-buffer,instanced-and-framebuffer-ops}.ts`) and now the shader-program slab beneath WebGL1.
- ~~**`@gjsify/webgl` — per-concern split of `context/texture-management.ts` (941 → 61 + 5 modules)**~~✓ — Third slice in the long-running `@gjsify/webgl` per-concern splits, sibling to PR #309 (`webgl2-rendering-context.ts` 1245 → 866 + 4 modules) and PR #273 (the prior WebGL2 lifecycle slice). Same `install*Methods(proto)` pattern, applied this time to the largest remaining base-class method-group module — `packages/framework/webgl/src/ts/context/texture-management.ts` (941 LoC) — extracting it into five per-concern sibling modules under `context/texture-management/`: lifecycle (`lifecycle.ts`, 229 LoC — `createTexture` / `deleteTexture` / `activeTexture` / `bindTexture` / `pixelStorei` + the `_detachTextureFromAllFramebuffers` helper that detaches a doomed texture from every FBO that still references it), 2D texture upload (`tex-image.ts`, 335 LoC — `texImage2D` / `texSubImage2D` including the 6-arg-vs-9-arg / 7-arg-vs-9-arg overload dispatch, the `GdkPixbuf.Pixbuf` + `TexImageSource` source extraction, the `UNPACK_PREMULTIPLY_ALPHA_WEBGL` premultiplication step and the `UNPACK_FLIP_Y_WEBGL` row-reverse step), copy + compressed (`copy-and-compressed.ts`, 184 LoC — `copyTexImage2D` / `copyTexSubImage2D` / `compressedTexImage2D` / `compressedTexSubImage2D`), parameters (`parameters.ts`, 111 LoC — `texParameterf` / `texParameteri` / `getTexParameter` + the `_getTexParameterDirect` helper that bypasses the parameter-validation switch and reads straight from the native GL), and introspection (`introspection.ts`, 187 LoC — every `_`-prefixed helper the other modules reach for: `_getActiveTextureUnit` / `_getActiveTexture` / `_getTexImage` / `_checkTextureTarget` / `_validTextureTarget` / `_validCubeTarget` / `_verifyTextureCompleteness` / `_computePixelSize` / `_computeRowStride` / `_checkDimensions`). 28 methods totalling ~880 LoC moved out of the base file verbatim. Each new module declaration-merges its `<Group>Methods` interface into `WebGLContextBase` via `declare module '../../webgl-context-base.js' { ... }` and exports an `install<Group>Methods(proto)` function. Base file `texture-management.ts` 941 → 61 LoC — now a barrel + composite `installTextureManagementMethods` that fans out to all five installs, plus a re-exported aggregate `TextureManagementMethods` interface (extends all five) so existing imports of just `TextureManagementMethods` from `context/index.ts` keep compiling. Each per-concern install is imported twice — once as a named import for the call site and once as a bare side-effect import — so tsc preserves the augmentation in the emitted `.d.ts` (same technique as PR #309 / PR #273 / the canvas2d-core split in PR #262). `context/index.ts`'s `installAllContextMethods(WebGLContextBase.prototype)` aggregator is unchanged — it still imports the single `installTextureManagementMethods` from `texture-management.ts`, which now fans out internally. The non-trivial cross-module call graph is preserved verbatim: `texImage2D` / `texSubImage2D` reach into introspection (`_getTexImage` / `_computePixelSize` / `_computeRowStride` / `_checkDimensions`) and call `_updateFramebufferAttachments` from the base class; `texParameter{f,i}` / `getTexParameter` reach into introspection (`_checkTextureTarget` / `_verifyTextureCompleteness`); `_verifyTextureCompleteness` calls back into the lifecycle module's `bindTexture`; `deleteTexture` calls back into the lifecycle module's `_detachTextureFromAllFramebuffers`. tsc `--noEmit` clean across `@gjsify/webgl`. 860 GJS webgl conformance tests still green (no count change).

- ~~**`@gjsify/fs.rmSync` no longer wipes the target of a top-level symlink (workspace-source-wipe iteration 2)**~~✓ — Closes a SECOND failure mode of the [v0.4.29 install data-loss fix](#current-state-v0429-2026-05-27-install-data-loss-fix). The original PR addressed resolver-side leakage (workspace package fetched from npm and extracted over its own source); a re-install in a real consumer (`pixel-rpg/map-editor`) surfaced a different chain: every `gjsify install` after the very first one finds preexisting `node_modules/@scope/<pkg>` symlinks pointing into workspace source dirs, and the install command's `rmSync(linkPath, { recursive: true, force: true })` cleanup-before-resymlink in `workspaceInstall` (`packages/infra/cli/src/commands/install.ts:398`) descended INTO the link and deleted every file in the target. Root cause was the polyfill itself, not the caller: `@gjsify/fs.rmSync` (`packages/node/fs/src/sync.ts:593`) and `@gjsify/fs.promises.rm` (`packages/node/fs/src/promises.ts:565`) used `Gio.FileQueryInfoFlags.NONE` (stat — follows symlinks) at the top-level type probe, classified a symlink-to-directory as a directory, then walked the target's children into the recursive descent. Fix: probe with `Gio.FileQueryInfoFlags.NOFOLLOW_SYMLINKS`; if the top-level path is `Gio.FileType.SYMBOLIC_LINK`, remove the link as a single entry via `file.delete(null)` and return — never descend. Matches Node's `fs.rmSync` semantics (a top-level symlink-to-directory is one entry, target untouched). New regression suite `packages/node/fs/src/rm-symlink.spec.ts` with 5 tests across `rmSync` + `promises.rm` × symlink-to-dir / symlink-to-file / non-recursive-symlink / real-recursive-dir. Verified end-to-end against `pixel-rpg/map-editor`: `gjsify install` now preserves `packages/{engine,gjs,story-gjs}/src/` + `games/zelda-like/{maps,spritesets}` across re-installs. fs test count: 651 → 656.

- ~~**`@gjsify/tls-native` Phase 2 — Path-A native flip (TLS session resumption + channel binding, functional)**~~✓ — Closes the POC-scaffold landing from #342 by replacing the `_resolve_native_session()` throw with a real `gnutls_session_t` extraction. New C shim at `packages/node/tls-native/src/c/gjsify-tls-private.{c,h}` reaches into `GTlsConnectionGnutls`'s private struct (vendored layout from `refs/glib-networking/tls/gnutls/gtlsconnection-gnutls.c` @ `be1c870`, master at submodule-add) via the public GLib 2.38+ `g_type_instance_get_private(instance, gnutls_type)` + a runtime `g_type_from_name("GTlsConnectionGnutls")` lookup. The 4-pointer `{credentials, session, interaction_id, cancellable}` layout is stable across glib-networking 2.74–2.84 (covers Fedora 43 + 44). Force-loads the dynamic GIO TLS module via `g_tls_backend_get_client_connection_type()` on every entry point so the type registers even before any TLS connection is instantiated. C shim exposes a 6-call surface (`is_supported`, `is_gnutls_connection`, `is_session_reused`, `get_session_data`, `set_session_data`, `get_channel_binding`) wired into Vala via `gjsify-tls-private.vapi`; the `SessionAccess` class's per-method bodies now delegate (each previously-`_resolve_native_session()` site becomes a one-line try/catch wrapping the C call). `get_finished()` auto-selects between `tls-unique` (TLS ≤1.2, RFC 5929 §3) and `tls-exporter` (TLS 1.3, RFC 9266) based on `Gio.TlsConnection.get_protocol_version()`. `is_supported()` now returns `true` on glib-networking GnuTLS-backend runtimes (Fedora 43+ / gjs 1.86+); `hasTlsSessionAccess()` accordingly. Graceful degradation paths preserved cleanly: a hypothetical `GIO_USE_TLS=openssl` backend returns `is_supported() === false` and every `TLSSocket.{getFinished,getPeerFinished,getSession,setSession,isSessionReused}` accessor degrades to the same `undefined`/`false`/no-op contract Node uses on a build without session support. Backward compatibility: zero JS-side API changes from #342 — flipping the predicate flips every consumer transparently. Unit specs updated: `tls-native/src/ts/index.gjs.spec.ts` asserts `is_supported() === true` (11 assertions total, 5 SessionAccess); `tls/src/session-access.gjs.spec.ts` asserts `hasTlsSessionAccess() === true` and that pre-handshake getters still degrade to `undefined`/`false` (no `_tlsConnection` bound on a freshly-constructed `TLSSocket`). Refreshed `.so`/`.gir`/`.typelib` prebuilds (linux-x86_64). Real round-trip resumption + channel-binding-byte assertions deferred to a separate `tests/integration/tls-session/` suite landing once a fixture TLS server is in place. Both "Open TODOs → Medium priority — TLS gaps" Phase-2 entries are struck through; the `Workstream B` row in the same section is updated to advertise the now-functional Phase 2.

- ~~**`@gjsify/tls-native` Phase 2 scaffold — TLS session resumption + channel binding (POC, Path B)**~~✓ — `@gjsify/tls-native` ships Phase 2 as a POC scaffold (Path B per the planning doc — chosen because the worktree has no `refs/glib-networking/` submodule to read the `GTlsConnectionGnutlsPrivate` struct layout from). New Vala class `GjsifyTls.SessionAccess` (`packages/node/tls-native/src/vala/session-access.vala`) wraps a `Gio.TlsConnection` and exposes the same shape the real impl will use: `is_supported() → bool`, `for_connection(conn) → SessionAccess?`, `is_session_reused()`, `get_session_data()`, `set_session_data(bytes)`, `get_channel_binding(type)`, `get_finished()` (Node alias for `tls-unique` / TLS 1.3 `tls-exporter`), `get_peer_finished()`, plus `get_negotiated_protocol_version()` which IS functional today (reads `Gio.TlsConnection.get_protocol_version()`). The gnutls-bound methods all funnel through one `_resolve_native_session()` private method which throws `SessionAccessError.NOT_SUPPORTED` — flipping that one method's body (the documented ~50-line change) makes EVERY surface method functional with zero JS-side changes. Sibling vapi `gnutls-session.vapi` fills the only missing piece in Vala 0.56's bundled `gnutls.vapi` — the `gnutls_session_channel_binding` declaration + `ChannelBinding` enum (`TLS_UNIQUE` / `TLS_SERVER_END_POINT` / `TLS_EXPORTER`). `meson.build` updated to include the new sources + the new vapi + `gio-2.0` dep. The JS-side surface is fully wired into `@gjsify/tls`: new `session-access.ts` re-exports `hasTlsSessionAccess()` (gates on `SessionAccess.is_supported()`; returns `false` today) and `TlsChannelBindingType`; `TLSSocket` gains `getFinished()` / `getPeerFinished()` / `getSession()` / `setSession()` / `isSessionReused()` (all degrade to `undefined` / `false` / no-op when the bridge isn't functional, matching Node's behavior on a build without session support); `connect()` accepts the new `{session}` option (forwards to `setSession()` before handshake, no-op when unavailable) and emits the `'session'` event post-handshake (gated on `hasTlsSessionAccess()`). Surface tests lock the contract in BEFORE the native flip — `index.gjs.spec.ts` gains 5 `SessionAccess` assertions (typelib loads, predicate is `false` today, `for_connection(null)` returns `null`, `TlsChannelBindingType` constants), and new `session-access.gjs.spec.ts` adds 13 surface assertions covering every Phase 2 public method on `TLSSocket`. The full research note + acceptance criteria for closing the POC (struct-layout vendor, real round-trip test in `tests/integration/tls-session/`, STATUS.md strike) lives at `docs/poc/tls-phase2-session-access.md`. The two "Open TODOs" entries `TLS session resumption` and `getPeerFinished() / getFinished()` now point at the POC's partial-unblocker status instead of "blocked"; the blocker has narrowed to the single struct-layout question. (Path B chosen over Path A because `ls refs/ | grep -i networking` returned empty; adding the submodule + flipping the native impl is the natural Phase-2.1 follow-up.)

- ~~**typescript/no-explicit-any → 0 in 8 small web packages (`abort-controller`, `dom-events`, `websocket`, `eventsource`, `message-channel`, `fetch`, `webstorage`, `xmlhttprequest`)**~~✓ — 63 `no-explicit-any` warnings eliminated (no-any wave 2b, small-web batch). `abort-controller` (10): 5 source `any`s on `AbortSignal.reason`/`onabort`/`[kAbort]`/`AbortSignal.abort`/`AbortController.abort` reason params + the `static abort(reason?: any)` shape ship under ONE file-level `oxlint-disable` with a tailored "W3C lib.dom spec mirror — abort reason is intentionally an arbitrary JS value" reason (`reason: any` is the literal lib.dom signature, not a stylistic choice); 5 spec casts `(AbortSignal as any)()` / `(signal as any).onabort` / `(signal as any).__proto__` / `(controller.signal as any).onabort` rewritten to typed structural / direct-property reads since our impl already exposes the W3C-public fields. `dom-events` (9): 4 W3C-default generics — `CustomEvent<T = any>`, `CustomEventInit<T = any>`, `MessageEvent<T = any>`, `MessageEventInit<T = any>` — match lib.dom verbatim and ship under ONE file-level disable that names the four types (narrowing to `unknown` would break unparameterized `new CustomEvent(...)` for consumers); 2 register-module `(globalThis as any)[name]` reads narrowed to `Record<string, unknown>` casts (`custom-events.ts`, `ui-events.ts`); 1 spec `consoleError` spy widened `any[]` → `unknown[]`; 2 deliberate-empty-object `target.addEventListener('foo', f as any)` registrations cast through `f as unknown as (evt: Event) => void` since `{}` is not assignable to `EventListenerObject` until `handleEvent` is added. `websocket` (9, spec-only): 4 `server.get_listeners()[0].get_local_address() as any` GI introspection narrowed to `Gio.InetSocketAddress` (the actual subtype that has `get_port()`); 4 `event: any` on `ws.onmessage`/`ws.onclose` typed via the W3C `MessageEvent<string>`/`MessageEvent<ArrayBuffer>`/`CloseEvent` from `@gjsify/dom-events`; 1 `ev: any` on the handshake-timeout 'error' listener typed via the impl-augmented `Event & { error?: Error; message?: string }` shape, with a final `Parameters<typeof ws.addEventListener>[1]` cast to bridge the global `Event` (from `@types/node` web-globals) vs dom-events `Event` nominal-identity gap surfaced by the clean type-check. `eventsource` (6): 3 source `any`s on `EventSource.{onopen,onmessage,onerror}` match lib.dom verbatim (`((this: EventSource, ev: Event) => any) | null`) and ship under ONE file-level disable; 3 spec `req: any`/`res: any`/`server.address() as any` rewritten in `index.spec.ts` to a typed `HttpHandler = (req: InstanceType<typeof IncomingMessage>, res: InstanceType<typeof ServerResponse>) => void` alias + an `AddressInfo` narrow on the SSE-server helper (string-address case rejected up-front). `message-channel` (5, spec-only): every `(e as any).data` in the cross-platform `MessagePort` test cast to `(e as { data: unknown }).data` — the smallest structural cast that compiles on both Node and GJS without dragging in dom-events `MessageEvent` typing (the spec covers the IDL surface, not the event subclass). `fetch` (5): 1 source `referrer.ts` `new URL(url) as any` simplified to just `new URL(url)` (URL's `username`/`password`/`hash`/`pathname`/`search` are all writable in lib.dom, the cast was unneeded); 1 GJS-only spec `(h as any).raw()` Headers-extension cast to `Headers & { raw: () => Record<string, string[]> }`; 3 XHR-fixture casts in `index.spec.ts` retyped to the W3C `XMLHttpRequest` from lib.dom — `XHR = (globalThis as { XMLHttpRequest: typeof XMLHttpRequest }).XMLHttpRequest`, `init: (xhr: XMLHttpRequest) => void`, `new Promise<XMLHttpRequest>(...)`. `webstorage` (3, spec-only): three `s.setItem('num', 42 as any)` deliberate-non-string-coercion tests retyped to `as unknown as string` with a "Storage.setItem coerces any value to string per spec" comment (the test exercises the spec-mandated coercion path). `xmlhttprequest` (16, all in `index.ts`): every `any` matches the W3C XHR surface — `response: any` matches lib.dom verbatim (the actual response shape varies by `responseType`: text/json/arraybuffer/blob); the 8 `on{load,error,progress,loadstart,loadend,abort,timeout,readystatechange}: ((e: any) => void) | null` handler signatures match lib.dom (the impl dispatches plain progress-shaped objects, not `ProgressEvent` instances); `send(_body?: any)`, `fetch(url).then((r: any) =>` (Soup boundary), `_listeners: Record<string, Array<(e: any) => void>>`, `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` callback `any`, and `_emit(event: Record<string, any>)`'s varying-shape payload — all carried under ONE file-level disable with a long tailored reason naming the W3C `any` surface and the GI/Soup introspection boundary. No public W3C type narrowed to `unknown` (CRITICAL SAFETY — the #334/#336 cascade broke main on `HTMLMediaElement.srcObject` / `Spy.ThrownCall.throw` narrowings), no `.oxlintrc.json` change, no test assertions weakened. VERIFIED in-worktree: each of the 8 packages `oxlint -c .oxlintrc.json packages/web/<pkg>/src` → 0 `no-explicit-any`, `gjsify workspace @gjsify/<pkg> check` → 0 real `tsc` errors (excluding `Cannot find module '@gjsify/...'` worktree build-ordering artifacts). Repo-wide `no-explicit-any`: ~538 → ~475.
- ~~**#336 over-narrowing broke the `check:examples` step + stale CLI GJS bundle (un-red `main`, full CI sweep)**~~✓ — The prior repair PRs (#334 build, #340 `check`/integration-tests) fixed earlier CI layers but never ran `check:examples` or the CLI-bundle freshness check locally, so two more CI gates stayed red on `main`. This PR ran the COMPLETE CI sequence (clean `build` → `build:examples` → `check` → `check:examples` → CLI bundle version-check → `lint`) locally and fixed every remaining error. **4 `tsc --noEmit` errors in the `check:examples` step**, all #336 over-narrowing in `examples/`/`showcases/` source: **(1)** `showcases/dom/excalibur-jelly-jumper/src/classes/level-scene.ts:335` — `makeSpawner`'s `spawn` callback return narrowed to `unknown`, but `EnemySpawner.spawn` requires `(pos) => EnemyActor` (TS2740, 130+ missing props). RESTORED to the concrete `EnemyActor` (added `import type { EnemyActor }`); the lambdas already return `SpiderEnemy`/`BirdEnemy` which extend it. **(2)** `showcases/dom/three-geometry-teapot/src/browser/browser.ts:280` — `(demo.effectController as Record<string, unknown>)[key]` (TS2352, non-overlapping cast). RESTORED type-safely: typed the toggle-row tuple as `Array<[AdwRow, 'lid'|'body'|'bottom'|'fitLid'|'nonblinn']>` so `key` is a known `boolean` key of `TeapotEffectController`, then assigned `demo.effectController[key] = Boolean(row.active)` directly — no cast. **(3,4)** `examples/node/net-express-hello/src/index.spec.ts:78,84` — `body.greeting`/`body.time` accessed on the helper's `body: unknown` (TS2339); #336 narrowed the helper return to `unknown` and added `(body as Record<string, unknown>).X` casts on one of three call sites but missed two. NARROWED-at-consumer consistently (`(body as Record<string, unknown>).greeting`/`.time`). No `any` reintroduced, no public type narrowed to `unknown`, no `.oxlintrc.json` change, no assertions weakened. Also **refreshed the committed CLI GJS bundle** (`packages/infra/cli/dist/cli.gjs.mjs`) — the version-check step (bundle `--version` must equal `package.json`) was failing on a stale bundle; rebuilt via `@gjsify/cli build && build:gjs-bundle`, now reports v0.4.37 == package.json. VERIFIED airtight, all run locally with 0 errors: clean full `build` (all `lib/`+`*.tsbuildinfo` nuked, `resolve-npm/lib` preserved), `build:examples`, `check`, `check:examples`, CLI-bundle version-check, `gjsify lint` (0 errors; pre-existing no-explicit-any *warnings* unchanged); e2e `run-command`/`self-host` green (confirm the refreshed bundle is functional).
- ~~**#334 `no-explicit-any → unknown` over-narrowing broke the `build` step (CONSUMERS)**~~✓ — #334 narrowed two PUBLIC type surfaces that real consumers depend on, reding the CI `build` step (incremental builds masked it by reusing stale pre-#334 `.d.ts`; only a clean full build surfaces it). **(1) `@gjsify/dom-elements` `HTMLMediaElement.srcObject` `any → unknown`** broke `@gjsify/video`'s `VideoBridge._onSrcObjectChange` (`stream.getVideoTracks?.()` on `unknown`). `srcObject` is a real public DOM property — RESTORED to a concrete type by adding a structural `MediaProvider` interface (`getVideoTracks?`/`getAudioTracks?`/`getTracks?`) in `html-media-element.ts` and typing `srcObject: MediaProvider | null` (dom-elements builds without the DOM lib, so the spec's `MediaStream | MediaSource | Blob` is modelled structurally; a concrete `MediaStream` from `getUserMedia` still structurally satisfies it). **(2) `@gjsify/unit` `Spy.ThrownCall.throw` `any → unknown`** broke `dom-events/src/event-target.spec.ts:429-430` (`f.calls[0].throw.name/.code`). A thrown value IS genuinely `unknown` — kept `unknown` and NARROWED at the (single) consumer with `(… as { name: string; code: number })`. Clean full `gjsify run build` + `build:examples` now pass with 0 tsc errors; dom-events 274 GJS tests green; `gjsify lint` 0 errors; no `any` reintroduced. — 138 `no-explicit-any` warnings across `tests/`, `examples/`, and `showcases/` (excluding `.oxlintrc.json`-ignored `tests/e2e/*/out`) reduced to **0**. Strategy: ~90% genuine fixes (typed callbacks with concrete types, `unknown` + casts, Excalibur generics, MCP SDK types, axios `isAxiosError` guards, WebTorrent typed promises, acorn `AnyNode`/`Program`, socket.io `ManagerOptions & SocketOptions`, three.js `AdwRow` helpers for Adwaita web components), ~10% justified `oxlint-disable-next-line` for genuinely untyped GJS runtime globals (`globalThis.imports`, `print`, Soup.WebsocketConnection callbacks, Gio callbacks, WebGLBridge canvas), @types-missing libs (bittorrent-protocol, webtorrent internals), and Excalibur framework patterns where `any` is the API-dictated type (e.g. `ex.Engine<any>` default generic, `ex.Query<any,any>` from `queryTags()`). No blanket disables added to `.oxlintrc.json`. No test assertions weakened.
- ~~**#336 over-narrowing broke the `check` step in `tests/integration/*`**~~✓ — #336 (the commit after #334) over-narrowed working `any`-typed spec code in `tests/integration/*` to concrete `@types/*` types / `unknown` that don't model the real npm-package runtime surface the tests exercise, reding the CI **`check`** step (`gjsify run check` → `tsc --noEmit`). Latent on `main` because CI dies at `build` before reaching `check`; the #334-repair fixes `build`, which then exposes these — so this PR (#340) fixes BOTH to un-red `main`. **45 errors across 4 integration packages**, all in files #336 touched: `integration-streamx/src/throughput.spec.ts:59` and `integration-ts-for-gir/src/parser.spec.ts:168` (`.length` on `unknown`) — RESTORED to concrete via narrowing cast at the use site (`(data as Buffer).length`, `(instanceParam as unknown[]).length`); `integration-autobahn/src/fuzzingclient-driver.ts` (5×) — `(ev: Event)=>void`/`(ev: MessageEvent)=>void` not assignable to the WebSocket `EventListener`, and `send()` arg mismatch — RESTORED by importing the matching `@gjsify/dom-events`/`@gjsify/websocket` `Event`/`MessageEvent`/`ErrorEvent` types (the listeners were resolving to lib.dom's distinct `Event` identity) + narrowing the `send()` cast to the impl's `string|ArrayBuffer|ArrayBufferView`; `integration-webtorrent/src/{bitfield,client-add,client-destroy,file-buffer,iterator}.spec.ts` (38×) — `@types/webtorrent` API-surface gaps. Fixed WITHOUT module augmentation: the `'warning'`/`'verified'`/`'ready'` events the typings omit fall back to the `NodeJS.EventEmitter.on(event, listener)` overload via a local `EmitterLike` alias; the `bitfield`/`paused`/`name` add/seed options use `as unknown as WebTorrent.TorrentOptions` at the call site; the internal `_hasStartupBitfield` flag and `TorrentFile.{arrayBuffer,done}` runtime members narrow to small local interfaces (`as unknown as { … }`); the `remove(id, cb)` 2-arg form (typings only model `remove(id, opts, cb)`) passes an explicit `undefined` opts (runtime-equivalent — webtorrent treats a function in the opts slot as the callback). No `any` reintroduced (all concrete narrowing casts), no `.oxlintrc.json` change, no test assertions weakened. VERIFIED: clean full `gjsify run build` (all `lib/`+`*.tsbuildinfo` nuked first) → 0 errors; `gjsify run check` → 0 errors; `gjsify lint` → 0 errors; streamx `test:node` 155 pass (incl. throughput); ts-for-gir `parser.spec.ts` green (the 8 remaining `cli.spec.ts` "(GJS bundle)" failures are pre-existing GJS-spawn env issues — the Node-bundle equivalents pass). webtorrent `test:node` not run end-to-end (env: `webtorrent-fixtures` strict-`exports` blocks the fixture-copy prebuild + missing `node-datachannel` native addon — both pre-existing, unrelated to these type-only edits; the test bundle builds clean).
- ~~**`gjsify install` — data-loss bug: native backend extracted published tarballs over workspace SOURCE dirs**~~✓ — In a monorepo, `gjsify install`'s native backend could `rmSync` + drop a published npm tarball over a workspace's OWN source tree, wiping `src/**` (the published tarball ships only `files`, so everything not in `files` was deleted; `package.json` survived because the tarball ships its own). The smoking gun in the [map-editor report](../pixel-rpg/map-editor/docs/reports/gjsify-install-deletes-workspace-sources.md): **publishable** workspaces (`@pixelrpg/{engine,gjs,story-gjs}`, real `version` + `"files":["dist"]`) got wiped while **private** ones (no npm tarball) survived. Root cause: `workspaceInstall` (`commands/install.ts`) only excluded deps using the explicit `workspace:` protocol from the native fetch/extract queue. A dep whose NAME matches a local workspace but is referenced by a **plain semver range** (`^1.2.3`) — or that exists same-named on npm — slipped into `externalSpecs`, got resolved to the published tarball, and `extractOne` (`utils/install-backend-native.ts`) wiped the workspace dir (on GJS, `rmSync` follows the root-hoist symlink into the source via Gio NOFOLLOW semantics; even on Node it replaces the workspace symlink with a stale published `dist/`). **Root fix:** any dep whose name matches a discovered workspace is now SYMLINKED, never fetched — regardless of spec form (`workspace:`, plain semver, `*`, `latest`); only an explicit out-of-workspace protocol (`link:`/`file:`/`portal:`/`git+`/`http(s):`) opts out. **Defense-in-depth:** `extractOne` now calls `assertNodeModulesDest()` — refuses to `rmSync`/extract any `dest` whose logical path or `realpathSync` resolution is not under a `node_modules/` segment (so a regression can never again wipe a working tree; a leaked workspace pkg fails loud instead of destroying source). Preserved: external installs, workspace symlinks, lockfile writes, and `gjsify pack`'s `files` filter (correct for the published tarball — it just never touches a working tree); native backend stays the default (no `--backend npm` escape). New e2e `tests/e2e/install-workspace-source-safety/` (3 tests): a fixture monorepo with a publishable `@fixture/lib` (`version`, `"files":["dist"]`, `src/index.ts`) + a consumer, against a mock registry serving a same-named `@fixture/lib@9.9.9`. Asserts `src/index.ts` survives and `node_modules/@fixture/lib` stays a SYMLINK to the workspace for both `workspace:^` and plain-`^9.0.0` refs (incl. a pre-seeded-root-hoist-symlink re-install case). FAILS on pre-fix code (plain-semver + re-install cases), PASSES after. Wired into `test:e2e` + `test:e2e:install-workspace-source-safety`.
- ~~**`@gjsify/fetch` — hold one shared `Soup.Session` against GC (`host->conns == NULL` libsoup-CRITICAL)**~~✓ — `@gjsify/fetch`'s `Request` constructor created a fresh `new Soup.Session()` per request, stored only in the `Request`'s `INTERNALS`. Once the `Response` body was read, that per-request session became unreachable and a SpiderMonkey GC sweep could finalize it while a libsoup connection was still registered on its host. libsoup's connection-manager teardown (`soup_connection_manager_free` → `remove_connection` disconnects the conn's `disconnected` signal handler via `g_signal_handlers_disconnect_by_data` WITHOUT calling `soup_host_remove_connection`, then `g_hash_table_destroy(http_hosts)` → `soup_host_free`) then asserts `g_warn_if_fail (host->conns == NULL)` (`refs/libsoup/libsoup/soup-connection-manager.c:84`), printing `(gjs:…): libsoup-CRITICAL **: runtime check failed: (host->conns == NULL)` on stderr during heavy fetch use — notably `@gjsify/npm-registry`'s many packument/tarball fetches inside `gjsify install` (it uses `globalThis.fetch`, so all session lifecycle is fetch's). Cosmetic (install still exits 0) but a real GC race. **Fix** (`packages/web/fetch/src/request.ts`): a process-wide module-scope `sharedSession` (lazily created via `getSharedSession()`, so importing the package for `Headers`/`Request`/`Response` types on Node/browser allocates no Soup object). The `Request` constructor reuses it for every HTTP/HTTPS request instead of `new Soup.Session()`; the one-time `remove_feature_by_type(Soup.ContentDecoder.$gtype)` moved from per-request `_send` to the session getter. The session is now permanently reachable → never finalized mid-flight → the assertion path is never reached, and libsoup can pool/reuse keep-alive connections across requests. Matches the existing GLib/Soup BoxedInstance GC-safety precedent (`@gjsify/timers` → `GLib.timeout_add`). **Regression test** (`packages/web/fetch/src/soup-session.gjs.spec.ts`, GJS-only, 3 cases, wired into `test.mts`): asserts two distinct HTTP `Request`s share the SAME `Soup.Session` instance (fails on the old per-request code — verified by stashing the fix), non-HTTP requests allocate no session, and a child-gjs subprocess running many `fetch()` + `System.gc()` against a local `Soup.Server` produces stderr clean of `host->conns`/`libsoup-CRITICAL`. fetch GJS suite: 76 → 79 tests, all green; Node suite unaffected (3 GJS-only tests ignored, 61 pass).

No blanket disables added to `.oxlintrc.json`. No test assertions weakened.

Spec files (60): all 4 (`index.spec.ts`/`stream.spec.ts`/`websocket-server.spec.ts`/`stream.spec.ts`) got a file-level `// oxlint-disable typescript/no-explicit-any -- <tailored reason>` header — each reason explains *why* `any` is the right tool there (assert ws's CJS post-load self-references; demonstrate npm-`ws`-shape consumer code with `(req, socket, head)` / `(info, cb)` / `(ws)` / `(msg)` callback shapes that real consumers write without importing internal types; GI introspection boundary on the Soup.Session websocket_connect_async callback). **No Soup-boundary justified-disable needed:** the GI hooks `add_handler`/`add_websocket_handler`/connection signals already had typed `Soup.ServerMessage`/`Soup.WebsocketConnection`/`GLib.Bytes`/`GLib.Error` params before this PR — kept intact (precedent #348 process/streams.ts was different — direct `globalThis.imports.gi` reads). No `.oxlintrc.json` change, no test assertions weakened. Source-file strategy (25, all FIXED — zero per-line disables): public API surface kept Node-compatible everywhere — `connect()`'s callback `socket: any` → `Socket | TLSSocket` via a `ConnectSocket = net.Socket | tls.TLSSocket` alias (runtime value is `null` since the transport is Soup-managed or @gjsify/http2-native, but the listener signature stays drop-in for Node consumers); `Http2ServerRequest.socket: any` → `Socket | TLSSocket | null` matching Node's public shape, with the two write sites in `server/http2-server.ts` widening structurally via `as unknown as NonNullable<typeof req.socket>` (the runtime carries only `{remoteAddress,remotePort,localAddress,localPort,encrypted}` since GJS has no real `net.Socket`; return }`) type-check against Node's documented behavior. `ServerOptions.{Http1IncomingMessage,Http1ServerResponse}: any` (deprecated Node option fields not dispatched through in this impl) → `new (...args: unknown[]) => unknown` constructor-shape — kept for Node `ServerOptions`-literal assignability. `client-session.ts` `Soup.Session.send_async` callback `_self: any` → `Soup.Session | null` matching `Gio.AsyncReadyCallback<Soup.Session>`'s real signature;
