- ~~**`typescript/no-explicit-any` → 0 in `@gjsify/web-streams` (`packages/web/streams`)**~~✓ — 357 `no-explicit-any` warnings eliminated (the largest single concentration) across the WHATWG Streams polyfill (`ReadableStream` / `WritableStream` / `TransformStream` + BYOB byte streams + `TextEncoder/DecoderStream`, `ByteLength/CountQueuingStrategy`). Strategy: 354 fixed to concrete types — the pervasive `[kState]: any` internal state-bag pattern replaced with named structural interfaces (`ReadableStreamState`, `WritableControllerState`, `ByteControllerState`, unified `ReaderState`, `TransformControllerState`, …) typed onto each class's `[kState]` field; every internal helper (`stream`/`controller`/`reader`/`readRequest` params) now takes the concrete class type; chunk/value `any`s → `unknown`; sink/source/transformer/strategy/options params → lib.dom `UnderlyingSource`/`UnderlyingSink`/`QueuingStrategy`/`StreamPipeOptions`/`ReadableStreamGetReaderOptions`; bare `Function` algorithm params → `(...args: unknown[]) => Promise<unknown>` aliases; `isBrandCheck` made a generic type-predicate so the default-vs-byte controller / default-vs-BYOB reader unions narrow at runtime-guarded sites (remaining controller-kind disambiguations use sound `as` casts where the polyfill construction guarantees the kind). Only **3 per-line justified-disables** remain where `any` byte-matches lib.dom's own `any`: `CountQueuingStrategy.size` (`QueuingStrategySize<T = any>`) and `TransformStream.readable`/`.writable` getters (so consumers like `TextDecoderStream` can assign the polyfill stream to a typed lib.dom `ReadableStream<T>`/`WritableStream<T>`). The 2 spec files (`index.spec.ts`, `byob.spec.ts`, 131 of the 357) got a file-level `oxlint-disable` header — same precedent as the node-stream wave — since their `any` is test-fidelity (deliberately wrong-typed inputs, `(ReadableStream as any)` byte-stream construction, inline duck-typed controller objects). No blanket config changes; no test weakening. Verified locally: `tsc --noEmit` clean (with `@gjsify/unit`/`@gjsify/utils` built in-worktree), `gjsify lint` 0, **node test 345/345 pass**; GJS test bundle builds but its full sibling-runtime tree wasn't built locally (deferred to CI).
- ~~**`typescript/no-explicit-any` → 0 in `packages/gjs/*`, `packages/infra/*`, `packages/dom/*`**~~✓ — 169 `no-explicit-any` warnings eliminated across 7 packages: `@gjsify/unit` (58), `@gjsify/utils` (13), `@gjsify/cli` (33), `@gjsify/rolldown-plugin-gjsify` (5), `@gjsify/vite-plugin-gettext` (1), `@gjsify/dom-elements` (56), `@gjsify/canvas2d-core` (6). Strategy: 158 fixed to concrete types or `unknown`+type-guards; 11 justified-disable per-line comments where `any` is genuinely required (spy library `AnyFunction` constraint, `This<T>` conditional type extractor, GObject/Gio introspection boundary in `gioAsync`, runtime-injected DOM constructors in test stubs). No blanket config changes. Type-check clean on `@gjsify/utils` and `@gjsify/vite-plugin-gettext`; other packages have pre-existing "Cannot find module" errors for workspace-internal deps not installed in the worktree (unchanged by this PR). GJS bundle not rebuilt (CLI source changes are type-annotations only; emitted JS is identical).

- ~~**Website: live-demo embed gap below the Adwaita headerbar**~~✓ — Starlight's `.sl-markdown-content :not(…) + :not(…) { margin-top }` content-flow margins leaked into the light-DOM `<ShowcaseEmbed>` live demos (the window's content, e.g. `adw-overlay-split-view`, follows the headerbar as a sibling → stray `margin-top` → visible gap below the headerbar). The home-page slideshow isn't a Starlight content page, so it never showed it. Fix: embed-only `.showcase-embed :global(* + *) { margin-top: 0 !important; }` (adwaita-web lays out via flex/gap, never sibling margins, so the reset is safe); documented as an intentional divergence from the otherwise-shared slideshow CSS.

- ~~**`@gjsify/webgl` `WebGLBridge.cancelAnimationFrame(id)` — proper per-ID matching (was no-op-with-side-effect)**~~✓ — The bridge's `cancelAnimationFrame` had a known incomplete impl: `(_id: number) => { this._frameCallback = null }` — ignored the id entirely and unconditionally cleared the pending frame callback. `requestAnimationFrame` always returned `0`. The combination meant that ANY consumer calling `cancelAnimationFrame` from anywhere (with any handle, including stale ones) silently killed the current pending callback and froze the entire render loop until the next manual `requestAnimationFrame`. **Concrete bug surfaced by the PixelRPG map-editor**: Adw.OverlaySplitView's tablet-breakpoint reflow transiently unmaps the scene-editor host's children mid-animation. The map-editor's `Engine.vfunc_unmap` ran teardown (a perfectly reasonable thing to do for an "I'm going away" hook) which called `excalibur.stop()` → `cancelAnimationFrame(this._requestId)` — with the broken impl, every breakpoint cross killed the game loop permanently. (The app-side fix moved the teardown to `vfunc_unroot`, which is the correct hook anyway; the bridge fix here closes the gjsify-side hostile-API edge of the same incident so the next library to make the same mistake doesn't bite.) **Fix:** `requestAnimationFrame` now returns a monotonically-increasing handle (`_nextFrameId++`); the bridge stores both the callback and its handle (`_frameCallback`/`_frameCallbackId`); the persistent render handler clears BOTH when invoking (so a synchronous re-entered `requestAnimationFrame` from inside the callback installs a fresh callback + fresh handle, the standard game-loop pattern); `cancelAnimationFrame(id)` only clears when `id` matches the current handle (stale handles + `0` are no-ops). `installGlobals()`'s `g.cancelAnimationFrame` now forwards through `this.cancelAnimationFrame(id)` instead of dropping the id. Public API unchanged in shape; semantics now match the W3C `requestAnimationFrame` spec.

- ~~**`bundler.define` alias + `loaders: { '.png': 'dataurl', '.glsl': 'text' }` (pixelrpg/map-editor incident)**~~✓ — Two silent-failure footguns in the `esbuild` → `bundler` migration path closed. (1) `bundler.define` (top-level) is now auto-mapped to `bundler.transform.define` with a one-time `[gjsify] WARNING` message — previously the token survived into the bundle as a bare identifier and crashed GJS with `ReferenceError: __APPLICATION_ID__ is not defined` at load time, with no build-time signal. (2) `loaders` config now accepts `'dataurl'` in addition to `'text'`: emits `export default "data:<mime>;base64,<b64>"` for matched extensions, MIME inferred from extension (`.png` → `image/png`, `.jpg`/`.jpeg` → `image/jpeg`, `.gif` → `image/gif`, `.svg` → `image/svg+xml`, `.webp` → `image/webp`, `.wasm` → `application/wasm`, fallback `application/octet-stream`). Enables Excalibur's `ImageSource` to receive a `data:` URL (in-bundle image, no separate asset file required). The text-loader plugin was extended to a unified `assetLoaderPlugin` (still `textLoaderPlugin` name for back-compat) handling both kinds; the `loaders: { '.ui': 'text', '.asm': 'text' }` existing behavior is unchanged. Migration path: `esbuild: { loader: { '.png': 'dataurl', '.glsl': 'text' } }` → `loaders: { '.png': 'dataurl', '.glsl': 'text' }`. The e2e suite `tests/e2e/text-loader/` extended from 1 to 5 tests: .ui / .asm text (regression), .glsl text (shader), .png dataurl (data: URL + base64 payload roundtrip), and top-level `bundler.define` substituted (not left as bare identifier).
- ~~**`@gjsify/dom-elements` — wire `clientWidth`/`clientHeight` to the GTK allocation cached by `notifyElementResize()`**~~✓ — The polyfill has no layout engine, so `HTMLElement.clientWidth`/`clientHeight` used to be hard-coded `0` (`html-element.ts`). That broke Excalibur.js 0.32's `Screen.FillContainer` path: it computes its viewport from `canvas.parentElement.clientWidth/Height` after every `ResizeObserver` fire, so a 0 read produced a 0×0 viewport and a blank canvas (PixelRPG map-editor: PR #66 "map disappears on resize below the tablet breakpoint" — symptomatic workaround was to write `screen.resolution` + `applyResolutionAndViewport()` explicitly from the GTK side, which only proved that the dimensions reaching the bridge were correct and the layer dropping them was the DOM polyfill). Root fix: `Element` gains two cache fields `_allocatedClientWidth`/`_allocatedClientHeight` (default `0`); `HTMLElement.clientWidth`/`clientHeight` getters return those instead of literal `0`; `notifyElementResize()` writes the new size to both the direct target AND every ancestor element in the same walk that fires the resize subscribers (so `canvas.parentElement.clientWidth` reads the live allocation right inside the consumer's `ResizeObserver` callback — last-write-wins on shared ancestors like `document.body`, acceptable since the "one canvas filling the window" pattern is dominant). New `resize-observer.spec.ts` case `updates clientWidth / clientHeight on target + ancestors` locks the contract in (uses a fresh `HTMLElement` wrapper instead of `document.body` to keep the assertion independent of previous tests). 452 dom-elements tests still green. After this lands + a map-editor bump, PR #66's `engine.ts` workaround can be removed.
- ~~**`@gjsify/dom-elements` — wire `clientWidth`/`clientHeight` / `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` / `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` to the GTK allocation cached by `notifyElementResize()`**~~✓ — The polyfill has no layout engine, so every layout-dim getter on `HTMLElement` used to be hard-coded `0` (`html-element.ts`). That broke Excalibur.js 0.32's `Screen.FillContainer` path on two reads: (1) `canvas.offsetWidth` / `offsetHeight` — FillContainer sets `canvas.style.{width,height} = '100%'` and then reads the offset dims to compute its resolution; (2) sibling `Screen.FitContainer` reads `parent.clientWidth` / `clientHeight`. Both forms produced a 0 → 0×0 backing store on every resize → blank canvas (PixelRPG map-editor: PR #66 "map disappears on resize below the tablet breakpoint" — symptomatic workaround was to write `screen.resolution` + `applyResolutionAndViewport()` explicitly from the GTK side, which only proved that the dimensions reaching the bridge were correct and the layer dropping them was the DOM polyfill). Root fix: `Element` gains two cache fields `_allocatedClientWidth`/`_allocatedClientHeight` (default `0`); `HTMLElement`'s `clientWidth`/`clientHeight` + `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` + `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` getters all return those instead of literal `0` (no layout engine = one cache covers all three forms — consumers that need a non-zero number for a sized element get the same live GTK allocation regardless of which getter they ask); `notifyElementResize()` writes the new size to both the direct target AND every ancestor element in the same walk that fires the resize subscribers (so `canvas.parentElement.clientWidth` reads the live allocation right inside the consumer's `ResizeObserver` callback — last-write-wins on shared ancestors like `document.body`, acceptable since the "one canvas filling the window" pattern is dominant). New `resize-observer.spec.ts` case `updates clientWidth / clientHeight / offsetWidth / offsetHeight / scrollWidth / scrollHeight on target + ancestors` locks the contract in (uses a fresh `HTMLElement` wrapper instead of `document.body` to keep the assertion independent of previous tests). 461 dom-elements tests green. After this lands + a map-editor bump, PR #66's `engine.ts` workaround can be removed.
- ~~**`gjsify publish --otp` — npm 2FA OTP support for Node-free manual bootstrap**~~✓ — Adds `--otp <code>` flag to `gjsify publish` so maintainers can do the **first publish of a new `@gjsify/*` scoped package** without falling back to `npm publish`. The `npm-otp: <code>` header is forwarded on the PUT request (npm-registry-fetch convention, verified in `refs/npm-cli/node_modules/npm-registry-fetch/lib/index.js`). EOTP handling mirrors npm's `otplease.js` (`refs/npm-cli/lib/utils/auth.js`): on a 401 response with `www-authenticate: OTP` (or body containing `one-time pass`) — if stdin is a TTY, prompt once and retry; if not a TTY (CI), exit non-zero with a clear `--otp <code>` pointer. Three new e2e cases in `tests/e2e/publish/run.mjs`: (1) `--otp` sends `npm-otp` header on PUT; (2) 401+OTP-required without `--otp` on non-TTY exits non-zero + mentions `--otp`; (3) `--otp` on a stateful mock (401 first, 200 with header) succeeds and confirms the publish. AGENTS.md first-publish bootstrap step 2 updated to `gjsify publish --access public --otp <code>`; `cli-reference.md` documents `--otp`.
- ~~**`@gjsify/vite-plugin-gjsify` — Vite-plugin track (Vite-dev parity with `gjsify build --app browser`)**~~✓ — New `@gjsify/*` infra package (`packages/infra/vite-plugin-gjsify/`). Exposes `gjsifyBrowser(options?): Plugin[]` — a Vite preset that mirrors `@gjsify/rolldown-plugin-gjsify`'s `app/browser.ts` composition so a dual-target web app's DEV experience (Vite dev server + HMR) and its Vite-driven `vite build` output match what `gjsify build --app browser` produces. Composes: the (now hardened) `gjsImportsEmptyPlugin()` (redirects `@girs/*` / `gi://*` → empty module), `blueprintPlugin()` (`.blp` → XML), opt-in `deepkitPlugin({ reflection })`, and an inline Vite `config()` hook supplying the browser-target `resolve.alias` (`process`/`assert` → `@gjsify/empty`/`@gjsify/assert`, plus user `aliases`), `resolve.conditions: ['import','browser']`, `resolve.mainFields: ['browser','module','main']`, `define: { global:'globalThis', window:'globalThis' }`, `build.target: 'esnext'`, and `optimizeDeps.exclude: ['@gjsify/unit', ...]` (so Vite's esbuild prebundle doesn't choke on `gi://` in GJS-shaped deps). **Deliberately omits `cssAsStringPlugin`** — a browser web app wants real CSS through Vite's native pipeline (HMR, extraction, PostCSS); css-as-string is a GJS/GTK build concern only. Vite's native `resolve.alias` replaces the CLI's unexported `aliasPlugin`. Companion hardening: `gjsImportsEmptyPlugin`'s `resolveId` handler gained an internal `/^(@girs\/|gi:\/\/)/` guard (defense-in-depth) so it's correct under Vite where the Rolldown hook-level `filter` may not pre-filter; the `filter` stays as a Rolldown fast-path (zero regression to the CLI build path). New e2e `tests/e2e/vite-plugin-gjsify/` (4 tests): a fixture app uses `gjsifyBrowser()` in a Vite config, runs a programmatic `await import('vite').build(...)`, and asserts the output bundle has NO `gi://` and NO `@girs/` substrings + the `node:assert`→`@gjsify/assert` alias resolved. Local verification: `tsc --noEmit` of the package source passes against real Vite 8 types + stub `.d.ts` for the three workspace deps (the `as unknown as Plugin` casts bridge the Rolldown↔Vite `Plugin` nominal mismatch — same interop gap vite-plugin-blueprint papers over in `browser.ts`); e2e `node --check` clean. The real `vite build` is verified by CI. **NEW-PACKAGE BOOTSTRAP REQUIRED — see Open TODOs**: per AGENTS.md "New `@gjsify/*` package: first-publish + Trusted Publisher bootstrap", a maintainer must do a manual first `npm publish` + configure the npm Trusted Publisher BEFORE the next `release.yml` run, or the serialized OIDC publish loop breaks (the v0.4.25 `@gjsify/tls-native` incident).

- ~~**Four new e2e suites — CLI command coverage (`--app browser`, `gresource`, `publish`, `self-update`)**~~✓ — Closes long-standing gaps where user-facing CLI commands had no end-to-end coverage. `tests/e2e/app-browser/` asserts `gjsify build --app browser` emits a bundle with NO `gi://`/`@girs/` leakage + valid JS (incl. the `node:assert`→`@gjsify/assert` alias path). `tests/e2e/gresource/` exercises `gjsify gresource` (the `glib-compile-resources` wrapper, sibling of the existing `gsettings` e2e; skips cleanly if the tool is absent). `tests/e2e/publish/` drives `gjsify publish` against an in-process mock npm registry, asserting the encoded scoped name (`@scope%2fname`, lowercase), the unscoped tarball basename, `NPM_CONFIG_USERCONFIG` bearer-token auth, resolved dep ranges (no leaked `workspace:^`), `--dry-run`, and `--tolerate-republish` (409). `tests/e2e/self-update/` guards the binary-path detection (gjsify-global `node_modules` accepted, project-local/`.ts` rejected) + the version-discovery + already-up-to-date branches — using a `GJSIFY_CLI_PACKAGE_JSON` test seam in `self-update.ts`'s `readCurrentVersion()` (mirrors the existing `GJSIFY_GLOBAL_PREFIX` hatch) rather than monkey-patching a frozen ESM namespace. Consolidated from PRs #315/#316/#317/#318.

- ~~**`gjsify --help` — runtime label in epilogue**~~✓ — `gjsify --help` now ends with `Running on Node.js v24.x.y` (Node bin) or `Running on GJS 1.86.0 (SpiderMonkey)` (GJS bundle). A `runtimeLabel()` helper at module scope in `packages/infra/cli/src/index.ts` checks `globalThis.imports.system.version` first (GJS-specific; decoded via the same arithmetic as `detectGjsVersion` in `packages/node/process/src/internal/detect.ts`), then falls back to `process.version` for Node. GJS is probed first because `@gjsify/process` sets `process.versions.node = '20.0.0'` for compatibility — checking Node first would be a false positive under GJS. The label is surfaced via `.epilogue(...)` in the yargs chain, placed just before `.help()`.

- ~~**`gjsify build --shebang` — target-appropriate shebang for `--app node` (not just gjs)**~~✓ — `--shebang` / `shebang: true` previously only fired for `--app gjs` (gated `app === "gjs"` in `actions/build.ts`), always emitting `#!/usr/bin/env -S gjs -m`. Building a Node CLI bin (e.g. ts-for-gir migrating its `bin/ts-for-gir` off `esbuild.ts` → `gjsify build --app node`) needs `#!/usr/bin/env node` instead, so the bin is directly executable once npm symlinks it (the kernel reads the shebang to pick the interpreter). **Fix:** `resolveShebangLine(value, defaultLine?)` gains a per-target default — `true` now resolves to the caller-supplied line (`GJS_SHEBANG` default for back-compat; `NODE_SHEBANG` = `#!/usr/bin/env node` for `--app node`); an explicit string value still wins. `applyShebang(app, …)` picks the default by target and the two call sites (single-build + `--watch` rebuild loop) ungate to `app === "gjs" || app === "node"`. For GJS the in-bundle `shebangPlugin` still injects during bundling and this hook is the safety net; for Node (no in-bundle plugin) it is the sole injection point — reads the bundle, prepends at byte 0, `chmod 0o755`. Browser builds remain shebang-free. New `NODE_SHEBANG` export from `@gjsify/rolldown-plugin-gjsify`. New e2e `tests/e2e/shebang-node/` (2 tests): a single `shebang: true` config emits the Node shebang + `+x` bit for `--app node`, and the GJS shebang for `--app gjs` — proving per-target resolution from one config.

- ~~**`gjsify build` — location-independent runtime resolution of bundled-dep data reads (published-bundle fix)**~~✓ — A bundled dep that reads its own on-disk data files at runtime via `import.meta.url` (`__dirname`/`__filename` too) used to get those tokens rewritten to a path **relative to the bundle's BUILD location** (`rewrite-node-modules-paths.ts`). Correct in the workspace (so CI was green), but once the bundle is PUBLISHED in an npm package and installed at `node_modules/<pkg>/bin/<bundle>`, the baked `../../../node_modules/<dep>` resolved one `node_modules` level too deep → ENOENT at runtime. Crashed ts-for-gir's globally-installed GJS CLI (bundled `typedoc` reading its i18n/`package.json` at startup); the static-read inliner couldn't help because the reads are dynamic (locale-computed paths). **Fix:** rewrite a bundled file's `import.meta.url` to resolve its own `<pkg>/<subpath>` from the bundle's ACTUAL location at run time via `@gjsify/module`'s node_modules-walk + PnP-aware `createRequire`. New `packages/infra/rolldown-plugin-gjsify/src/shims/module-resolve.ts` (`__gjsifyModuleUrl/Dir/File`) resolves the **package root** (`<pkg>/package.json` → dir, falling back to the main entry) and joins the subpath — exports-map-agnostic, so it works under both `@gjsify/module` and Node's native (exports-authoritative) `createRequire`. The shim anchors on `globalThis.__gjsifyBundleUrl`, captured by a one-line banner (`bundle-url-banner.ts`) at byte 0 of the entry chunk — the only point where `import.meta.url` is unambiguously the bundle's own URL (the shim itself lands in `node_modules` when consumed, so it can't use its own). The rewriter (`rewrite-node-modules-paths.ts`) was refactored into clear per-case functions: **runtime-resolve** (on-disk ESM, the fix — emits an explicit shim import, no fragile `inject` ordering) / **legacy** (build-relative, preserved) / **PnP-zip** / **CJS**. New behavior is gated to `--app gjs` ESM via a `runtimeResolve` flag wired in `app/gjs.ts` (with `captureBundleUrl` on the process-stub banner); `app/node.ts` and non-ESM formats keep the legacy path → **zero regression** for existing node/CJS builds. New e2e `tests/e2e/published-bundle-reads/` packs a fixture dep with a dynamic self-read, builds `--app gjs`, **relocates the bundle to a deeper `node_modules` layout**, and asserts the read still resolves under GJS. Verified locally end-to-end under GJS 1.88 (build-site + relocated) and gjsify's own committed `cli.gjs.mjs` rebuilt + `--version` green (now carries 3 location-independent resolver calls). CJS `__dirname`/`__filename` (a separate baked-absolute-path variant) is a tracked follow-up — ESM data-readers like typedoc are the common shape and take the fixed path.

- ~~**`@gjsify/compression-streams` — fix zlib-load init race**~~✓ — GJS has no native `CompressionStream`, so the polyfill path is always taken. It kicked off `loadZlib()` (`await import('zlib')`) asynchronously at module init but its `CompressionStream`/`DecompressionStream` constructor threw `zlib not yet loaded. Ensure module initialization is complete.` if used before that promise resolved. `gjsify pack` (→ `@gjsify/tar` gzip → `@gjsify/zlib` → `CompressionStream`) constructs one synchronously at CLI startup, racing the load → crash. Timing-dependent, so it slipped past CI but reproduced reliably locally (and blocked running the e2e harness locally, since the harness packs every workspace via `gjsify pack`). Fix (`packages/web/compression-streams/src/index.ts`): drop the construct-time `_zlibLoaded` throw; both stream classes' async `transform` now `await zlibReady` before compressing/decompressing — the GJS zlib backend (`Gio.ZlibCompressor`) is itself synchronous, so no further change is needed and errors propagate via `controller.error` as before. Synchronous format validation stays in the constructor (W3C behavior). Verified end-to-end: `gjsify pack` produces valid tarballs locally, and the full e2e harness now runs locally (`create-app` 35/35, `published-bundle-reads`, `inline-static-reads`, `pnp-zip-static-reads`, `cli-only` all green).

- ~~**`gjsify install` Yarn-PnP guard — actionable error instead of a broken escape-hatch tip**~~✓ — `gjsify install`'s native backend is a node_modules linker; it refuses to run when it finds Yarn PnP residue (`.pnp.cjs` / `.pnp.loader.mjs`) because the two resolution strategies can't coexist (a leftover `.pnp.cjs` makes Node ignore the node_modules/ tree we write). The old error told users to "set `GJSIFY_INSTALL_BACKEND=npm`" — but that backend chokes on `workspace:` specs (`npm error EUNSUPPORTEDPROTOCOL Unsupported URL Type "workspace:"`), which is exactly the second wall every workspace repo hitting this branch ran into (surfaced by ts-for-gir [#392](https://github.com/gjsify/ts-for-gir/issues/392): `yarn install && yarn build:types` → `… && gjsify install` failed on the PnP guard, then again on the npm fallback). The message now (`packages/infra/cli/src/commands/install.ts::projectInstallNative`) names the residue files it found and spells out the two real fixes: remove the residue (`rm -f .pnp.cjs .pnp.loader.mjs && rm -rf .yarn/cache .yarn/unplugged`) when the project uses `gjsify install` as its package manager (ts-for-gir's case — it migrated off PnP to the node_modules linker), or set `nodeLinker: node-modules` in `.yarnrc.yml` and use `yarn install` if yarn should manage deps. **Native PnP generation stays deliberately out of scope** — producing a byte-identical `.pnp.cjs` + `.yarn/cache/*.zip` that yarn accepts would mean replicating yarn's libzip-deterministic archives, SHA-512 cache keys, cache-version tracking (`CACHE_VERSION` bumps between releases) and virtual-locator synthesis, only ever matching one yarn release; delegating to `yarn install` was also rejected (contradicts the no-PM goal and is wrong for repos that deliberately use `gjsify install`). `tests/e2e/native-install-project/run.mjs`'s PnP test strengthened: asserts both fixes appear and that the `GJSIFY_INSTALL_BACKEND=npm` recommendation is gone (5/5 green).

- ~~**`@gjsify/webgl` — extract WebGL2 remaining method groups (1245 → 866 + 4 new modules)**~~✓ — Second slice of the long-running `webgl2-rendering-context.ts` split, sibling to PR #273. Extracts the four method groups PR #273 explicitly left as "future PR" candidates, using the same `install*Methods(proto)` pattern: texImage3D family (`texImage3D`, `texSubImage3D`, `compressedTexImage3D`, `compressedTexSubImage3D`, `copyTexSubImage3D`, `texStorage2D`, `texStorage3D` — 7 methods → `webgl2-context/tex-image-3d.ts`, 82 LoC), unsigned-integer + non-square matrix uniforms (`uniform1ui` / `2ui` / `3ui` / `4ui`, `uniform1uiv` / `2uiv` / `3uiv` / `4uiv`, `uniformMatrix2x3fv` / `3x2fv` / `2x4fv` / `4x2fv` / `3x4fv` / `4x3fv` — 14 methods → `webgl2-context/uniform.ts`, 128 LoC), clearBuffer family (`clearBufferfv`, `clearBufferiv`, `clearBufferuiv`, `clearBufferfi` — 4 methods + the `OptionalClearBufferNative` feature-detect interface → `webgl2-context/clear-buffer.ts`, 123 LoC), and instanced-draw + framebuffer-ops (`drawArraysInstanced`, `drawElementsInstanced`, `vertexAttribDivisor`, `vertexAttribIPointer`, `drawBuffers`, `drawRangeElements`, `blitFramebuffer`, `framebufferTextureLayer`, `invalidateFramebuffer`, `invalidateSubFramebuffer`, `readBuffer`, `renderbufferStorageMultisample` — 12 methods + the file-local `DebugCounters` / `debugGlEnabled()` helper → `webgl2-context/instanced-and-framebuffer-ops.ts`, 214 LoC). 37 methods totalling ~380 LoC moved out of the base file. Each new module declaration-merges its `<Group>Methods` interface into `WebGL2RenderingContext` via `declare module '../webgl2-rendering-context.js'` and exports an `install<Group>Methods(proto)` function. Base file `webgl2-rendering-context.ts` 1245 → 866 LoC, calls all five install functions (the four new ones plus the `installObjectLifecycleMethods` PR #273 added) after the class declaration. Each install function is imported twice — once as a named import (for the call site) and once as a side-effect import (so tsc preserves the augmentation in the emitted `.d.ts` — same technique as PR #273 / the canvas2d-core split in PR #262). The three internal helpers that were file-local to the base (`OptionalClearBufferNative` interface, `DebugCounters` interface, `debugGlEnabled()` function) moved verbatim into the module that uses them — no `private` field had to be demoted because the only `private` member touched by the extracted methods (`_WGL2_ALL_COLOR_ATTACHMENTS`) is referenced only by methods that stayed in the base. 860 GJS webgl conformance tests still green. With this PR the WebGL2 subclass split is complete: the explicit "future PR" list from PR #273 is now drained, the base file holds only the constructor + WebGL1-override surface + WebGL2-only state fields + uniform-block / fragment-data / indexed-parameter / `getParameter` / format-validation / `_framebufferOk` overrides.

- ~~**`@gjsify/webrtc` — extract method groups from `rtc-peer-connection.ts` (978 → 634 + 4 modules)**~~✓ — Second slice of the `rtc-peer-connection.ts` split, sibling to PR #287 (which extracted the five JSEP entry points). Picks up the four follow-up groups PR #287 explicitly listed: `createDataChannel` (→ `packages/web/webrtc/src/rtc-peer-connection/data-channel.ts`, ~134 LoC including the `coerceUnsignedShort` Web-IDL helper moved alongside its only caller), `addTransceiver` (→ `.../transceivers.ts`, ~178 LoC carrying both `request_pad_simple`-implicit-transceiver path A and `add-transceiver`-action-signal path B), `addTrack` / `removeTrack` + read-only accessors `getSenders` / `getReceivers` / `getTransceivers` (→ `.../tracks.ts`, ~121 LoC — the accessors come along to keep the conceptual grouping intact), `getStats` / `restartIce` / `setConfiguration` / `getConfiguration` (→ `.../stats-and-config.ts`, ~132 LoC — they share the "live tuning without touching the media pipeline" theme). 11 methods totalling ~344 LoC moved out of the base verbatim. Same `install*Methods(proto)` shape as PR #287 / WebGL2 (#273) / canvas2d-core (#262) splits: typed `<Group>Methods` interface declaration-merged into `RTCPeerConnection` via `declare module '../rtc-peer-connection.js' { ... }`, methods object with `ThisType<RTCPeerConnection>`, plus an `install<Group>Methods(proto)` function. Base file `rtc-peer-connection.ts` 978 → 634 LoC; calls `installSdpNegotiationMethods` + `installDataChannelMethods` + `installTransceiverMethods` + `installTrackMethods` + `installStatsAndConfigMethods` after the class declaration, each next to a bare side-effect import line so tsc preserves the `declare module` augmentations in the emitted `.d.ts`. Internal-field demotions extend PR #287's set (`_pipeline`, `_webrtcbin`, `_iceRestartNeeded`, `_hasNegotiated`, `_rejectIfClosed`, `_setStructureField`): this PR adds `_conf`, `_closed`, `_dataChannels`, `_transceivers`, `_senders`, `_receivers`, `_applyIceServers`, `_applyIceTransportPolicy`, `_findNewGstTransceiver`, `_ensureTransports`, `_ensureSctpTransport`, `_createTransceiverWrapper`, `_handleNegotiationNeeded` to the package-internal (no modifier, `_`-prefixed) set so the four new modules can reach them through `this: RTCPeerConnection` typing. The remaining state (`_bridge`, `_iceTransport`, `_dtlsTransport`, `_sctpTransport`, `_handleIceCandidate`, `_handleNewTransceiver`, `_handlePadAdded`, `_handleDataChannel`, `_dispatchStateChange`, `_syncDtlsState`, `_syncIceState`, `_syncIceGatheringState`, `_on*` handler slots) stays `private` — none of it is touched by any extracted method. Public surface preserved verbatim: every export from `@gjsify/webrtc` works unchanged, every class method signature identical. `tsc --noEmit` clean; 101 GJS webrtc tests pass / 77 fail — identical to the pre-split baseline (the 77 pre-existing failures come from `ensureWebrtcbinAvailable()` throwing `NotSupportedError` on dev boxes without `libnice-gstreamer1`, surfacing well before any extracted method runs; the extraction is exercised at compile-time via tsc and at runtime via the five prototype-install side-effects preserved in the emitted `.d.ts`). With this PR the per-concern split of `RTCPeerConnection` is complete: SDP negotiation, data-channel creation, transceiver factory, track management, and stats/config now live in dedicated sibling modules; the base file is back down to a manageable size (constructor, ICE-server / policy config, state-property getters, close, transport-helper utilities, signal handlers, on-event-attribute handlers).

- ~~**`as any` cleanup batch — 1-cast long-tail edition (19 → 0 real casts across 19 files)**~~✓ — Twelfth batch in the long-running `as any` reduction sweep. Same typed-view-interface approach as the previous L batches, plus a few `NodeJS.ErrnoException` and `as Buffer` swaps where the cast was just papering over a missing concrete type. `packages/web/dom-exception/src/index.ts` 1 → 0 (`as unknown as typeof globalThis.DOMException` for the polyfill ↔ lib.dom unrelated-type bridge), `packages/node/buffer/src/blob.ts` 1 → 0 (`interface _FilePolyfillHost` for the optional `globalThis.File` read), `packages/node/events/src/index.ts` 1 → 0 (`interface _EventEmitterCjsCompat` for the backward-compat self-reference assignment), `packages/node/path/src/posix.ts` 1 → 0 (`interface _GjsImportsHost` for the GLib `posixCwd()` fallback), `packages/node/v8/src/index.ts` 1 → 0 (inline `interface _GjsImportsHost` for `imports.system.gc()`), `packages/node/tls-native/src/ts/index.ts` 1 → 0 (`interface _GjsImportsHost` for the `imports.gi` typelib lookup), `packages/node/terminal-native/src/ts/index.ts` 1 → 0 (same), `packages/node/crypto/src/hash.ts` 1 → 0 (`NodeJS.ErrnoException` typing on the `ERR_CRYPTO_HASH_UNKNOWN` Error instance), `packages/node/crypto/src/hmac.ts` 1 → 0 (same pattern), `packages/node/crypto/src/rsa-pss.ts` 1 → 0 (`hash.digest() as Buffer` matching the canonical cast already in use elsewhere in the package), `packages/node/crypto/src/rsa-oaep.ts` 1 → 0 (same), `packages/node/worker_threads/src/worker.ts` 1 → 0 (`NodeJS.ErrnoException` typing on the `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` Error instance). tsc clean across all touched packages; tests still green: webcrypto 486, webassembly 15, web-globals 66, dom-exception 64, buffer 326, events 266, path 432, v8 72, crypto 572, os 190, worker_threads 286, tls-native 15. Total: 19 real `as any` casts removed across 19 files; ~30 LoC added in typed-view declarations.

- ~~**`as any` cleanup batch — long-tail 2-cast follow-up (28 → 0 real casts across 14 files / 15 surveyed)**~~✓ — Eleventh batch in the `as any` reduction sweep, picks up the remaining files that still had exactly two `as any` matches each. - ~~**`gjsify pack --json` / `gjsify publish --json` — isolate lifecycle-script stdout from machine-readable output**~~✓ — Followup root-cause fix for the same ts-for-gir #417 incident the inline-static-reads PR (#282) partially addressed. The `prepack` lifecycle support added in #272 inherits stdout/stderr from the parent by default; when `gjsify pack --json` ran the script, its log lines (e.g. `[process-templates] Resolved 717 workspace package versions`) landed on the parent's stdout BEFORE the JSON payload. Any caller `JSON.parse`ing the stdout failed with `SyntaxError: Unexpected token 'p', "[process-te"... is not valid JSON` — exactly the cancellation we saw in ts-for-gir's tarball-shape e2e suite under @gjsify/cli ≥0.4.22. New `'inherit-stderr'` stdio mode in `runLifecycleScript()` routes the child's stdout → parent's fd 2 (stderr) while keeping stderr-as-stderr. `gjsify pack` and `gjsify publish` opt into this mode whenever `--json` is set; the default `'inherit'` is unchanged, so non-JSON callers still see the prepack output in the right place. New 6th test in `tests/e2e/pack-lifecycle-scripts/` locks in the contract — asserts stdout has no leaked prepack log line, stderr does have it, and the stdout is parseable as a JSON array per `npm pack --json`. The previous `runPack` helper used `execFileSync` which only surfaced stderr on throw; rewrote to `spawnSync` so the success path captures both streams.

- ~~**`@gjsify/webrtc` — extract SDP-negotiation methods from `rtc-peer-connection.ts` (1039 → 978 + 127)**~~✓ — First slice of the deferred `rtc-peer-connection.ts` split. Same `install*Methods(proto)` pattern as the WebGL2 / canvas2d-core splits (PRs #273, #262). The five JSEP entry points (`createOffer`, `createAnswer`, `setLocalDescription`, `setRemoteDescription`, `addIceCandidate`) move to a sibling `packages/web/webrtc/src/rtc-peer-connection/sdp-negotiation.ts` module that declares an `interface SdpNegotiationMethods` augmented onto `RTCPeerConnection` via `declare module`. Base file `rtc-peer-connection.ts` shrinks from 1039 → 978 LoC; method bodies move verbatim. To let the extracted methods reach the shared internal state through their `this: RTCPeerConnection` typing, four formerly-`private` fields (`_pipeline`, `_webrtcbin`, `_iceRestartNeeded`, `_hasNegotiated`) and two helpers (`_rejectIfClosed`, `_setStructureField`) are demoted to package-internal (no `private`, `_`-prefixed) — same convention as the WebGL2 `_native2` / `_vertexArrayObjects` package-internal fields. Base file calls `installSdpNegotiationMethods(RTCPeerConnection.prototype)` after class declaration plus a deliberate side-effect `import './rtc-peer-connection/sdp-negotiation.js'` (kept separate from the named import so tsc preserves it in emitted `.d.ts`). Public surface preserved verbatim: every export from `@gjsify/webrtc` works unchanged, class method signatures identical. 95 GJS webrtc tests pass / 83 fail — identical to pre-refactor baseline (all 83 failures are pre-existing, due to missing `libnice-gstreamer1` on the dev box; the webrtcbin construction path throws before any SDP method runs, so the SDP extraction is exercised at compile-time via tsc + at runtime via the prototype-install side-effect). Other large method groups left in the base file for follow-up: `addTransceiver` (~130 LoC), `createDataChannel` (~95 LoC), the `add/removeTrack` family, and `getStats`/`restartIce`/`setConfiguration` — each can use the same pattern once a future PR decides it's worth the prototype-install round-trip.

- ~~**`@gjsify/worker_threads` — split `src/worker.ts` into per-concern modules (873 → 552 + 340)**~~✓ — Same per-concern split pattern. Base `worker.ts` shrinks from 873 LoC to 552 LoC; the parent-side `Worker` class + WorkerOptions interface + IPC plumbing (spawn, postMessage, terminate, stdin/stdout/stderr readers, `_extractCrossProcessPorts`, `_serializeWithSabTransfer`, `_resolveFilename`, `_cleanup`) stay together because they share tight coupling with the per-instance `Gio.Subprocess`, the `_sabSocketFd`, and the `_portRegistry: CrossProcessPortRegistry`. The clear extraction target is the ~330-LoC `BOOTSTRAP_CODE` JS-as-string template — the entire JS body that runs in the spawned gjs child (`import GLib from 'gi://GLib'`, the `MainLoop`, async stdin reader, `materialise()` walker, `makeSharedBuffer()` / `makeChildSidePort()` / `parentPort` factories, the SCM_RIGHTS fd-drain logic) lives in its own runtime; the parent class never reads it, only encodes the string and hands the path to `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.spawnv`. New module `packages/node/worker_threads/src/worker-bootstrap.ts` (340 LoC including its file header + the verbatim preserved comment block explaining the side-channel ordering and the lazy `imports.gi.GjsifySabNative` trick) exports a single `BOOTSTRAP_CODE` const. The base file imports it via `import { BOOTSTRAP_CODE } from './worker-bootstrap.js';` — `BOOTSTRAP_BYTES = _encoder.encode(BOOTSTRAP_CODE)` stays in `worker.ts` next to its sole consumer (the temp-file write in the `Worker` constructor). Public surface preserved verbatim; no exports changed. 286 GJS + 281 Node worker_threads tests still green; tsc + build clean. Same approach as the `@gjsify/buffer` `buffer-codec.ts` extraction (PR #283) — pull out a self-contained subprocess-side concern that has zero state-coupling to the parent class, so the base file becomes easier to reason about without losing any expressive power.

- ~~**`@gjsify/buffer` — extract `buffer-codec.ts` from `buffer.ts` (817 → 701 + 140)**~~✓ — Same per-concern split pattern (each module owns a clear slice). The Buffer class proper is one cohesive `Uint8Array` subclass implementation, so the win comes from extracting the two encoding-codec functions that aren't class methods. Layout: `buffer-codec.ts` (140 LoC) holds `encodeString(str, encoding)` and `decodeString(buf, encoding, start?, end?)` — the per-encoding String↔Uint8Array switch logic for utf8/ascii/latin1/base64/base64url/hex/utf16le — plus the small `checkOffset(offset, ext, length)` range-check helper. `buffer.ts` shrinks from 817 LoC to 701 LoC; its imports section is noticeably less cluttered (the `textEncoder` / `textDecoder` module-locals live in `buffer-codec.ts` now; the 2 remaining call sites in `Buffer.byteLength` use a one-shot `new TextEncoder()` — TextEncoder is cheap to construct, and `byteLength` is rarely on a hot path). Public surface preserved verbatim; no exports changed. 326 GJS buffer tests still green; tsc + build clean.

- ~~**`README.md` modernized — `npm`/`yarn` commands replaced with `gjsify <subcommand>`**~~✓ — Top-level user-facing README brought in line with v0.4.37's Node-free CLI. Quick Start now leads with the `curl … install.mjs | gjs -m -` Node-free bootstrap; the legacy `npm install -g @gjsify/cli` is demoted to a clearly-marked escape hatch. New-project flow is `gjsify create` + `gjsify install --immutable` + `gjsify build` + `gjsify run start` (was `npm create @gjsify/app` + `npm install` + `npm run build` + `npm start`). Prerequisites section drops `nodejs` from the Fedora/Ubuntu apt/dnf one-liners and labels Node.js itself as optional (only needed for the cross-validation `test:node` track or for managing `@gjsify/cli` via `npm install -g`). Development section: `yarn build` → `gjsify foreach -A -t build`, `yarn check` → `gjsify check`, `yarn test` → `gjsify foreach -A test`, `yarn test:integration*` → per-suite `gjsify workspace <name> test`, per-package `yarn test:{node,gjs}` → `gjsify test [--runtime gjs|node]` with a paragraph explaining the new `gjsify test` runner. Verified the cited commands by running each of `gjsify --help` / `install --help` / `dlx --help` / `run --help` / `foreach --help` / `workspace --help` / `check --help` / `test --help` / `build --help` / `create --help` against the committed bundle.

- ~~**`inline-static-reads` — refuse foreign-namespace `.join` evaluation + bail on `readdirSync({withFileTypes:true})`**~~✓ — Root-cause fix for a build-time inliner regression that crashed ts-for-gir's GJS bundle when running `doc`/`json` subcommands. `identifierName()` extracted the property name from any `MemberExpression` callee, so `dir.join('/')` (Array.prototype.join — typedoc's `discoverFiles` walks a string-array stack) was misread as `path.join('/')`. The static evaluator then resolved that to `'/'`, called `readdirSync('/', {withFileTypes:true})` at build time, and inlined the **build machine's root filesystem listing** (`['afs','bin','boot',...]`) as a string array. At runtime the consumer iterates that array and calls `child.isFile()`, which throws `i.isFile is not a function` because each element is a string, not a `Dirent`. Two layered fixes: (a) `identifierName()` now requires the MemberExpression object to be a known module namespace identifier (`path` or `fs`), so unrelated `.join()` / `.dirname()` / `.resolve()` etc. method calls bail out instead of resolving. (b) `readdirSync` inlining bails when the call passes `{withFileTypes:true}` — even with a correctly-evaluated path, we'd substitute `string[]` for the expected `Dirent[]`, breaking any consumer that uses `.isFile()`/`.isDirectory()`. Defence in depth against new static path-resolution heuristics we might add later. New `packages/infra/cli/src/inline-static-reads.spec.ts` locks the regression: 4 cases covering the typedoc shape, the `withFileTypes:true` bail-out, the legitimate `path.join`-resolved happy path that still inlines, and a generic `arr.join('/')` non-match. The bug only manifested in bundles whose source happened to use Array.prototype.join with a single string argument (typedoc 0.28's `discoverFiles`, possibly others) and whose static path arg coincidentally resolved to a real directory at build time — silent for everything else, which is how it stayed in master for the full @gjsify/cli 0.4.x train.

- ~~**`@gjsify/crypto` — split `src/cipher.ts` into per-concern modules (952 → 542 + 412)**~~✓ — Same per-concern split pattern. Base `cipher.ts` shrinks from 952 LoC to 542 LoC; the AES math and the algorithm/buffer/PKCS#7 helpers move out into two focused modules. Layout: `aes-primitives.ts` (315 LoC — `SBOX` / `INV_SBOX` / `RCON` constants, the `gmul` GF(2^8) helper, `keyExpansion()`, `aesEncryptBlock()` / `aesDecryptBlock()` block primitives, `incrementCounter()` / `gcmIncrementCounter()` CTR/GCM counter helpers, the GF(2^128) `gfMul` private helper, and the `ghash()` GHASH function per NIST SP 800-38D), `cipher-utils.ts` (97 LoC — `interface AlgorithmInfo`, `parseAlgorithm()`, `toBuffer()`, `encodeOutput()`, `incompleteUtf8Tail()`, `pkcs7Pad()` / `pkcs7Unpad()`). Base file retains `CipherBase`, `Cipher`, `Decipher` classes + the `createCipher` / `createCipheriv` / `createDecipher` / `createDecipheriv` / `getCiphers` factories — these three classes are tightly coupled (Decipher inherits CipherBase like Cipher does, and the GCM authenticated-encryption path threads through both), so they stay together. Public surface preserved verbatim; no module exports changed. 572 GJS crypto tests still green; tsc + build clean. Same approach as the `@gjsify/util` split (PR #268): the extracted modules are pure utility code with stable surface that's easier to spot-check and reuse (e.g. a future hand-rolled GCM authentication-tag verification helper for streaming uploads could import `ghash` directly without dragging in the whole Cipher class).

- ~~**`gjsify install --backend=native|npm` flag — closes the long-standing Phase-4 follow-up**~~✓ — Closes the "🔲 `gjsify install <pkg>` user-facing CLI" Open TODO under "Phase 4: gjsify install / dlx native backend". The TODO entry was stale: the native backend has been the default since the `install-backend.ts` flip much earlier, but the only way to opt back into `npm install` was the undocumented `GJSIFY_INSTALL_BACKEND=npm` env var. New `--backend native|npm` flag on `gjsify install` exposes that toggle as a first-class CLI argument; precedence is explicit `--backend` > env var > `native` default. The env var stays honoured so existing scripts (CI, dotfiles) keep working unchanged. File header + `--help` text updated to document escape-hatch use cases: Yarn PnP repos (the native backend rejects with a clear error), npm lifecycle scripts (deferred behind a separate `--allow-scripts` follow-up that's still open), and npm's per-parent `overrides` shape that the native resolver doesn't yet flatten. No new dependency — the flag just routes the existing `projectInstallViaNpm` / `projectInstallNative` codepaths.

- ~~**`@gjsify/webgl` — extract `WebGL2RenderingContext` object-lifecycle methods (1439 → 1236 + 285)**~~✓ — First slice of the long-running `webgl2-rendering-context.ts` split. Same `install*Methods(proto)` pattern as the parent `webgl-context-base.ts` split, applied to the WebGL2 subclass. Extracts the five object-lifecycle method groups that share a near-identical shape (`createX` / `deleteX` / `isX` / `bindX` plus type-specific param setters/getters): VAO (`createVertexArray` / `deleteVertexArray` / `isVertexArray` / `bindVertexArray`), Query (`createQuery` / `deleteQuery` / `isQuery` / `beginQuery` / `endQuery` / `getQuery` / `getQueryParameter`), Sampler (`createSampler` / `deleteSampler` / `isSampler` / `bindSampler` / `samplerParameteri` / `samplerParameterf` / `getSamplerParameter`), Sync (`fenceSync` / `isSync` / `deleteSync` / `clientWaitSync` / `waitSync` / `getSyncParameter`), Transform Feedback (`createTransformFeedback` / `deleteTransformFeedback` / `isTransformFeedback` / `bindTransformFeedback` / `beginTransformFeedback` / `endTransformFeedback` / `pauseTransformFeedback` / `resumeTransformFeedback` / `transformFeedbackVaryings` / `getTransformFeedbackVarying`). 32 methods totalling 203 LoC moved out of the base file. New module `packages/framework/webgl/src/ts/webgl2-context/object-lifecycle.ts` (285 LoC including the `interface ObjectLifecycleMethods` declaration-merged into `WebGL2RenderingContext` via `declare module`, the methods object, and the `installObjectLifecycleMethods(proto)` function). Base file `webgl2-rendering-context.ts` 1439 → 1236 LoC, calls `installObjectLifecycleMethods(WebGL2RenderingContext.prototype)` after class declaration plus a deliberate side-effect `import './webgl2-context/object-lifecycle.js'` retained in the emitted `.d.ts` so downstream consumers see the augmentations. Same `.d.ts` preservation technique as the canvas2d-core split (PR #262). Unused `WebGLActiveInfo` import dropped from the base. 860 GJS webgl conformance tests still green. Larger remaining method groups (texImage3D family ~190 LoC, uniform family ~110 LoC, clearBuffer family ~110 LoC, instanced drawing + framebuffer-ops ~150 LoC) are left in the base; future PRs can extract them with the same pattern if motivated.

- ~~**`@gjsify/fs` — extract `file-handle.ts` open-flag helpers (855 → 797 + 89), add `FileHandle.pull` / `FileHandle.writer` stubs**~~✓ — Targeted helper extraction rather than a full class split (the `FileHandle` class itself is one cohesive implementation of `IFileHandle` with every method tied to the per-instance `GLib.IOChannel`, so an `install*Methods` split would just shuffle lines without exposing meaningful module boundaries). The file-level helpers `resolveIOMode`, `openIOChannel`, `mapOpenError`, the POSIX `O_*` constants, and the `IOMode` type — all of them concerning the conversion between Node's open-flag forms (numeric POSIX vs string fopen-like) and GLib.IOChannel's `'r'`/`'r+'`/`'w'`/`'w+'`/`'a'`/`'a+'` modes — move to a sibling module `packages/node/fs/src/file-handle-open.ts` (89 LoC). Added one new export `shouldCreate(flags)` that consolidates the "should we create the file if missing?" check from the constructor's `(options.flags & O_CREAT) !== 0` numeric-only check into a routine that also handles string aliases (`'w'`, `'w+'`, `'wx'`, `'wx+'`, `'a'`, `'a+'`, `'ax'`, `'ax+'`, `'as'`, `'as+'`) — the previous implementation would silently NOT auto-create for string flags, surfacing as ENOENT for `await fsPromises.open(path, 'w')` on a non-existent path when the underlying IOChannel mode is `'r+'`. Caught while writing the helper. Additionally added experimental `pull(...)` / `writer(...)` stub methods to satisfy the `FileHandle` interface contract on `@types/node` 25.8.0+ — these are the new `node:stream/iter` API entry points; both throw `ERR_METHOD_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` until the iter implementation lands. `file-handle.ts` is now 797 LoC (down from 855) and its imports section is noticeably less cluttered. 640 GJS fs tests still green; tsc + build clean.

- ~~**`as any` cleanup batch — impl-code edition (48 → 0 real casts across 7 files)**~~✓ — Fourth batch in the `as any` reduction sweep, this time targeting actual implementation code (not register modules). Each fix surfaces real type-safety where the previous `as any` was eating an assignability check. Files touched (before → after, all 0 real casts remaining, residual matches are doc comments): `packages/web/webrtc/src/get-user-media.ts` 9 → 0 (new `interface _GstElementProps extends Gst.Element` capturing the dynamic GObject properties `caps` / `is_live` / `wave` / `pattern` that don't appear on the GIR-typed `Gst.Element`), `packages/web/xmlhttprequest/src/index.ts` 8 → 0 (new `interface _URLObjectURLPatch` for the `URL.createObjectURL` / `URL.revokeObjectURL` / `__gjsify_objecturl` properties this module patches onto the URL constructor; `(this as any)['on' + type]` for XHR event handlers becomes a typed `Record<\`on${XHREventType}\`, …>` lookup), `packages/framework/video/src/pipeline-builder.ts` 7 → 0 (local `interface _PaintableSink extends Gst.Element` / `interface _GlSinkBin extends Gst.Element` / `interface _Playbin extends Gst.Element` for `gtk4paintablesink`/`glsinkbin`/`playbin` GObject properties; `request_pad_simple` vs legacy `get_request_pad` probed via a typed-optional structural fallback), `packages/web/webrtc/src/register/peer-connection.ts` (handled in earlier register PR — not this one), `packages/node/sqlite/src/param-binding.ts` 6 → 0 (new `setHolderPrim(holder, value)` helper consolidating the `holder.set_value(prim as unknown as GObject.Value)` cast so each call site stays free of `any`; this hides GJS' auto-conversion from JS primitives to `GObject.Value` behind one place), `packages/node/perf_hooks/src/index.ts` 6 → 0 (new `interface _PerfHooksGlobals` for the optional native `PerformanceObserver` / `PerformanceEntry` / `PerformanceObserverEntryList` / `PerformanceMark` / `PerformanceMeasure` constructors; the GLib bootstrap path also gains a structural `(globalThis as {imports?:{gi?:{GLib?:{get_monotonic_time():number}}}})` view), `packages/node/fs/src/fd-ops.ts` 6 → 0 (`fstatAsync` now picks the right `fstatSync` overload at runtime based on `options?.bigint`; `readSync`/`writeSync` use the inferred narrow types instead of casting each parameter to `any`), `packages/framework/webgl/src/ts/webgl-bridge.ts` 6 → 0 (local `interface _RafGlobals` for `requestAnimationFrame`/`cancelAnimationFrame`/`performance` and a typed `{document?:{body?:{appendChild(el:unknown):void}}}` view for the GTK→DOM document attach). tsc clean across all 7 touched packages. Net change: 48 real `as any` casts removed; each replacement is more verbose by 5-10 LoC (the typed-view declaration), so total LoC grew by ~80 — but every call site now type-checks the property access at compile time. The `(this as any)['on' + type]` change in xmlhttprequest in particular surfaces a real protection: if a future PR adds a new `XHREventType` member it must also add the matching `on<X>` property to the class signature, otherwise the typed lookup will fail.

- ~~**`as any` cleanup batch — register modules (53 → 5 real casts)**~~✓ — Third batch in the long-running `as any` reduction sweep, this time targeting the densest hotspots in `/register` side-effect modules across the web + DOM pillars. Same approach as PR #263: each module now has a single module-local `interface _<Name>Globals` typed view of the `globalThis` slots it writes (and reads for the existence-guard), declared once at the top and used through the install branch. Files touched (before → after): `packages/web/streams/src/register/readable.ts` 12 → 1 (the kept cast is the typed-view itself), `packages/web/webrtc/src/register/peer-connection.ts` 8 → 0, `packages/web/webaudio/src/register.ts` 8 → 0, `packages/web/web-globals/src/register.ts` 6 → 1, `packages/web/webrtc/src/register/media.ts` 6 → 0, `packages/web/websocket/src/register.ts` 6 → 0, `packages/dom/dom-elements/src/register/document.ts` 7 → 1. The dom-elements register file additionally needed `Parameters<EventTarget['addEventListener']>[…]` indirection to keep the global-side listener / Event types assignable to `@gjsify/dom-events`'s structurally-narrower ones — a real type-safety net that the previous `as any` cast was eating silently. tsc clean across all 6 packages. Total real cast reduction: 53 → 5 (the 5 remaining are the typed-view `as unknown as` lines themselves, no `any` involved).

- ~~**`@gjsify/util` — split `src/index.ts` into per-concern modules (787 → 121 + 4 modules)**~~✓ — Same per-concern split pattern (each module owns a clear slice of the Node `util` surface). Base `index.ts` shrinks from 787 LoC to 121 LoC — just imports, named re-exports, default export, and the `globalThis.{TextDecoder,TextEncoder}` re-export. Layout: `inspect.ts` (270 LoC — `kCustomInspect` + `inspect()` + its `.custom` / `.defaultOptions` / `.colors` / `.styles` properties + module-local `inspectValue` / `inspectArray` / `inspectObject` helpers), `format.ts` (220 LoC — `format()` + `formatWithOptions()` + `stripVTControlCharacters()` + `styleText()` + the local `ANSI_REGEX`, imports `inspect`), `compat.ts` (240 LoC — `promisify()` + `callbackify()` + `kCustomPromisify` + `deprecate()` + `debuglog()` + `inherits()` + `isDeepStrictEqual()` + `toUSVString()` + `aborted()`), `legacy-predicates.ts` (70 LoC — the `util.isXxx` family). Pre-existing `types.ts` (`util.types` namespace) and `errors.ts` (`getSystemErrorName` / `getSystemErrorMap`) untouched. Public surface preserved verbatim; default export unchanged. Caveat caught during testing: literal ESC bytes (`\x1b`) in `stripVTControlCharacters`'s short-circuit check and `styleText`'s open/close codes don't survive a file copy through tooling — replaced with `\x1b` escape sequences as a permanent fix (4 ESC + 2 CSI control chars in the original were all of this shape). 270/270 Node + 270/270 GJS util tests still green; tsc + build clean.

- ~~**`@gjsify/canvas2d-core` — split `canvas-rendering-context-2d.ts` into per-concern modules (1208 → 417 + 6 modules)**~~✓ — Applies the same `install*Methods(proto)` pattern proven by `@gjsify/webgl`'s `webgl-context-base.ts` split. Base file `canvas-rendering-context-2d.ts` shrinks from 1208 LoC to 417 LoC — retains class shell + constructor + fields (`_surface`, `_ctx`, `_state`, `_stateStack`, `_surfaceWidth`, `_surfaceHeight`) + ALL internal helpers (`_ensureSurface`, `_resetState`, `_applyFillStyle`, `_applyStrokeStyle`, `_applyPatternFilter`, `_applyLineStyle`, `_applyCompositing`, `_getSurface`, `_hasShadow`, `_deviceToUserDistance`, `_renderShadow`) + ALL state accessors (fillStyle, strokeStyle, lineWidth, lineCap, lineJoin, miterLimit, globalAlpha, globalCompositeOperation, imageSmoothingEnabled/Quality, line-dash, shadow*, font, textAlign/textBaseline/direction) + save/restore (keeping the `_stateStack` interaction local) + `_toDataURL` + `_dispose`. Methods grouped under `src/context/`: `transforms.ts` (144 LoC — translate/rotate/scale/transform/setTransform/getTransform/resetTransform), `path-ops.ts` (151 LoC — beginPath/moveTo/lineTo/closePath/bezier+quadraticCurveTo/arc/arcTo/ellipse/rect/roundRect), `drawing.ts` (319 LoC — fill/stroke/fillRect/strokeRect/clearRect/clip/isPointInPath/isPointInStroke/drawImage + private `getDrawImageSource` helper), `pixels.ts` (127 LoC — createImageData/getImageData/putImageData), `text-rendering.ts` (246 LoC — fillText/strokeText/measureText with `parseFontToDescription`/`createTextLayout`/`getTextAlignOffset`/`getTextBaselineOffset` as module-local helpers), `factories.ts` (49 LoC — createLinearGradient/createRadialGradient/createPattern), and an `index.ts` (34 LoC) barrel exposing `installAllContextMethods(proto)`. Each module declares an `interface *Methods` augmenting `CanvasRenderingContext2D` via `declare module '../canvas-rendering-context-2d.js'` so type-merging at consumer sites is automatic — but only when the augmenting modules are loaded. tsc strips value-only named imports from emitted `.d.ts`, so the base file adds a deliberate side-effect import `import './context/index.js';` at the top: tsc preserves bare side-effect imports in the emitted declarations, ensuring downstream packages (`@gjsify/canvas2d`, `@gjsify/dom-elements`) pick up the method signatures from the published types (regression caught in `framework/canvas2d/src/index.spec.ts` calling `.beginPath()` / `.fillRect()` / `.fillText()` / etc., resolved by adding the side-effect import). Public surface preserved verbatim; 578 GJS tests still green; tsc clean across `@gjsify/canvas2d-core` + `@gjsify/dom-elements`. Only one pre-existing unrelated `notifyElementResize` error remains in `framework/canvas2d/src/canvas2d-bridge.ts`, untouched by this PR.

- ~~**`@gjsify/utils` audit Phase 1 — extract `base64` + `encoding` to `@gjsify/buffer`**~~✓ — First slice of the long-promised `@gjsify/utils` kitchen-sink audit. The two extracted modules describe the Buffer-encoding contract (`normalizeEncoding`, `checkEncoding`, `Encoding`, `base64Encode`, `base64Decode`, `atobPolyfill`, `btoaPolyfill`), not generic GJS utilities — every consumer is either `@gjsify/buffer` itself, `@gjsify/string_decoder`, or `@gjsify/crypto`. Moving them places them next to their natural callers and frees `@gjsify/utils` to be a slimmer GJS-only utility pillar. Layout change: `packages/gjs/utils/src/{base64,encoding}.ts` deleted; `packages/node/buffer/src/{base64,encoding}.ts` created with the same exports; `packages/node/buffer/src/index.ts` re-exports them for downstream consumers. Consumer updates: `@gjsify/buffer/src/buffer.ts` switches from `import { … } from '@gjsify/utils'` to two local `from './encoding.js'` / `from './base64.js'` lines; `@gjsify/crypto/src/{hash,hmac}.ts` switch from `@gjsify/utils` to `@gjsify/buffer`; `@gjsify/string_decoder/src/index.ts` switches to `@gjsify/buffer` and drops its `@gjsify/utils` dependency entirely (no other utils symbol used there). `@gjsify/utils/src/index.ts` updated to remove the two `export *` lines plus a comment block pointing future readers to `@gjsify/buffer`. `gbytesToUint8Array` (the `byte-array.ts` helper) STAYS in `@gjsify/utils` — it's a GJS-runtime shape wrapper (`imports.byteArray.fromGBytes`) used by `@gjsify/net` + `@gjsify/child_process`, not Buffer-encoding domain. No circular-dep risk: `@gjsify/utils` does not depend on `@gjsify/buffer`, `@gjsify/buffer` keeps its existing `@gjsify/utils` dependency. tsc + build clean across all 4 packages; tests still green: buffer 326, string_decoder 103, crypto 572, utils 138.

- ~~**`scripts/check-test-coverage.mjs` — structural per-package spec-coverage audit + `node-globals/register` Step 4 closed**~~✓ — New top-level dev tool that reports per-package source-file ↔ spec-file gaps: for each `src/<name>.ts` impl file, checks whether a sibling `<name>.spec.ts` (or `.gjs.spec.ts` / `.browser.spec.ts`) exists OR whether some sibling `*.spec.ts` already imports `'./<name>'` / `'./<name>.js'`. Files matching well-known patterns are skipped (`index.ts`, `test.mts`, `*-types.ts`, `types.ts`, `constants.ts`, `cjs-compat.cjs`, `*.d.ts`). 92 packages / 495 impl files / 177 spec files surveyed in the first pass; the script is intentionally structural — it does NOT measure assertion or branch coverage. Flags `--verbose` (list each uncovered file) and `--strict` (exit 1 if any uncovered) make it embeddable into CI later if the team decides to enforce a baseline; today it ships as informational. Aim is to make it harder for new impl files to land without any spec at all — the audit run is `node scripts/check-test-coverage.mjs`. Same PR closes the long-pending Step 4 line under "Split `@gjsify/node-globals/register` into topic-specific packages": catch-all stays indefinitely as the explicit "full Node runtime surface" entry point (no deprecation), since Steps 1–3 already made it genuinely opt-in (examples / showcases / integration tests rely on `--globals auto` post-tree-shake detection instead).

- ~~**`as any` cleanup batch — `@gjsify/dom-events` + `@gjsify/streams` + `@gjsify/unit` (38 → 0 real casts)**~~✓ — Continuation of the long-running `as any` reduction (same approach as `tls/src/index.spec.ts` 65 → 0 and `@gjsify/webgl` 74 → 44 from prior PRs). All three modules now express their internal pierced-private / erased-host-shape accesses as typed views rather than `any` casts. (a) **`@gjsify/dom-events/src/index.ts`** 12 → 0. The 12 casts were `(event as any)[kDispatching]`/`[kTarget]`/`[kCurrentTarget]`/`[kEventPhase]`/`[kStop]`/`[kImmediateStop]`/`[kInPassiveListener]` inside `EventTarget.dispatchEvent` — accessing symbol-keyed `private` slots declared on `Event` from outside the class. Replaced by a single module-local `interface _EventInternals` listing those symbol-keyed slots; `dispatchEvent` casts the incoming `Event` once at the top (`const ev = event as unknown as _EventInternals`) and uses normal property access through the rest of the loop. (b) **`@gjsify/streams/src/util.ts`** 11 → 0. The 11 were `return Int8Array as unknown as any` etc. in `typedArrayConstructorByTag`'s switch; the lib.es5 typed-array constructors disagree on buffer-kind parameters so the return-type couldn't be narrowed naturally. Replaced by a local `type ArrayBufferViewCtor = new (buffer: ArrayBufferLike, byteOffset?: number, length?: number) => ArrayBufferView` and `as unknown as ArrayBufferViewCtor` on each switch arm — call sites now see a real constructor type instead of `any`. (c) **`@gjsify/unit/src/index.ts`** 15 → 0. Mix of `(globalThis as any).process?.env` / `.performance?.now?.()` / `.imports?.gi?.GLib` / `.imports.system.exit(…)` / `.document` plus four `(error as any).__testFailureCounted = true` brand-writes. Replaced by a module-local `interface _RuntimeGlobals` capturing every GJS/Node/Browser surface the test runner reads (`imports.{mainloop,gi,system}`, `process.{env,versions,exit}`, `performance.now`, `document`, `__gjsify_test_results`) plus a `const runtimeGlobals = (): _RuntimeGlobals => globalThis as unknown as _RuntimeGlobals` accessor, and a brand interface `_CountedError { __testFailureCounted?: boolean }` for the counted-error pattern (`error as Error & _CountedError`). Both replacements are 1 cast in total per call site instead of 1 per access. tsc clean across all 3 packages; 274 dom-events + 345 streams + 169 unit tests still green. The 3 remaining `as any` matches in those files are all inside doc-comments (explanatory references to the old pattern), no real code.

- ~~**`@gjsify/http2` — split `src/server.ts` into per-concern modules (1453 → 49 + 4 modules)**~~✓ — Applies the proven webgl/process/tls per-concern split pattern to the http2 server-side surface. `src/server.ts` shrinks from 1453 LoC to a 49-LoC thin composer that just re-exports from `src/server/`; the implementation is redistributed across four files: `server/request.ts` (76 LoC — `Http2ServerRequest` Readable side), `server/session.ts` (140 LoC — `ServerHttp2Session` with `_allocatePushId` + `_buildPushPromise` via the @gjsify/http2-native bridge), `server/response.ts` (811 LoC — `Http2NativeBackend` interface + `Http2ServerResponse` + `ServerHttp2Stream` + the `_respondFromFD`/`_makeDetachedSoupMessage` helpers — kept together because Response and Stream are tightly coupled), `server/http2-server.ts` (452 LoC — `Http2Server` + `Http2SecureServer` classes + `_toPemString`/`_createTlsCertificate` TLS helpers + the `_activeServers` GC-guard Set). All public surface preserved: same exports, same imports work unchanged from `@gjsify/http2`. 102 Node + 231 GJS http2 tests still green; type-check + library build clean. No new behaviour, no new tests — straight refactor.
- ~~**`@gjsify/dom-elements` `ResizeObserver` polyfill: real bridge wiring (replaces stub)**~~✓ — `ResizeObserver` was a no-op stub: every method returned undefined, `observe()` never fired its callback. Excalibur.js 0.32's `Screen._setResolutionAndViewportByDisplayMode()` installs `new ResizeObserver(cb).observe(canvas.parentElement || document.body)` under `DisplayMode.FillContainer`; the stub broke that path, leaving rendered scenes stuck at their initial GTK allocation. **Fix:** real implementation in `packages/dom/dom-elements/src/resize-observer.ts`. `Element` gains `_onResize(cb): () => void` (subscribe + unsubscribe) and `_fireResizeSubscribers(width, height)` (internal dispatch); `notifyElementResize(target, width, height)` (new `notify-resize.ts`, also exported from the public surface) fires the target's own subscribers + walks `parentNode` chain firing ancestor subscribers — the latter is what makes Excalibur's `observe(canvas.parentElement)` pattern work, since the bridge resize signal hits the canvas, not its parent. Resize storms collapse into a single microtask-batched callback per observer (matches the spec's "deliver resize loop notifications" cycle). Each bridge that owns a polyfill DOM element now forwards its GTK `resize` signal: `WebGLBridge` (`Gtk.GLArea` → `_canvas`), `Canvas2DBridge` (`Gtk.DrawingArea` → `_canvas`), `VideoBridge` (`Gtk.Picture` → `_video`), `IFrameBridge` (`WebKit.WebView` → `_iframe`, **new resize listener**). 8 new specs in `packages/dom/dom-elements/src/resize-observer.spec.ts` (direct fire, ancestor walk, unobserve / disconnect, idempotent observe, multi-observer isolation, entry shape, microtask batching) — all 442 dom-elements tests pass on Node + GJS. Closes the PixelRPG map-editor `FillContainer` parity gap documented in the original report.

- ~~**`css-as-string` plugin: restore npm-package `@import` resolution (esbuild → rolldown regression)**~~✓ — `@gjsify/rolldown-plugin-gjsify`'s `cssAsStringPlugin` calls lightningcss `bundleAsync` to resolve `@import` chains during `.css` → JS-string conversion. The old esbuild-based predecessor (`@gjsify/esbuild-plugin-css`, retired in the rolldown migration) walked `node_modules` for bare specifiers + honoured `package.json#exports`, so consumers could write `@import "@scoped/pkg/styles.css"` from any workspace CSS file. The npm-track of the new plugin invoked `bundleAsync` with no `resolver`, leaving lightningcss's default file-only walker — which only handles relative + absolute paths and otherwise hits ENOENT. Surfaced by the `pixel-rpg/map-editor` migration: `apps/maker-gjs/src/application.css` couldn't import `@pixelrpg/gjs/index.css` and required a 3-segment relative-path workaround. Fix: add a `cssBundleResolver` (~20 LoC) that distinguishes the three path shapes — absolute pass-through, relative resolved against the importer's dirname, bare specifier resolved via `createRequire(pathToFileURL(from).href).resolve(specifier)` so `node_modules` lookups + `exports` maps both work. The same resolver is now wired into `loadNpmBundler()`'s `bundleAsync` call. Native track (`@gjsify/lightningcss-native`) is unchanged — its Vala bridge signature takes a single `filename` + browserslist string and has no resolver hook; documented limitation, only relevant under GJS-runs-build (Phase D-3) which downstream monorepos don't hit yet. `tests/e2e/css-bundling/run.mjs` extended with a second test case that plants a fake `@css-fixture/shared` package under `node_modules` (with `package.json#exports` exposing `./styles.css`) and asserts (a) the bare-specifier @import resolves end-to-end, (b) the resolved CSS content is inlined into the bundled JS string, (c) relative + bare @imports cohabit in the same file. Unblocks the map-editor CSS workaround which can now be reverted in its next gjsify-cli bump.

- ~~**`@gjsify/child_process` async `spawn()` stdin-piping + `@gjsify/stream` `_destroy` subclass-override fix (#218 follow-up)**~~✓ — Closes the Medium-priority Open TODO surfaced by `tests/integration/execa/`'s ignored `passes stdin via the input option (string)` case. **Two coupled fixes:** (1) `packages/node/child_process/src/index.ts` gains `GioOutputStreamWritable` (Node `Writable` wrapping `Gio.OutputStream`) — `_write` routes chunks through `write_bytes_async`, `_final` closes the pipe sync (avoids a hang on `close_async` against a kernel pipe whose read-end was held by an already-exited subprocess), `_destroy` cancels in-flight writes + sync-closes. Wired into async `spawn()` when `stdio[0] === 'pipe'`: `child.stdin = new GioOutputStreamWritable(proc.get_stdin_pipe())`. The `proc.wait_async` exit handler also calls `child.stdin.destroy()` to match Node's documented behaviour of auto-destroying stdin on subprocess exit — without this, execa's `await finished(child.stdin)` (from `wait-stream.js`) hangs forever waiting for a `'finish'` event that no one would emit. (2) Found and fixed a base-class bug in `packages/node/stream/src/writable.ts`: `Writable.destroy()` called the private `_destroyImpl` field (only set via constructor opts) directly instead of dispatching to `this._destroy(...)`. That meant Node-convention subclass `_destroy()` overrides + execa's `spyOnStdinDestroy` (which monkey-patches `child.stdin._destroy` to record exitCode-at-cleanup-time) were both dead code. Added a default `_destroy(error, cb)` method that delegates to `_destroyImpl` (backwards-compat for the constructor-opt API) AND changed `destroy()` to call `this._destroy(...)` so subclass overrides take precedence via normal JS prototype lookup. The execa Open TODO was un-ignored in the same commit; **44/44 execa tests now pass on GJS** (was 43/43 + 1 ignored). All 515 Node + 517 GJS `@gjsify/stream` unit tests stay green. All 120 Node + 124 GJS `@gjsify/child_process` unit tests stay green. All 275 GJS + 270 Node `@gjsify/worker_threads` unit tests stay green (worker_threads also depends on stream).

- ~~**`gjsify barrels` — barrelsby replacement**~~✓ — New top-level CLI command that regenerates `index.ts` re-export files for a list of directories. Drop-in replacement for the unmaintained `barrelsby` (last release 2022) at consumer sites (pixel-rpg/map-editor, future easy6502 / ts-for-gir cleanup). Inspired by `beabee-communityrm/monorepo apps/dev-cli`'s `generate-index` action — same separation between pure async generator (`actions/barrels-generate.ts`, ~95 LoC, no external deps, uses `node:fs/promises`) and yargs CommandModule (`commands/barrels.ts`). Behavior: sorted output (locale-compare for deterministic diffs), `export type *` auto-emitted when the directory is literally named `types/` (avoids dragging value imports through type-only barrels), `export {};` fallback for empty directories (TypeScript still parses as a module), configurable import extension (`js` / `ts` / `none`; default `none` for bundler-mode resolution). Flags map mechanically to barrelsby's: `--exclude REGEX` (repeatable, defaults to `\.test\.`, `\.spec\.`, `\.test-data\.`), `--single-quotes` (default true), `--no-semicolon` (default — matches biome.json defaults), `--header <text>`, `--check` (CI guard: exit 1 + drift report without writing). Directories accepted as positional or `--paths`/`-p` (yargs merges both forms). 11 new unit tests under `src/barrels-generate.spec.ts` cover basic output, `types/` heuristic, empty-dir fallback, exclude regex, extension setting, quote/semicolon style, `--check` drift detection, missing-dir robustness, `index.ts` self-exclusion, and `.mts`/`.tsx` source handling. Smoke-tested against `pixel-rpg/map-editor/packages/engine/src/systems/`: byte-identical output to barrelsby modulo the header comment line. Replaces a 2.6 MB `barrelsby` devDep + transitive `lodash`/`yargs-parser`/`commander` chain with ~120 LoC of native fs-promises.

- ~~**Minimalist Browser Showcase + `IFrameBridge` navigation methods**~~✓ — New `showcases/dom/minimalist-browser/` (URL bar + Back / Forward / Reload + iframe content area + built-in srcdoc page library + postMessage round-trip status line). Cross-variant by construction: the shared `src/browser-demo.ts` (`BrowserCore` class with application-side history stack) operates on an `IFrameHandle` duck-type satisfied by both a real `<iframe>` element (browser variant) AND an `IFrameBridge.iframeElement` (GJS variant — `IFrameBridge.iframeElement` IS an `HTMLIFrameElement`). Validates `@gjsify/iframe` is feature-complete against a non-trivial cross-platform application. Built-in page library (`page:welcome`, `page:about`, `page:postmessage`, `page:adwaita`) — each carries an inline `<script>` that calls `window.parent.postMessage({type:'page-loaded', title, url}, '*')` on `DOMContentLoaded`; the parent listens via `iframe.contentWindow.addEventListener('message', …)` and surfaces the announcement in a status line. **`IFrameBridge` enhancement (same PR)**: added `goBack()` / `goForward()` / `canGoBack` / `canGoForward` thin wrappers around WebKit.WebView's internal back/forward navigation list. `reload()` is inherited verbatim from `WebKit.WebView` (camelCase already, no rewrap needed). The wrappers exist for apps that want to expose the WebKit-internal history list directly; both variants of this showcase use an application-side history stack instead, for parity with the browser variant where cross-origin iframes cannot be navigated via `contentWindow.history`. Showcase count: 7 → 8 (after rebase against #216 + #214 merge). `packages/infra/cli/showcases.json` gained the new entry; CLI rebuild lists it under DOM. Builds clean on both targets (browser bundle: 9 KiB; GJS bundle: 217 KiB).

- ~~**WebRTC Loopback Showcase promoted from `examples/dom/` to `showcases/dom/`**~~✓ — `examples/dom/webrtc-loopback/` was a private dev/test example since the early WebRTC sprint. With Phases 2-4 of `@gjsify/webrtc` complete (Media, DataChannel, Stats), the demo is now polish-ready. `git mv examples/dom/webrtc-loopback → showcases/dom/webrtc-loopback`; package gained a `files: ["dist"]` array, dropped `private: true` (now publishable), gained an `exports` map (`./browser`, `./loopback-demo`, `./package.json`) for website-embedding, moved `@gjsify/webrtc` from `devDependencies` to `dependencies` (runtime prebuild dep per AGENTS.md). Browser entry refactored from inline-DOM-and-go to `mount(container: HTMLElement, options?): ShowcaseHandle` returning `{pause, resume, isPaused, restart}` — same pattern as `three-postprocessing-pixel`. New `src/browser/browser-main.ts` is the standalone entry (`mount(document.body)`); the website slideshow can call `mount()` directly without a standalone page. `packages/infra/cli/showcases.json` gained the new entry (`category: "dom"`, `needsWebgl: false`). Showcase count: 7 → 8. Out of scope: `webrtc-{video,dtmf,states,trickle-ice}` remain private examples for now — `webrtc-video` is the natural second-showcase candidate (getUserMedia + media pipeline) but a separate workstream.

- ~~**Cross-process MessagePort transfer via Worker subprocess IPC (#204 follow-up)**~~✓ — `Worker.postMessage(value, [port])` now actually moves a `MessagePort` across the parent ↔ child boundary instead of silently dropping it. New `packages/node/worker_threads/src/subprocess-port-transport.ts` (~80 LoC) defines the `SubprocessPortTransport` adapter — same pattern as `@gjsify/iframe`'s `BridgePortTransport` (PR #198), both implementing the `MessagePortTransport` interface that `@gjsify/message-channel`'s shared MessagePort honours via its `_inner._transport` hook (PR #204). Wire format: `{ __msgport: <portId>, op: 'send' | 'close', data: ... }` JSON lines on the existing worker IPC pipe (parent's `child.stdin.write` / child bootstrap's `process.stdout.write`); orthogonal to the existing `{ type: 'message' | 'terminate' | 'error' }` lines so dispatch is unambiguous. **Parent side**: `Worker.postMessage` walks transferList for MessagePort entries, severs each one's in-process partnership, allocates a fresh portId via monotonic counter (odd ids for parent, even for child — disjoint namespaces avoid coordination), attaches a `SubprocessPortTransport` to the kept end's `_inner._transport`, and emits a `{ __gjsifyTransferredPort: true, portId }` placeholder. The parent's stdout-reader recognises `__msgport`-key lines and routes them via the per-Worker `_portRegistry: Map<portId, MessagePort>`. **Child side**: the bootstrap (`BOOTSTRAP_CODE` in `worker.ts`) materialises port placeholders into an inline EventEmitter-shaped port object (analogous to `makeSharedBuffer`) backed by a child-side `childPortRegistry`; outbound traffic writes JSON lines to stdout. Both sides clean up on `close()` and on Worker exit (no fd or port-registry leak). 4 new integration tests in `tests/integration/worker-stress/src/cross-process-port-transfer.gjs.spec.ts` (round-trip, lifecycle close-propagation, multi-message ordering, 20 spawn/transfer/terminate cycles) — all green; GJS integration count 1035 → 1057. Worker_threads unit tests (275 GJS / 270 Node) and the worker-stress Node integration suite (1169) unchanged. transferList-within-transferList chains (port-in-port) and ArrayBuffer-over-wire stay out of scope (separate Open TODOs).

- ~~**Retire the misclassified "Vala/C-shim zero-copy" Open TODO (#209 follow-up correction)**~~✓ — The "Medium priority — True zero-copy `SharedBuffer.viewBytes()` via Vala/C shim" Open TODO added by PR #212 promised an internal alternative to the upstream GJS gap, but the strategy is technically not implementable: `JS::NewExternalArrayBuffer` requires a `JSContext*` that GJS does not expose to GObject-introspected `.so` plugins.
